# moirai — Architecture research: the design of record

*Final research deliverable for moirai. Date: 2026-09-26. Status: research/design only; nothing is implemented and this file is the only artifact written. It is the synthesis [30] revised by the final editorial pass: every blocker and major finding of the completeness review is resolved here, and the Review log at the end lists each finding with its disposition and the section that carries the fix. It supersedes the synthesis and proposals A–D as the design of record; where it changes them, it says so. Amended on 2026-09-26 by the owner's decisions to use no third-party database at any stage and to build every component once, to its final specification, in dependency order: §2.13 records the decisions, §9 summarises the roadmap of record [60], and the Review log lists what changed. Amended again on 2026-09-26 by the integration of R4 (move-proof file links, §5e) and R5 (the query language Lachesis, §7.7) from their revised designs [40] and [50], which stay normative for detail; the Review log's integration entry records the contradictions between [40], [50] and [60] it resolved. Amended a third time on 2026-09-26 by the final editorial pass over five audits on the owner's priorities — speed, minimal RAM, correctness, minimal agent tokens — and on feasibility and the configuration boundary ([70]–[74]): every blocker and major finding is fixed in the section it concerns, §8.3 consolidates the budgets and gates per priority, §11 now lists only real owner decisions, the new §13 is the configuration reference, and the Review log's audit entry maps every audit finding to its resolution. Amended a fourth time on 2026-09-26 by a verification pass (the third-party-database rule made absolute, the M6/M9/M10 exits and the R2 gate aligned with the gate catalogue, owner decision #41 on scope exclusions, per-hook enable keys, and numeric and citation drift; Review log, verification-pass entry). Amended a fifth time on 2026-09-26 by owner decision #32 — Linux and macOS designed now, Windows implemented and gated in M0–M11, the Linux and macOS implementations in a later, unscheduled port phase: the cross-platform design [80] (revision 2, which answers its review [81]) is integrated into every section it changes and summarised in the new §14 (Review log, last entry). Amended a sixth time on 2026-09-26 by owner decisions #43 (the agent interface must work in Codex and other harnesses, not only in Claude Code) and #44 (a cross-target `cargo check` for Linux and macOS is a gate from M0, so every dependency is pure Rust): the harness-agnostic design [90] (revision 2, after its review [91]) is integrated into §0–§2, §4, §5a, §6–§14 and the Review log, and §9's calendar is re-issued with every delta since the pre-audit baseline. Amended a seventh time on 2026-09-26 by the owner's answers to §11 (binding inputs): no additional machine — the laptop-only infrastructure profile L (#34); two lanes in parallel, both building on the laptop (#2); LQ-Bench on Opus 5.5 only for now (#38 (a)); a public GitHub repository (#36) whose commits carry no Claude co-author; every other open decision as recommended. §0, §1, §2.3, §2.13, §5e.9, §7.7.5, §7.8, §8.2, §8.3, §9–§11, §13 and §14 are updated, §9's calendar is re-issued for two lanes in profile L, and the Review log's last entry lists the changes. Amended an eighth time on 2026-09-27 by the owner review of the approval checklist (binding inputs): this document, with the normative [40], [50], [60], [80] and [90], is approved as the M0 specification; LQ-Bench runs through the owner's Claude Code subscription in headless mode, with no API billing and no API key (§7.7.5, §11 #38); the Windows OS-crash rig (GT15 and its calibration) is deferred to after the release together with the port phase, real power loss becomes an explicit residual risk (§10 risk 17) and §9's calendar is re-issued without the rig's machine time; the Review log's last entry lists the changes. Amended a ninth time on 2026-09-27 by the dispositions of the A1 re-review of [40] and [50] revision 2 (M0 WP-80a, `docs/spec/reviews/a1-dispositions.md`): §4.3–§4.6, §4.10, §5b.2, §5e, §7.1, §7.7.5, §8.2 and §8.3 carry them, and the Review log's last entry lists the changes and the frozen bytes they touch.*

**Conventions.** Citations `[NN §name]` point into those documents. Labels such as `G15`, `F-D1`, `N2`, `X4`, `D2` are the critiques' own finding labels and are used unchanged so the resolution ledger in §2.17 can be checked against them; `CB1–CB3`, `CM1–CM9` and `CL1–CL9` are the completeness review's blocker, major and minor findings in the order the review listed them, resolved in the Review log at the end. Numbers carry the tag of their source: **[M]** measured on the owner's machine, **claimed** (vendor/third party), or **est.** (arithmetic with inputs shown); **[doc]** marks a statement taken from vendor documentation as quoted by the cited report (for example `[doc, 70 §1.3]`). The single letters [A]–[D] name proposals A–D only, never a source class. `RG1`–`RG12` are the release-gate criteria of [60 §6], and `GT1`–`GT20` the gates of [60 §3.13]. **CI** means the PR checks on hosted GitHub Actions runners (#36): the build, the lints, the type check and the suites on synthetic data, with no owner data and no model API key; every timing, RAM, floor, crash and kill-loop gate and every gate on owner-derived data runs on the owner's laptop — nightly in its agent-free windows and at each milestone exit (#34, #37) — and the lints and the type check also run in the local pre-merge gate ([60 §3.13]). Everything not tagged is a design decision. Section numbers 0–12 follow the synthesis so that cross-references in the critiques and in [30] still resolve. External precedents (jj's git compatibility and conflicts, git's hash-function transition, gitoxide, Dolt's git remotes, git-bug, hg-git/cinnabar, Fossil, the SQLite session extension, Kleppmann's move operation, Pearce–Kelly, Beads issues and releases, Claude Code hooks/MCP/Workflow docs, Microsoft `LockFileEx`/`NtFlushBuffersFileEx`/`CreateFileMapping` documentation) are cited through those documents, each verified on 2026-09-25 by the report or critique that quotes it. No web request was made while writing this document.

**For implementers.** The contract is §3 (data model), §4 (on-disk format and write path), §5 (version control, git image, git independence, coordination, and §5e move-proof file links), §6 (concurrency) and §7 (agent surface, and §7.7 the query language); §8 holds the budgets and the gates that enforce them, and §8.3 is the normative table of budgets and gates per owner priority (speed, RAM, correctness, tokens); §9 the build order and calendar; §11 the owner decisions — only questions no configuration key can change later — all decided on 2026-09-26 except the port-phase and on-demand items (#42, #45's extras, #21 (d)), which keep recorded defaults ([60 §3.14]), and confirmed by the owner review of 2026-09-27, which amended #34 (the OS-crash rig deferred to after the release) and #38 (LQ-Bench through the Claude Code subscription); §12 what is deliberately not built; §13 every configuration key with its type, default, scope, reload class and trade-off; §14 the OS layer for Windows, Linux and macOS and the unscheduled port phase ([80]).

**Binding inputs.** The owner's brief (graph DB from scratch in Rust, typed fields, git-like versioning, subtasks, blockers, CLI/MCP/skills, synchronous references, maximum performance, minimal RAM, Windows 11) and the owner update of 2026-09-25: **R1** full branching for all versioned data with create/switch/list/delete/diff/log/merge (ideally tags, revert, cherry-pick, reflog/undo); **R2** moirai's own VCS with zero git dependency in the core; **R3** a deterministic git-compatible image that can be saved to and loaded from git. R1–R3 override every earlier position; in particular "single shared trunk + provenance scoping" [04 §9.3], [08 §7.2], [A T3], [C T3] and "coordination plane never branched" [B §3.1] are no longer acceptable branch models. On 2026-09-26 the owner added **R4** (links from nodes to project files, and to places inside them, that survive moves, renames, edits and deletions) and **R5** (moirai's own query language). Their designs are [40] (revision 2, answering its review [41]) and [50] (revision 2, answering its review [51]); they are integrated as §5e (R4) and §7.7 (R5) and in every section they change (§0–§4, §5a–§5d, §6–§12), and §9 schedules them. Where this document summarises them, [40] and [50] stay normative for detail; where the three documents disagreed, the integration decided on the evidence and fixed all of them (Review log, integration entry). On 2026-09-26 the owner also decided that the system must work under macOS and Linux too, without building or testing those binaries for now (owner decision #32, §11): designed now, ported after the Windows release (§14, [80]). Standing instruction of the owner: every agent that builds moirai — design, implementation, review — runs on Opus; it does not govern the models the owner's working agents run inside other harnesses (GPT-5.6-Luna in Codex), which are inputs to #38 and #43 (#38 leaves them unmeasured for now). **The owner's answers of 2026-09-26** to §11 (verbatim translation; quoted once here and cited elsewhere as "the owner's answers of 2026-09-26"): "For now no additional machine will be used, everything is here. Two lanes in parallel. For now benchmarks only on Opus 5.5. The moirai project itself will be stored in a public repository on GitHub. Record that all commits must be made WITHOUT Claude co-authorship. Everything else I approve as you wrote it." §11 records the decisions. **The owner review of 2026-09-27** answered the approval checklist of the Russian description (`docs/architecture-approval-ru/15-approval-checklist.md`; its items А1–А8, Б1–Б14 and В1–В10 are cited in the English documents as A1–A8, B1–B14 and V1–V10, and the review itself as "the owner review of 2026-09-27"): **this document, with the normative designs [40], [50], [60], [80] and [90], is approved as the M0 specification** (A4); A1–A8 are decided as recommended — the re-review of [40] and [50] revision 2 happens inside the independent M0 specification review, before the format freeze (A1); the harness configuration with AI attribution off is committed (A2); the published repository content is reviewed and acceptable (A3); the consistency fixes of 2026-09-27 and the design team's six resolutions stand (A5); LQ-Bench at M0 runs on the reference model's own parser and binder (LQ-3), with §7.7.5 and [50 §7.4] item 6 as the normative GT13 gate list (A6); five R4 replay targets gate M0 and the three resolver-dependent ones gate M6 (A7); the lanes build in windows agreed with the owner at M0 start, none fixed yet (A8) — and B1–B14 are confirmed. Two answers change the plan (verbatim translations): on LQ-Bench's billing (V1), "I will not buy API access; only Claude Code by subscription is available" — LQ-Bench runs through the owner's Claude Code subscription in headless mode, with no API billing and no API key (§7.7.5, §11 #38); on the OS-crash rig (V7), "No way to install it for now; such a deep test is postponed until the release, like mac and linux" — the real OS-crash / power-loss rig (GT15, its calibration, the guest and its licence question, the WSL2 and Memory Integrity choices) is deferred to after the release together with the port phase, crash safety in M0–M11 rests on the fault-model simulator with crash-point enumeration (GT1), GT3, the kill loops (GT4), durable-before-acknowledge, `backup`/`restore` and the daily image export, and real power loss is an explicit, unverified residual risk (§8.2, §9, §10 risk 17, §11 #34, §14). V8 is acknowledged; V2–V6, V9 and V10 remain open resource items for M0. **Repository rule** (binding, not a §11 decision): the moirai repository is public on GitHub (`github.com/bluesteelll/moirai`, #36), and every commit in it is authored by the owner with no Claude or other AI co-author — no `Co-Authored-By:` trailer naming Claude or another AI, and no "Generated with Claude Code" line in a pull-request description; the agents' harness attribution settings are off in the repository's configuration, and the local pre-merge gate (a `commit-msg` check) and PR CI refuse a commit message or pull-request body that carries one ([60 §3.1] item 7). The rule binds from the repository's **first commit**, which precedes M0 (the design documents and `AGENTS.md`, which states the rule, are committed first; Claude Code adds a co-author trailer by default): the attribution settings are turned off in the repository's committed harness configuration — approved by the owner on 2026-09-27 (A2; `.claude/settings.json` for Claude Code; the commits made before it carry no AI trailer) — and until [60 §3.1] item 7 installs the `commit-msg` check the owner reviews each commit message; the owner reviewed the published tree and accepted it on 2026-09-27 (A3; §10 risk 41). **License:** Apache-2.0, copyright Mark Boyko (LICENSE, NOTICE at the repository root; decided by the owner 2026-09-26). Every dependency must carry an Apache-2.0-compatible license (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD, ISC, Zlib or similar).

---

## 0. Executive summary

**What moirai is.** A single static Rust binary that opens a small memory-mapped store in about a millisecond and gives AI coding agents one typed graph for tasks, subtasks, blockers, plans, rules, decisions, findings, verdicts and measurements. The graph is versioned by moirai's own git-shaped version control: a BLAKE3 content-addressed commit DAG of typed changesets with before-images, first-class branches for every datum, a typed three-way merge engine with conflicts stored as data, tags, reflog, undo and revert. The store can be serialized into a deterministic, human-readable git image (one text file per node, one git commit per moirai commit or per checkpoint) and read back, so the data can live in any git repository and travel through any git remote, while git itself is never required to run the system. Agents reach it through a CLI (skill + hooks), a ten-tool MCP server for the roles that have no shell, and budgeted context packs that replace the owner's hand-written briefs. Nodes can point at project files and at places inside them (a symbol, a heading, a quoted passage) through links that survive renames, moves, edits and deletions by anyone (R4), and every question an agent can ask, including every read verb, is a query in moirai's own language Lachesis (R5).

**The architecture in twelve bullets.**

1. **Engine (T1):** an append-only, checksummed operation log is the canonical history (≈ 0.35–0.5 KB per small commit, est., §4.3); current state is materialized into immutable, read-only-mapped, zero-copy columnar segments (node columns, forward and reverse CSR adjacency, frozen bitsets, symbol table, content-addressed bodies) plus a bounded in-process overlay built from the log tail. Open is O(1) plus a bounded tail; nothing is O(history) or O(nodes) on the hot path. Base: [B §4] as adopted by [D §4], with every graft of [20] (G1–G28) and [21] N3 written into the format.
2. **Process model (T2):** purely embedded multi-process; one writer at a time elected by a blocking byte-range lock wait on a dedicated `LOCK` file (`LockFileEx` on Windows, open-file-description locks on Linux and macOS, §14), holding it only to scan, re-validate and append a changeset computed before the lock (the three-phase write, §4.5); a separate flush byte lets one flush cover every writer that appended before it — leaderless group commit on every OS, each group chained to its predecessor and every acknowledgement checked by identity (§2.8, §4.5), lock-free readers, no daemon, no timers, no thread outside a command except the MCP server's one blocked stdin reader, zero idle CPU by construction. The session's MCP server is an ordinary long-lived client; an opportunistic leader is never required for correctness and is built (in M1) only if the M0 measurements require it (§2.2).
3. **Branches (T3′, R1):** `main` is just a branch. A branch is a ref plus a *pinned checkpoint segment set* ⊕ trunk ops to the fork ⊕ the branch's own ops, found through a per-ref op index (G15). Fork costs one durable commit and no copying; ~50 live branches cost tens of MB of pinned disk and no RAM until read. HEAD is per client (explicit `--branch`, then the presented lease, then working-directory bindings and the environment, in [90 §4.1]'s order of record, §5a.4), so many agent processes read and write different branches of one store concurrently.
4. **Merge (T7):** typed per-field three-way merge with the base taken at the LCA state (N1), status lattices, `Incr` counters, add-wins sets, diff3 text, Kleppmann moves; value conflicts land as conflict values (jj), structural violations (dangling edge, cycle, hierarchy cycle, schema) never advance a ref and are staged on `merge/<dst>/from/<src>` (TerminusDB/Dolt). Merges into `main` are sync-first, so the daily path has a unique LCA and criss-cross cannot arise (N7).
5. **Coordination under branching (T16):** everything in the graph is versioned per branch, including `status`/`done` and `blocks`. Only runtime coordination is store-level: leases with fencing tokens, **`settled` and `deleted` markers** written at the *op* level by every path that lands a completion or a deletion on any branch (`complete`, `set --done`, MCP `write` (`tx.complete`, or a `TX` that sets `done`), `apply`, merge, sync, cherry-pick, revert, image import — a task completed or deleted on an unmerged branch is never dispatched again from another branch, N2/D1, CB3) and cleared in O(1) through per-ref *absorbed* vectors instead of an ancestry walk (CM1) — the markers are a cache of an exclusion rule defined on branch *states* (I26′), recomputed whenever `undo`, `op restore` or `branch -D` move a ref [72 M4] — leases whose holder is the session's long-lived MCP server, never a short-lived CLI process [72 B2], idempotency results, change-feed cursors, the `#N` allocator, HEAD bindings, pins and the git id map. Each is justified in §5d.
6. **Git independence (T14, R2):** the store is a `moirai` directory discovered by flag → env → walk-up (`.moirai` directory or pointer file, with store id) → an optional textual `.git`/`commondir` *hint*. Inside a git repository `init` places the store at `<git-common-dir>/moirai/`, outside every working tree and shared by all of the owner's worktrees without pointer files (CM6; [13 §1.3] counted 44 `git worktree list` entries and [41 §1] 46 a day later — the count moves as lanes open and close, so gates test every HEAD `git worktree list` reports); outside a repository it is `./.moirai/`. The core never spawns `git` and never links a git library; provenance is read from two small text files; `moirai init` refuses to shadow a hinted store (D4). `check`/`stale` answer ancestry in-process (the `ANCESTRY` cache, the commit-graph, else a generation-pruned commit walk through the git object layer, CM7); the only `git` spawns left are four explicit, optional call sites — network transport of the image, ref updates in a reftable destination, `doctor lanes --refresh-graph` and `file mv --git` on request — each only when git is present, and a spawn lint and a dependency lint enforce it (§5c, §8.3 GT20).
7. **Git image (T15, R3):** a specified canonical `.moi` text file per node under a two-level `uid` fan-out, out-edges in the source file (tombstones keep their retained out-edges, CM4), bodies byte-exact (CM3), counters as append-only ledger lines, conflict values representable, commit metadata in git trailers that carry **every** field of the canonical form (`Moirai-Hlc`, `Moirai-Git-Base` and the rest, CB1) so a `Moirai-Commit` id is re-derivable from trailers plus a tree diff for every commit kind; `sync` commits are two-parent git commits reconstructed like merges (CM2); no store-local datum in hashed content (the exporting store id and the granularity live in an unhashed side ref, CB2). Export and import are hand-written end to end: the semantic layer runs on moirai's own in-process git object layer (loose objects, packs, idx, commit-graph, refs, bundles; SHA-1 and SHA-256), so exporting and importing never need `git`; the git CLI is an independent test oracle and, when present, the network transport. Default destination: a separate bare image repo, SHA-1, `main` + `tags/*` + `lane/*`, one checkpoint of `main` per merge into `main` and one per day for `main` and every live lane (so unmerged lane work always has an off-store copy, CM8); 1:1 commits on request.
8. **Deletion and "node 40" (T5):** hard delete in the current state, tombstone in history, per-edge-kind policies executed in one commit over the reverse index (O(degree)); a deleted blocker leaves a flagged edge that keeps dependents out of `ready` until resolved or re-pointed (X4); across branches the delete applies where it is received (commit, sync, merge, import) and is visible elsewhere on request and through the `deleted` runtime marker.
9. **Agent surface (T9):** CLI with a frozen output contract (ids first and bare in argv, one line per record, explicit drop footers, exit codes 0–10, bodies via stdin or `-f`), three skills (core `moirai`, `moirai-orchestrate`, the `moirai-ql` card), fail-open hooks that run spawn-free as `mcp_tool` handlers on the session's moirai server wherever it is connected (SessionStart brief and link settle, UserPromptSubmit delta, SubagentStart role pack, SubagentStop lease safety net, PreToolUse stamp on write tools only, file evidence hooks), a ten-tool MCP server (`query` replaces `find`, every tool deferred by default) whose every tool takes an explicit `branch`, and context packs budgeted per role in UTF-8 bytes with per-class quotas; every budget is a `config` key (§13) and every agent-facing surface has a gated size (§8.3 TOKENS). Nothing required depends on one harness (owner decision #43, §7.8, [90]): the required contract is the CLI on `PATH`, the dual-era stdio MCP server with plain-text results and explicit parameters, a ≤ 600-byte block at the top of `AGENTS.md` and a portable skill; Claude Code and Codex are the optimized Tier A targets and every other harness is served by that contract; every hook is an accelerator with a defined fallback, and write rights come only from presented, server-issued leases — the orchestrator's included.
10. **Build order (T13), done right:** no SQLite or other third-party database at any stage, no interim or throwaway stages, no temporary modes and no use before the release gate (owner decisions of 2026-09-26, §2.13). Dependency order: **M0** contract and evidence — the on-disk format v1 frozen with every R1/R3 reservation, the R4/R5 reservations of [40]/[50], the `Vfs` fault model and store parameters for every threshold, and the OS layer specified for Windows, Linux and macOS with every item [80 §3] freezes (§14); the complete Rust reference model; the query surface frozen by an accuracy benchmark; R4's pure file libraries validated on the owner's corpora; measurements and the measurement protocol; the public GitHub repository with hosted CI for the synthetic checks (#36), the laptop-only infrastructure profile L (#34: no test host; every timing, crash and kill-loop gate runs on the owner's laptop); **M1** storage engine with the final multi-process protocol, recovery, the physical branch machinery and the section registry; **M2** graph core; **M3** version control (R1); **M4** in-process git object layer; **M5** git image (R3); **M6** file-link runtime (R4); **M7** query language (R5); **M8** CLI (R2 user-visible); **M9** agent interface; **M10** MCP; **M11** release hardening to the "complete and hardened" gate. Linux and macOS are implemented afterwards, in a port phase that is documented with its gates but not scheduled (§14, [80 §5]). R4's graph layer and merge rules are built inside M2 and M3. Each milestone meets its final specification and passes its full gates before anything builds on it; the test workstream (fault-model simulation, crash enumeration, kill loops, fuzzers, mutation testing, differential tests against the reference model and the git CLI) runs from M0 [60]; the OS-crash loop on a virtual machine (GT15) is deferred to after the release, with the port phase (the owner review of 2026-09-27, §9).
11. **File links (R4, §5e, [40]):** a link is an `at` edge from any node to a file node (the widened `artifact` kind, identity derived from the registration path) carrying anchors captured from what agents already write (`path`, `path:L-M`, `path::Type/fn`, `path#Heading`): quote + context + ±16-line window + scope, never a bare line number. Link *intent* (paths, content id, anchors, removal intent, directory-move history as the versioned `path_moves` field) branches, merges and travels through the image; *resolution* is per worktree, runtime-only, never hashed or exported. Explicit file verbs (`file mv`/`rm` with a crash-safe intent record) plus lazy, deterministic re-binding: reads compute link states live and write nothing; settle points write re-binds only on exact evidence (file or parent-directory id, captured intent, a git rename inside one commit), only from the branch's single writer tree, only when that tree has received the observation; equal content alone never re-binds, absence never deletes. No watcher, no git process, zero idle CPU; ≤ 3 ms per 50-link pack.
12. **Query language (R5, §7.7, [50]):** Lachesis (LQ), a Cypher-shaped, GQL-tolerant, requirement-traced language with Cypher/GQL counting semantics, two-valued logic with an explicit absent value, the engine's derived state as built-ins (`ready`, `unblocked`, `blockers()`, `link_state()`), a git/jj revision grammar (`USE lane/x`, `main~5`, `diff(main...lane/x)`) and guarded `TX` writes (one block = one commit, `EXPECT`, `IF TIP`, `DRY`). Every read verb is a named query and every write verb a named mutation, so there is one parser, planner and executor behind the whole surface; reads hold only a read-only view and never write; budgets are counted and deterministic (exit 10 when a result is incomplete). The surface is frozen at M0 by an accuracy benchmark (LQ-Bench) run on the reference model.

**Why this design.** Every report agrees on the engine family: path-copy Merkle state costs 4 KiB × depth per tiny commit (~52 KB measured on DoltLite [04 §3.2]) while changesets cost bytes [04 §6]; Windows forbids resizing mapped files and mapped views are not coherent with `WriteFile` [05 §6.1–6.2], so only immutable files are mapped; a durable flush costs ~2 ms on the owner's NVMe [M, 05 §2.2] and 3–20 ms on Apple silicon [X17 §3.2], so flushes are coalesced (§2.8), and process spawn 15–73 ms [M, 05 §2.1], so the engine is a small share of an agent call and O(1) open matters more than micro-optimisation; the owner's machine has 1.8 GB free with 16 agent processes resident [M, 05 §2.4] and a zero-idle-CPU rule for benchmark windows [02 §9]. Proposal D is the only design that meets R1–R3 and it inherits the best layer of each earlier proposal (A's protocol and invariants, B's merge rigour and hygiene, C's agent surface); the critics scored it first on all three lenses (7.5 / 7.0 / 7.0) and found no flaw that requires abandoning its structure. This document is D with every critique finding resolved (§2.17), every blocker and major finding of the completeness review fixed in place (Review log), the two daily-path wrong answers (N1, N2/D1) closed by rule, the two format-level defects (F-D1, F-D3) fixed before the first byte is written, and the build ordered by technical dependency, each component built once to its final specification (§9, [60]).

**The biggest risks.** (1) The multi-process file protocol on Windows: SQLite's WAL-reset race hid for 16 years and fell to deterministic simulation in minutes [08 §3.1]; Beads lost 7 of 8 acknowledged closes under agent load [03 §2.7]. Mitigation: readers never read past `committed_lsn`, the ref move is inside the commit record, simulation with crash/fsync/lock-delay injection, crash-point enumeration over subsets of unflushed writes and 16-writer kill loops gate the storage milestone (M1) before anything is built on it, and every acknowledgement follows a covering flush (counted); `moirai backup`/`restore` (M1) and the daily image export of `main` and every lane bound what an OS crash on this host can cost (the development machine has experienced OS crashes and disk-full events [02 §9], CM8). The OS-crash loop on a virtual machine (bugchecks and hard power-offs, GT15) is deferred to after the release (the owner review of 2026-09-27), so real power loss stays an explicit, unverified residual risk until it runs (§10 risk 17). (2) Branch isolation fighting the workflow: lanes miss rules and completions on `main` if nobody syncs. Mitigation: `~main` rules in every pack, `behind main` notices in every brief and hook, `settled`/`deleted` markers, `sync --check` gating; the `shared` field class is not built (#1, decided 2026-09-26: choosing it later is a format-version-2 change). (3) Image determinism drift across formats and stores. Mitigation: no store-local data in hashed content, byte-identical round-trip property tests, retained encoders per format version, the exporter re-parsing what it writes. (4) Build size and time to first use: two long poles (protocol and image determinism), R4 (≈ 68–93 units) and R5 (≈ 56–83 units) on top, and no use before the release gate (≈ 369–508.5 units with every delta since the pre-audit baseline — the audits, the cross-platform design and the harness-agnostic design; release at ≈ 35–75 weeks with two lanes, both building on the owner's laptop, which also carries every timing, crash and kill-loop gate in agent-free windows (profile L, #2, #34): P50 ≈ 50.5, P90 ≈ 60.5, est. [60 §7], re-issued on 2026-09-27 without the deferred OS-crash rig; re-issued with measured velocity at the M0 and M1 exits, §9). Mitigation: dependency order with a second lane for leaf components, every decision, measurement and accuracy experiment that shapes the frozen format settled before M0 exits, the reference model as the executable specification, and validation on recorded data instead of live use [60 §8]. Both lanes and every gate share one laptop with ≈ 1.8 GB free under a campaign: M0 item 21 measures a lane's peak, and the lanes build in owner-agreed windows under one build semaphore (§10 risk 40). (5) Merge-semantics creep: the type set is closed and rules are schema data.

---
