Mutexes for non-task resources (benchmark slot, merge queue) are `task{work_kind = mutex}` with lease semantics ([C §3.2] `resource`, [07 §7.4] "merge slots"). A "critical note about the project" is a `note` or `rule` with `criticality = critical`; it sorts first in every brief and pack.

### 3.3 Edge kinds and policies

Edges are binary, typed, keyed by `(src, kind, dst)` with set semantics, stored in both directions in the same commit (I-P3). A sparse side table holds the edge properties: `pinned_commit` (16 B commit-id prefix) for `cites`/`implements`/`derived_from`, and R4's anchor records for `at`. The edge key gains an optional 128-bit **discriminator** (the anchor uid) so one node can cite several places in one file; it is empty for every other kind ([40] R-4). Every edge kind also carries, as schema data, an LQ name that reads correctly in its stored direction, reverse aliases, a reading and declared endpoint kinds ([50] F1, §7.7); `contradicts` and `relates` are symmetric for reading, and `blocks` sources are tasks or questions (as §7.6 uses).

| Edge (src → dst) | Class | Acyclicity / cardinality | On dst deleted | On src deleted |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `parent` (child → parent) | structural | forest, depth ≤ 12 | **restrict** (`--cascade` deletes the subtree; `--reparent` moves children up) | drop; rollups updated |
| `blocks` (A → B): A done before B starts | structural | part of the precedence DAG (I5′), PK | drop (the dependent B is gone; A's `is_blocker` recomputed) | **re-point** with `--replaced-by` (the edge becomes `replacement → B`), else **flag** (`has_dangling` on B; B stays out of `ready` until `resolve`) (X4 — a deleted blocker never silently unblocks) |
| `gates` (verdict → task): completion blocked while the verdict is `open` with `outcome ∈ fail_*` | structural | checked for acyclicity with `blocks` | drop (the gated task is gone) | flag as above (a deleted failing verdict never silently ungates) |
| `merge_after` (lane → lane) | structural | DAG | drop + notify | drop |
| `runs_in` (run → lane) | structural | ≤ 1 | restrict | drop |
| `answers` (decision/note → question) | structural | ≤ 1 active | restrict | drop; question reopens |
| `scoped_to` (knowledge → area) | structural | many | restrict or `--reassign` to the parent area | drop |
| `duplicate_of` (dup → canonical) | structural | chain length 1 | restrict or re-point to canonical | drop |
| `depends_on` (section → section) | structural | DAG | drop + src `suspect` | drop |
| `supersedes` (new → old) | historical | acyclic; **≤ 1 active superseder per target** (X12) | tombstone ref | old stays superseded; `doctor` warns |
| `derived_from` (note/summary/verdict → source) | historical | acyclic by construction | tombstone ref + **src `suspect`** | — |
| `cites` (any → knowledge, `pinned_commit`) | historical | — | tombstone ref + **src `suspect`** | — |
| `implements` (task/artifact → decision/section, `pinned_commit`) | historical | — | tombstone ref; src `suspect` if the target is superseded | — |
| `refutes` / `confirms` (finding/measurement → finding/decision/rule) | historical | — | tombstone ref | — |
| `verifies` (measurement/verdict → finding/task/decision) | historical | — | tombstone ref | — |
| `addresses` (task/artifact → finding) | historical | — | tombstone ref | — |
| `about` (finding/verdict/measurement/question → target) | historical | ≤ 1 typical | tombstone ref | — |
| `discovered_from` (new → task) | historical | acyclic by construction | tombstone ref | — |
| `produced` / `consumed` (run → node/artifact) | historical | — | tombstone ref | — |
| `contradicts` (rule ↔ rule, one direction stored) | historical | — | tombstone ref | — |
| `mentions` (any → any; parsed from `#N` in title/abstract/body at write time, only for `N < next_id`, sigil rule: `#N` not preceded by an alphanumeric and not followed by `/` or `.digit`) | historical | — | tombstone ref, rendered `#40 (deleted c812 by dev#2: "dup of #52" → #52)` | recomputed from text |
| `relates` | historical | — | tombstone ref | — |
| `at` (any → `artifact`; R4 link, §5e.2) | historical | ≥ 1 anchor per edge (I-F3); one CSR entry per (src, dst), anchors in `ANCHORS` keyed (src, dst, anchor) | tombstone ref + **src `suspect`**; a file node that is `removed` (`file rm`, `links fix --drop`) also makes its referrers `suspect` | anchors kept with the tombstone's retained out-edges (I39′) |

`finding.where {file:symbol@sha}` is an `at` edge with a symbol anchor, and a `cites` to a file is an `at` edge with a `pinned` anchor ([40 §2.8]); `produced`/`consumed` targets are file nodes; `lane.worktree_path` and `run.script_path`/`journal_path` are `abs` artifacts (existence only).

A verdict never carries `blocks` (X5): `gates` constrains `complete` (exit 6 with the gating verdict named), never `claim`, so a failed review does not deadlock the fix round when a lease expires or a task is reopened.

### 3.4 Invariants (checked on every write; re-checked after any merge, import, revert and by `doctor --verify`)

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| I1 | `#N` is unique across all branches, allocated under the writer byte, never reused (including across imports and GC: a `#N` binds to at most one `uid` over the store's life, I35′); `uid` is unique. Creating a derived-uid node (R4 file and root nodes) whose uid the store already knows, on any branch, reuses that uid's `#N`, so `#812` means the same file in every lane (I-F2); the store-wide `UIDX` (uid → `#N`, folded at checkpoints, indexed from the tail's `Create` ops) is probed under the writer byte, so two unmerged lanes that link one file get one `#N` [72 M7]. |
| I2 | Every structural edge has live endpoints in the same visible version; a flagged (`has_dangling`) blocker edge is the only exception and it excludes its dependent from `ready`. |
| I3 | Historical edges may reference dead ids; they resolve through the tombstone view. |
| I4 | `parent` is a forest with depth ≤ 12. |
| I5′ | The combined precedence graph `blocks ∪ gates ∪ child→parent ∪ {X→D : blocks(X,P), X ∉ subtree(P), D ∈ subtree(P)}` is acyclic; a node never blocks its own descendant; blockers are inherited from outside the subtree only; `link X --blocks P` on a container checks every descendant; `move` re-derives the exogenous classification for edges touching the moved subtree. |
| I6 | `supersedes(new, old)` implies `old.status = superseded` in the same commit; ≤ 1 active superseder per target. |
| I7 | The target of `duplicate_of` is canonical. |
| I8 | Status transitions follow the kind's machine; `blocked`, `ready`, `stale`, `claimed`, container-`done` are never stored as source truth. |
| I9 | Every derived counter, bitset, `topo` and the reverse CSR equal a full recomputation (property tests, `doctor --verify`). |
| I10 | Every mutation belongs to exactly one commit carrying provenance (actor, role, session, git head/branch/worktree, branch ref, idempotency key hash, message). |
| I11 | Fields conform to the schema version of their commit; symbols are never GC'd; enum integers are never reused. |
| I12 | Every branch head satisfies I1–I11 at all times; a merge, import, revert or cherry-pick with a structural violation never advances a ref (staged on `merge/*`). |
| I13 | A `finding` with `f_kind ∈ {perf, complexity}` reaches `fixed` only with an `addresses` edge from a different actor **and** a `verifies` edge from a review verdict, not a test run alone ("retest is not re-review" [01 §3]). |
| I14 | A `run` closes `green` only when every `expected_artifacts` symbol has a `produced` artifact whose `oid` was read back ("0 errors but no plan file" [01 §7 L2]). |
| I14′ | An idempotency key is bound to its payload hash and branch; a hit with a different payload is exit 9. |
| I17′ | A lease mutation must present the current fencing token; expiry never bumps the token; the same holder may renew an expired, unreclaimed lease. |
| I18′ | As-of output carries no derived fields unless `--recompute` or a query projects them; a derived predicate a query uses at a past view is recomputed there and charged to the query's budget; runtime and tree-derived state at a past view is an error (E302), never a value (§7.7). |
| I25′ | For every key untouched on side S since the LCA, `merge` never emits a conflict on that key; the base of a key is its value at the LCA. |
| I26′ | **Defined on states** [72 M4]: `#N` is excluded from `ready`/`claim` on branch R, and never listed there as a live blocker, iff some live ref X ≠ R of kind `work` holds `#N` done, cancelled or deleted at `tip(X)` and the commit on X's history that last set that state is not an ancestor-or-self of `tip(R)`. The engine's `settled`/`deleted` markers and absorbed vectors are a cache of this definition (keyed `(#N, ref_id, commit)`, `cleared` scoped to `(#N, ref_id)`, derived from net ops, recomputed when `undo`, `op restore` or `branch -D` move refs; staging refs produce none); the reference model evaluates the definition itself. |
| I27′ | Every commit reachable in the log after recovery is reachable from a ref, the reflog or a pin, or is marked orphan and never satisfies an idempotency lookup. |
| I28′ | The git object ids of a moirai commit are a function of moirai data, the object format and the destination's anchor-text mode (`full` or `hash-only`, recorded in the unhashed side ref); the moirai commit id is a function of moirai data only, because the canonical form hashes anchor text through digests (§4.6, [72 M6]). |
| I29′ | Importing an image of the same format version reproduces every native commit id; a demotion affects one commit only. |
| I30′ | A foreign two-parent git commit is imported as a moirai merge computed by the typed rules; counters are never taken from a text merge. |
| I31′ | `merge` has exactly one base-selection rule for multiple LCAs. |
| I32′ | `rm` refuses while any live lease covers the `#N` on any branch unless `--release`. |
| I33′ | On `plan/*`, `status`/`resolution`/`assignee`/claims are read-only; `blocks`, `parent`, `gates` are writable and validated. |
| I34′ | `revert`/`cherry-pick` stage on `NotFound` and record `DATA` mismatches as conflict values. |
| I36′ | `claimed`, `settled`, `deleted` markers and lease state are never versioned and never exported. |
| I37′ | At merge, `parent` moves are applied and the implied I5′ edges re-derived before any precedence edge is checked. |
| I38′ | Every field of a commit's canonical form (§4.6) has exactly one carrier in the image (§5b.4 table); the importer recomputes every native commit id from the trailers plus the tree diff against the first parent, for every commit kind including `sync`. |
| I39′ | A tombstone file carries every out-edge the store retains for the dead node (flagged structural edges marked `flagged`, historical edges as-is), so `has_dangling` and the reverse index after import equal the exporting store's. |
| I40′ | Body bytes survive export and import exactly; the only normalisation a body ever receives is CRLF → LF in the store at write time, never in the image codec. |
| I41′ | Staging refs exist per `(dst, src)` pair; a staged merge or sync of one pair never blocks another pair. |
| I42′ | A commit's `affected` list names every node whose value of any derived predicate (including `unblocked`) changed, or the commit carries `affected_complete = 0` and as-of derivation recomputes instead of trusting it ([50] F15, F16). |
| I43′ | A commit's unhashed `append_hlc` is monotonic in `seq` order (checked on append) ([50] F14). |
| I-F1…I-F14 | R4's invariants ([40 §2.10]; summarised in §5e.8): one live present/planned file node per (root, exact path) per view; derived uids equal their derivation; every `at` edge carries ≥ 1 anchor; no machine-local datum in versioned, hashed or exported data; reads append nothing; automatic re-binds only on exact evidence, from the writer tree, when fresh; `removed` never inferred from absence; path rules; no bare-line-number anchor; `resolve` is a pure function; hands off project files; one designated tree per branch; no content-only re-bind; no resurrection. |
| I-P3 | Reverse adjacency equals the inverse of forward adjacency in every visible version. |

### 3.5 Derived state (each predicate defined once in the engine, maintained eagerly for affected nodes only)

| Derived | Definition | Maintenance / cost |
|---|---|---|
| `open_blockers[n]` | count of `blocks`/`gates` in-edges whose source is not done/accepted, plus flagged dangling blocker edges | ±1 on source status change or edge add/remove/flag, O(out-degree) |
| `open_blockers_exo[n]` | as above, excluding sources inside n's subtree | decided at edge-add time by walking the source's ancestors, O(depth) |
| **`unblocked`** (the persisted bitset; R5's name for the structural predicate, [50 §3.8], owner decision #27) | `kind = task ∧ status = open ∧ ¬deleted ∧ ¬conflicted ∧ ¬container ∧ open_blockers = 0 ∧ no ancestor with open_blockers_exo > 0` (flagged dangling edges count) `∧ defer_until ≤ now()` — the bitset holds only the structural clauses; `defer_until` is evaluated per candidate at the view's `now()` | valid at any view; membership recomputed for touched nodes; the ancestor clause walks ≤ 12 parents per candidate at query time |
| **`ready`** (read-time, tip only) | `unblocked ∧ no live lease by another holder ∧ no active settled/deleted marker whose ref position the reading branch has not absorbed ∧ defer_until ≤ wall-clock now` — exactly the `moirai ready` verb and LQ's `t.ready` | the runtime clauses are applied at read time: one `MARKERS` probe by `#N` plus one `absorbed[ref_id]` lookup per marker — O(1), no DAG walk (CM1); at a past view `ready` is an error with `unblocked` as the fix (E302) |
| `blocked`, `unfinished` | `blocked` = `kind = task ∧ unfinished ∧ (open_blockers > 0 ∨` an ancestor has an open exogenous blocker`)`; `unfinished` = `¬done` for kinds with a status machine | any view; from the same counters |
| `is_blocker` bitset | not done ∧ has an outgoing `blocks` edge to a not-done task; "ids of all blocking tasks" = this bitset ∧ `kind:task` | O(out-degree) |
| rollups `children_total`, `children_done`, `ready_to_close` | direct children; container with all children done | O(depth) on child status change or reparent |
| `suspect` | a `derived_from`/`cites`/`implements`/`depends_on` target was retracted, superseded, deleted, or its current commit differs from the edge's `pinned_commit`; or an `at` target file node is `removed` or deleted (R4, graph state only); purely derived from `(target state, pinned_commit)`; "re-confirm" re-pins the edge | O(closure) along reverse derivation edges, op-budgeted at 10k |
| `stale` (measurement, note) | `measured_on`/`observed_git_sha` is not an ancestor of the bound worktree's git tip, or files under `applies_to` changed since (for artifacts, R4's tree-derived link state replaces `stale`, next row) | on demand only (`stale`, `check`), never on the pack/brief/ready path; ancestry from the `ANCESTRY` cache, the commit-graph when it holds both commits, else the git object layer's in-process commit walk inside these two verbs, cached (§2.14, CM7) |
| link state, anchor state (R4, read-time, tree-derived) | [40 §2.9]: `ok`, `moved-auto`, `moved-needs-confirm`, `ambiguous`, `deleted`, `replaced`, `stale-anchor`, `missing`, `absent-in-tree`, `pending`, `planned`, `unverified`; anchors `fresh`/`moved`/`edited`/`ambiguous`/`orphaned` — resolved live against the caller's resolved worktree (§5e.3) | computed at read time at a tip with an eligible tree, never stored, never gates `ready`; 1 stat per link (one enumeration per directory when ≥ 4 rendered links share it), ≤ 20 ms of file work per read command; anchor results cached in the runtime `ANCHORRES` table per (anchor uid, file `oid`, resolver version), written by settles and the edit-evidence hook and shared by every process, so a read re-hashes only files edited since the last observation [70 S7] |
| `answered` (question) | a live `answers` edge is visible on the reading branch | O(1) |
| `conflicted` | an unresolved conflict value exists on this node on this branch | O(1) flag set by merge, cleared by `resolve` |
| `diverged` (read-time, `--across` only) | another listed branch holds a different value for the same key | computed while reading; never stored |
| `settled_elsewhere`, `deleted_elsewhere` (read-time) | an active runtime marker for this `#N` names a ref position `(ref_id, ref_seq)` that the reader's branch has not absorbed: `absorbed_R[ref_id] < ref_seq` (§5d.1) | one `MARKERS` probe + one vector lookup, O(1), ~0.2–1 µs, independent of the marker's age (CM1) |
| critical path | longest open path in the precedence DAG under a subtree | on demand, DP over `topo` order |
| review-loop termination | `count(finding{about ⊂ target, status = confirmed, severity ≥ important, ¬fixed}) = 0` | bitset intersection (`stats loop`) |
| refuted share | per critic, per round: refuted / raised | bitset counts |

### 3.6 Status machines (guarded transitions)

- **task**: `open → in_progress → done`; `open|in_progress → cancelled|deferred|frozen`; `deferred → open`; `done → open` only through `reopen --reason` (an explicit op incrementing `reopen_count`; never a merge artefact). `phase_state` is advanced by verdicts (`return_to` moves it back explicitly). `complete` refuses (exit 6) while any child is open or an open `fail_*` verdict `gates` the task.
- **finding**: `open → confirmed|refuted|deferred|withdrawn`; `confirmed → fixed` (I13). A refutation is a finding with a `refutes` edge; the status move is a separate guarded write, never an automatic flip.
- **question**: `open → answered` (with an `answers` edge and an answer) or `dropped`; reopen explicit.
- **verdict**: `open → accepted|superseded`.
- **knowledge** (note, rule, decision, doc): `proposed|draft → active|accepted|current → superseded` only together with a `supersedes` edge; `retracted` with a reason; `archived`.
- **lane**: `active → ready_to_merge → merge_pending → merged`; side `frozen`, `abandoned`, `measuring`.
- **run**: `running → green|red|stopped|died` (I14).
- **measurement**: `current → moved_declared|retracted`.

---

## 4. Storage engine (final)

### 4.1 Files (all under `<store>/` — `<git-common-dir>/moirai/` inside a repository, `.moirai/` otherwise; a dozen stable files plus sealed blob/hist files plus pinned checkpoint sets)

| File | Size / growth | Mapped? | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HEAD` | 2 × 4 KiB checksummed slots | no (`pread` only) | cache of the current sequence, segment set, pointers to the ref/pin/head tables, counters (§4.2) |
| `LOCK` | 36 KiB | no | layout v1 ([80] X-F1), created only by `init`/`restore`, never deleted or resized: `LockHdr` at 0; writer diagnostics `{seq, ProcId, session hash, command, hlc}` at 2048; the leader record (reserved) at 3072; 256 liveness-slot records × 128 B `{kind, nonce, primary and alias session hashes (16 B each, [80] X-F1), ProcId}` at 4096. Lock bytes carry no data and lie beyond EOF: 2^62 + {0 writer, 1 leader (only if built, §2.2), 2 maintenance, 3 quiet-advisory, 4 **flush** (group commit, §2.8)}, and slot i at 2^62 + 2^16 + i, held by a session's MCP server (Codex: a thread's) from the moment it knows that identity — at start, or lazily at its first call carrying `_meta.threadId` ([90 §4.4]) — until it exits (the lease holder anchor of §6.2, [72 B2]) or by a CLI for the life of one file intent. `LockFileEx` on Windows, OFD locks on Linux and macOS; ownership decided in user space first; one fixed lock order ([80 §2.2]) |
| `log.NNNN` | 64 MiB extents whose bytes beyond the tail read as zero, created by the per-file-system method of [80 §2.3.3] (zero-filled with one full `FlushFileBuffers` on NTFS) and made durable (`durable+meta` + `durable-name`) before the first commit in them is acknowledged; one `durable` flush covers each set of pending groups (§2.8); a group never spans two extents (a rotation pads the old extent with one `Noop` group); rotated when full; ≤ 4 kept as active history before retirement | no (explicit I/O; never mapped) | canonical history: commits, ref moves, client heads, leases, markers, idempotency results, checkpoints, lazy records |
| `hist.NNNN` | sealed compressed frames (the codec of §8.2 item 6; expected `ruzstd` zstd frames, [90 §11.3]) of at most 256 commits **and at most 1 MiB raw** (a larger commit is its own frame of independently decompressible ≤ 1 MiB blocks, so decoding one commit never costs more than 2 MB, [71 RAM-B1]) + a per-frame commit index (`id16 → lsn`); each frame header carries `first_seq`, `last_seq`, `first_append_hlc`, `last_append_hlc` ([50] F8) | yes, read-only | retired log extents; the commit index lives here, never in the base segment |
| `seg.base.G` | ≈ 99 B/node hot + titles/fields + blob table | yes, read-only | materialized `main` state at generation G |
| `seg.dK` | small, tiered (≤ 3 live before a fold) | yes, read-only | delta segments: touched rows, list replacements, bitset ± lists, new tombstones/symbols |
| `seg.b<ref_id>.K` | one set per promoted branch (M1); `ref_id` and K both decimal | yes, read-only | promoted branch delta segment + `TOUCH` bitmap |
| `blobs.NNNN` | bodies only; one file per delta checkpoint that sealed bodies, merged at every tiered delta fold and at rollup | yes, read-only, **mapped lazily on first body read** through `BLOBTAB.file` | content-addressed (BLAKE3-128) dictionary-compressed bodies in the codec of §8.2 item 6 (expected `lz4_flex` blocks with `dict.D`); sealed, never extended while mapped |
| `dict.D` | ≤ 64 KiB with LZ4 (32–110 KiB with zstd) | yes | raw-content dictionary D (retrained at rollup when bodies grew > 25 %); absent if §8.2 item 6 chooses no dictionary; a formatted (magic-prefixed) zstd dictionary if it chooses an own zstd-format encoder ([90 §11.3] option (3)) |
| `gitmap.NNNN` | 41 B per commit per algorithm per destination | yes | sealed pages of `(commit_id16, dest u8, algo u8, git_oid[32])` sorted by commit id with a fan-out table and probed in place (never loaded into a per-process hash set, [71 RAM-m3]); tail entries are `GitMap` log records |
| `cs.NNNN` | one per **bulk commit** (a changeset above `store.commit.inline-max-bytes`, 1 MiB) | yes, read-only | a sealed changeset segment in delta-segment layout, streamed with bounded memory and flushed before the `Commit` record that names it (file number, length, BLAKE3); readers map it as one more delta layer, the next checkpoint folds it, `hist` keeps the reference ([71 RAM-B1]) |
| `config` | text, git-config syntax | no | the **store-scope** configuration keys of §13 (every operational policy with a documented default the owner may change, never an owner decision); user-scope keys (machine paths, remotes, discovery) live in `%APPDATA%\moirai\config` on Windows and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/moirai/config` on Linux and macOS ([80 §2.12]); a rewrite goes through `<store>/tmp/` and a durable replace-rename; `HEAD.config_gen` tells the long-lived MCP server to re-read ([74 A10]) |
| `trash/<intent>/` | files moved aside by `moirai file rm --trash` (same volume only) | no | never mapped; purged by `gc` after `gc.trash-expire` (14 d) ([40 §3.5]) |
| `tmp/` | spill runs of ≤ 1 MiB for the external sorts of bulk producers | no | removed by the orphan sweep |

Rules: no file is ever truncated or renamed over while it can be mapped; growth is by new files; reclamation writes a new file and switches `HEAD`; old files are deleted after `HEAD` has pointed elsewhere for 60 s and no pin references them, tolerating delete-pending (files are opened with `FILE_SHARE_READ|WRITE|DELETE`); segments under construction use a temp name and an orphan sweep runs in the next maintenance holder and in `doctor` (G14). Every sealed file is created under its final monotonic number (or renamed with a directory barrier), made durable with `durable+meta` and `durable-name` before the record that names it, and then set read-only on disk (`FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY` / `0444`; GC clears the attribute before deleting on Windows); its header states its total length, which is checked against `fstat` before it is mapped, and a fault inside a mapping exits 7 naming the file ([80 §2.5]). Store file names are built from decimal numbers and fixed ASCII words only ([80] X-F10): a promoted branch's segment is `seg.b<ref_id>.K` with the decimal, never-reused `ref_id`, never a ref name, so a name like `lane/x` never creates a subdirectory and fold-equal ref names never collide on NTFS or APFS. The store is refused wherever one of its guarantees cannot hold ([80 §2.6]); the allow-list admits only crash-gated file systems: NTFS on Windows (ReFS/Dev Drive, FAT, exFAT, network, OneDrive, `\\wsl$`/`\\wsl.localhost` and other UNC paths are refused); ext4, XFS and btrfs on Linux (ZFS, f2fs and bcachefs until gated, NFS, SMB, FUSE and virtiofs, 9p — WSL2 `/mnt/*` — tmpfs and every overlayfs are refused); local APFS on macOS (HFS+ until gated, non-local volumes, iCloud Drive, File Provider roots and iCloud-managed `~/Desktop`/`~/Documents` are refused). In M0–M11 NTFS is admitted on the GT1/GT3 fault-model evidence and GT4 on real NTFS; its GT15 variant runs with the OS-crash rig, deferred to after the release (§10 risk 17). Byte-range locks do not cross a kernel boundary, so a store is touched by one kernel only ([72 §6 W-m], [X18 §9]). The full check runs at `init` and `restore` (with a durable-write, directory-sync and lock probe; `ENOTSUP` refuses), and a cheap classification with the OS-version check runs at every open. Pinned checkpoint sets (branch bases, tags) keep their base and delta files alive across rollups; `doctor` reports pinned files and their holders. **File count (CL3)**: the stable set is `HEAD`, `LOCK`, `config`, ≤ 4 active `log` extents, one base segment, ≤ 3 delta segments and one dictionary — a dozen files — plus one `hist` file per retired extent and at most one `blobs` file per delta checkpoint since the last fold (1–2 per day; the tiered fold of d1..d3 also merges their blob files into one and a rollup merges all of them), plus `gitmap` pages and the files pinned by branches and tags. A process maps only the segments in the current set and the blob files it actually reads, so the O(checkpoints) blob/hist files cost directory entries, not mappings or Defender scans on the commit path.

### 4.2 `HEAD` slot (4 KiB, `zerocopy`, G18 layout)

```
HeadSlot {
  magic "MOIR", format u16, flags u16 (bit0 quiet, bit1 fts_tier2, bit2 readonly, bit3 retired),
  slot_seq u64,            // increments per publish; readers take the valid slot with the higher value
  epoch u64,               // store epoch: random at init; re-rolled on restore/repair (G25)
  committed_lsn u64,       // visibility bound: readers never read past it
  durable_lsn u64,         // end of the last flushed group; advanced only by durable publishes ([72 B1])
  boot_id [16],            // boot identity: constant for one boot, invariant under clock steps, suspend and hibernation (Windows BootId counter, Linux boot_id, macOS kern.bootsessionuuid, [80 §2.7.1]); unreadable → Unknown-boot mode
  config_gen u32,          // bumped by `moirai config set`; the MCP server re-reads config when it changes ([74 A10])
  checkpoint_lsn u64,      // first log record not folded into a segment
  commit_seq u64,          // last commit seq (change-feed cursor)
  next_id u32,             // #N allocator (non-versioned)
  next_anchor u32,         // R4 anchor-handle allocator `aN` (non-versioned; [40] R-6, the former reserved u32)
  fence u64,               // lease fencing-token allocator (non-versioned)
  active_log u32, n_segments u8, _ [3]u8,
  segments [SegRef; 8],    // {file_no u32, kind u8, upto_lsn u64, blake3_16} = 29 B each
  refs_lsn u64,            // lsn of the newest RefTable record (folded into the REFS section at checkpoint)
  pins_lsn u64,            // newest Pin record  → PINS section
  heads_lsn u64,           // newest ClientHead record → HEADS section
  markers_lsn u64,         // newest settled/deleted/cleared record → MARKERS section
  image_cursor [4]{dest u8, algo u8, seq u64},
  seq_ring [32]{seq u64, lsn u64},   // recent commits for cheap `changes --since`
  reserved …,
  xxh3_128 [16]
}
```

The slot holds no variable-size table. The ref table (name → `{ref_id u32 (never reused), kind, commit_id, lsn, base_pin, fork_commit, fork_seq, ops_since_fork, overlay_ops u32, overlay_bytes u32, ref_seq_next u32, promoted_seg, gen, absorbed: [(ref_id, ref_seq)]}` ≈ 68 B + 6 B × live refs per ref, ≈ 18 KB in `REFS` at 50 refs; `overlay_ops`/`overlay_bytes` count what a view of the ref must replay beyond its promoted segment — trunk ops to the fork, own ops and every synced window since the last promotion — maintained in O(1) per commit and sync and used by promotion, §5a.3, [70 S1]), the pin table (segment file → refcount + holders), the client-head table and the marker table live in log records (`RefTable`, `Pin`, `ClientHead`, `Marker`) whose newest lsn the slot names; a checkpoint folds them into the `REFS`/`PINS`/`HEADS`/`MARKERS` segment sections, so a fresh process reads the section plus the records since. Cost: one extra `pread` per open (10–170 µs). The slot is written after the commit flush and **not** flushed itself on the commit path (1PC+C). Because an OS crash may leave the on-disk slot older **or newer** than the durable log ([72 B1]; fault model (1)), three rules make it safe:
- **Durable bound.** `durable_lsn` names the end of the last flushed group. A lazy append publishes `committed_lsn` beyond it; a durable publish sets both. Recovery never trusts `committed_lsn` as a starting point: it scans from `min(durable_lsn, checkpoint_lsn)`.
- **Boot check.** Every process compares `boot_id` with the current boot before its first read. On a mismatch it takes the flush byte and then the writer byte (the lock order, [80 §2.2.3]), scans from `min(durable_lsn, checkpoint_lsn)` and re-writes every complete group in `(durable_lsn, end]`, releases the writer byte, flushes the log, re-takes the writer byte to republish with the current `boot_id` (a read-modify-write, §4.5), and flushes `HEAD` once — once per boot, off the commit path — and only then reads. A process that cannot read its boot identity runs in Unknown-boot mode ([80 §2.7.1]): it skips this check and never republishes `boot_id`, its readers rely on the validity rules of §4.3 and §4.7, and its writers publish any acknowledged but unpublished group at their next flush. No reader therefore serves a pre-crash view, and an acknowledged commit whose publish was lost is visible to the first reader after the reboot.
- **Two-slot barrier.** Before any file deletion, extent retirement or recycling — and only after its own `Checkpoint` has been published and passed its identity check (§4.5) — maintenance makes sure both slots name the post-deletion state (a no-op publish under the writer byte if needed; every publish is a read-modify-write of the newest slot, so none can republish the old set) and then flushes `HEAD` (`durable+meta`) **outside the writer byte** — one flush, off the commit path, where a 3–20 ms macOS flush would not stall writers ([80 §2.3.2]) — so no surviving slot combination can name a deleted file ([72 M2]); recovery that nevertheless finds a slot naming a missing file rebuilds the segment set from the durable `Checkpoint` records instead of failing.
The `retired` flag is set in an old store by `restore`'s swap (§4.10); every process checks it per operation and re-discovers the store ([72 m6]).

### 4.3 Log records and the commit body

```
RecHdr (32 B): len u32 | kind u8 | flags u8 (bit0 lazy, bit1 group_end) | _ u16 | lsn u64 | epoch u64 | xxh3_64 u64
```

A record is **valid** iff its length and kind are sane, its epoch equals `HEAD.epoch`, its `lsn` equals its position in the scan, and its xxh3 matches (the position check rejects a same-epoch record left from a recycled extent's previous life, [72 M1]). A **group** is valid iff its records are valid and the 8-byte `chain` trailer of its `group_end` record — XXH3-64 over the group's bytes, seeded with the chain value that ends the preceding group (read by position at `[start − 8, start)`; XXH3-64(epoch) for the first group after `init` or an epoch re-roll) — matches, so a group validates only behind the exact predecessor it was validated against; groups never span extents (a rotation pads the old extent with one `Noop` group), and a scan stops at the first invalid group ([80 §2.4.3], [81 B1]). Records are written in **flushed groups** — a commit with its `Lease`/`Marker` records, a `RefUpdate` with what accompanies it, a lazy record alone — and the last record of every group carries `group_end`; recovery adopts a group all or nothing and ends the log at the first record of an incomplete group, so a completion can never be adopted without its marker.

Kinds: `Commit`, `RefUpdate` (non-commit ref moves: `branch`, `tag`, `undo`, `op restore`, `branch -d`; fork and undo records carry the ref's absorbed vector), `ClientHead`, `Lease`, `Marker` (settled/deleted/cleared), `Idem`, `GitMap`, `Pin`, `Checkpoint` (segment set change + per-ref lsn lists for the window, G15, each entry with its `append_hlc`, [50] F9), `RefTable`, `Lazy`, `Noop`; and R4's runtime kinds ([40] R-7): durable `FsIntent`, `FsIntentDone`, `FsIntentAborted` (the crash-safe file-operation protocol, §5e.5) and lazy `FileObs`, `Pending`, `FPrint`, `JournalCursor` (was `UsnCursor`), `DirMap`, `TreeReg` (also a settle's epoch record `{tree key [16], scope_kind u8 (full-tree | lane-owned | partial), _ [3], scope_ref u32, scope digest [16], hlc u64}` and the tree's dirty count, [70 S5, S8]; the scope kind makes an epoch's coverage decidable, [40 §2.6], A1 re-review A1P-04), `PrefixEv`, `GitFacts`, `AnchorRes` (anchor results per (anchor uid, file `oid`, resolver version), [70 S7]); durable `Backup` (`{dir, committed_lsn, digest}`, written by `backup` after its files and directory are flushed, [72 M9]); lazy `SessionMark` (the rules a `SubagentStart` hook showed an agent, `{agent, rule ids, rev}`, [73 F4]). Replay indexes lazy runtime records by key → lsn and decodes them only on use ([71 RAM-M5]). An invalid record (above) ends the log for recovery purposes; §4.5 phase 2 says which invalid records are a lost lazy tail and which are corruption.

`Commit` body:

```
commit_id      [32]  BLAKE3-256 over the canonical form (§4.6)
n_parents u8, parents [{id16, lsn}; n]      // 24 B each; 2 for merges, 0 for root
gen u32                                      // 1 + max(parent.gen)
seq u64                                      // store-wide monotonic
ref u16, ref_id u32                          // branch this commit lands on (symbol + never-reused ref id)
ref_old id16                                 // the branch tip this commit replaces (N3): adoption of a complete record
                                             // implies the ref move ref_old → commit_id, CAS-checked against the ref table
prev_on_ref u64                              // lsn of the previous commit on the same ref (G15)
ref_seq u32                                  // position on the ref's chain; monotonic per ref, never reused after undo (CM1)
kind u8                                      // canonical kind (hashed): ordinary | merge | sync | revert | cherry-pick
import u8                                    // provenance flag (not hashed): 0 local | 1 import-native (verified) | 2 import-foreign | 3 import-checkpoint
hlc u64                                      // hybrid logical clock (ms << 16 | counter)
actor u32, role u16, session u32             // symbols (actor widened from u16, [71 RAM-m6]; role u16 as in `CREATOR`, §3.1)
git {algo u8, head [32], branch u16, worktree u16, base [32]}
foreign_git {algo u8, oid [32]}              // commits created by foreign or checkpoint import: the git object they came from (hashed)
idem_key [16], idem_payload [16]             // BLAKE3-128 of the key and of the payload (I14′); not hashed
sync_base id16                               // sync/merge only: the src commit absorbed (G17 merge-by-reference)
n_absorbed u16, absorbed [(ref_id u32, ref_seq u32)]   // merge/sync records only: dst's absorbed vector after this commit (CM1; ≤ 300 B at 50 refs)
verified u8                                  // import-native: canonical hash matched the trailer (N5)
stmt_origin u8, actor_src u8, stmt_sym u32, stmt_hash [16] // R5 and [90 §10.1], not hashed: stmt_origin = verb | named mutation | tx | MCP write | merge | import | file verb,
                                             // and which named mutation or TX produced the commit ([50] F10); actor_src = how the actor was resolved (§7.1)
append_hlc u64                               // R5, not hashed: this store's HLC at append; = hlc for local commits,
                                             // later for imports; monotonic in seq order ([50] F14, I43′)
msg_len u16, msg
affected_len u32, affected_complete u8, affected [u32]   // ids whose derived state changed (change feed); u32 and the
                                             // completeness flag per [50] F16 (I42′); not hashed
changeset_digest [32]                        // BLAKE3-256 of canonical item 10 (§4.6), so a re-parented candidate re-hashes in O(1)
cs_ref {file u32, len u64, blake3_16}        // bulk commits only: the sealed `cs.NNNN` holding the changeset ([71 RAM-B1])
n_ops u32, ops[]                             // inline changeset ≤ store.commit.inline-max-bytes (1 MiB); empty when cs_ref is set
```

**Commit size is bounded** ([71 RAM-B1]): an inline changeset is at most `store.commit.inline-max-bytes` (1 MiB), and every log record fits one extent (checked by the recovery scan). A larger changeset — a checkpoint-image import into a fresh store, `migrate` over a kind, `rm --cascade` of a campaign, a directory `file mv` with thousands of links, a long lane's merge — is a **bulk commit**: its ops are streamed into a sealed `cs.NNNN` segment in delta-segment layout (rows sorted by `#N`, list replacements, bitset ± lists; bodies straight to `blobs`), flushed (`durable+meta`), renamed from its temporary name by `rename_noreplace` and made durable with `durable-name` before the `Commit` record that names it is appended ([80 §2.3.2]); readers map it as one more delta layer instead of replaying it into a private overlay, and the next checkpoint folds it. The crash state "segment flushed, record not" leaves an unreferenced file that the orphan sweep removes. Agent-facing verbs (`TX`, `apply`) never create bulk commits: above their write budget they refuse with E501 and a split hint (§4.5).

Ops are tagged varints; every op that changes a value carries its before-image (SQLite-changeset style [04 §3.13]) and a `prev` delta to the node's previous op lsn on this ref, so per-node history is a linked chain: `Create{id, uid, kind, fields}`, `Delete{id, reason, replaced_by, before-image}`, `Undelete{id, before-image}`, `SetField{id, field, old, new}`, `SetStatus{id, old, new, resolution}`, `Incr{id, field, delta}`, `SetBody{id, old_hash, new_hash}`, `AddEdge`/`RemoveEdge{src, kind, dst, disc, props}` (`disc` = R4's anchor discriminator on `at` edges, whose props are the anchor record; an `AddEdge` that creates an anchor also carries its store-local `aN`, not hashed, from which recovery derives `next_anchor`, [40] R-6, A1 re-review A1P-12), `SetEdgeProps{src, kind, dst, disc, old, new}` (R4 anchor repins and pins, [40] R-4), `Move{id, old_parent, new_parent, old_order, new_order}`, `Schema{weaken|strengthen|query, payload}` (`query` = a project named-query definition, [50] F3), `Conflict{key, class, base, ours, theirs}`, `Violation{class, description, suggested}`, `Resolve{key, choice}`. Within one commit the writer coalesces ops per key (a field set twice keeps the first before-image and the last value; two `Incr` on one field sum; a `Create` followed by `SetField` folds into the `Create`), so the stored op list is the commit's *net* changeset and equals the canonical op list of §4.6 for every kind except `sync`, whose stored list is the residue of §5a.3; markers are derived from this net list, so `TX { REOPEN t; SET t.done = true }` on a done task emits none [72 M4]. A body travels in the log tail raw, with a codec byte, and is dictionary-compressed only when a checkpoint seals it into `blobs`, so no writing process builds a compression context (1–2 MB for zstd); if the M0 measurement shows the tail must hold compressed bodies, the chosen pure-Rust codec's smallest-window parameters with a by-reference dictionary keep that ≤ 0.5 MB [71 RAM-M6], [90 §11.3]. **Encoding and size** (frozen at M0 with the format). The commit header opens with a u32 presence bitmap; optional groups — `git`, `foreign_git`, the idempotency pair, `sync_base`, the absorbed vector, `verified`, `stmt_hash`, `cs_ref`, `msg` and `affected` — are omitted when empty; integers other than `hlc` are LEB128 varints (`append_hlc` as a varint delta from `hlc`, one byte for a local commit; absorbed entries as varint pairs, ≈ 6 B each, hence ≤ 300 B at 50 refs); ids, digests and `hlc` are fixed width. With every field present and fixed width, the header is ≈ 409 B (the 32 B `RecHdr` + 377 B with one parent, `actor_src` and a u16 `role` included, before `msg`, `affected` and ops). A typical local agent commit — one parent, git provenance from a worktree, an idempotency key, a named mutation, 2–4 affected ids — has a ≈ 310 B header (≈ 210 B outside a repository and without a key); with a short message and 1–3 small ops (≈ 40–150 B) a small commit is **≈ 0.35–0.5 KB** before compression (est.) — about 100× below the ~52 KB measured per DoltLite single-row commit [04 §3.2]. About 200 B of each header are ids and digests that do not compress, so `hist` frames compress ≈ 1.5–2.5× (est.).

### 4.4 Segment layout

```
SegHdr { magic "MSEG", format u16, seg_kind u8 (base|delta|branch|hist|blobs), n_rows u32, base_seq u64, upto_lsn u64, total_len u64,
         n_sections u16, sections: [{tag u16, flags u16 (bit0 derived-optional), off u64, len u64, xxh3 u64}], blake3 [32] }
```

`total_len` is the file's whole length, checked against `fstat` before the file is mapped (`gitmap` pages and dictionaries carry the same field, [80 §2.5]). A section marked **derived-optional** is rebuildable from the log and ignored by a reader that does not know its tag, so a later derived index (for example a lease-history index) is an additive change, not a format version bump; `repair --rebuild-from-log` builds it ([74 A23]). `seg_kind` gains `changeset` for the `cs.NNNN` segments of bulk commits (§4.3).

Sections of `seg.base` (rows dense by `#N`; dead rows keep their tombstone header):

| Section | Layout | Bytes / row (est.) |
|---|---|---|
| `NODE` | `[NodeHdr; n]` 60 B fixed | 60 |
| `UID` | sorted `[(u128, u32)]` (cold; touched by import/export/`--uid`) | 20 |
| `TOPO`, `DEFER`, `DUE` | u32 each | 12 (cold) |
| `TITLE_OFF` + `TITLE_BLOB` | u32 + varint-len UTF-8 (≤ 200 B) | 4 + ~60 |
| `FIELDS_OFF` + `FIELDS_BLOB` | u32 + tagged-varint blocks | 4 + ~24 |
| `BODY_REF` → `BLOBTAB` | u32 → `(blake3_16, file u32, off u64, len u32, raw_len u32)` | 4 + per unique body |
| `OUT_OFF`, `OUT_DST`, `OUT_KIND` | CSR `[u32; n+1]`, `[u32; e]`, `[u8; e]` sorted by (kind, dst) | 4 + 5/edge |
| `IN_OFF`, `IN_SRC`, `IN_KIND` | reverse CSR | 4 + 5/edge |
| `EDGE_PROPS` | sparse sorted `(edge idx u32, pinned_commit id16)`; R4's anchor records live in `ANCHORS` below | rare |
| `BM_*` | frozen bitsets: per kind, per (kind, status), `unblocked` (the structural predicate; `ready` adds its runtime clauses at read time, §3.5), `is_blocker`, `deleted`, `suspect`, `conflicted`, `container`, `has_dangling` (~80 sets) | ≤ 128 KiB per set at 1e6 |
| `SYMTAB` | sorted string table | small |
| `TOMB` | sorted `{id, tx, reason_sym, replaced_by}` 16 B | per deleted node |
| `IDEM` | open-addressing `(key16, payload16, branch_sym, result blob ref)`; 30-day retention | 40 B per key |
| `LEASES`, `MARKERS`, `MARKERS_OLD` | `LEASES` sorted by `(#N, lease id)` `{#N (0 for a role lease, which holds no task), lease id (L-<n>), kind (task \| role), role (symbol, u16), holder, anchor (32 B: kind none, session, intent, leader or session-ttl; os; slot hint; the 16 B primary session hash for session and session-ttl, or the nonce and a diagnostic hash prefix for intent; boot hash; [80] X-F2 as amended by [90 §4.4]), token, expires {wall u64, boot_hash u64, mono u64 — the boot clock, which includes suspend}, run, branch, bound (16 B hash of the thread a session role lease or an environment lease is bound to; zero = unbound), root session (16 B hash of a Codex holder's root session, for grouping; zero otherwise), files_owned globs (captured at `claim` and at any `set files_owned` under the lease), `ProcId` (diagnostics only)}` (§6.2, [72 B2], [70 S5]); `MARKERS` sorted `{#N, ref_id, commit id16, kind settled\|deleted\|cleared, ref_seq, hlc, seq}` holding only **active** markers; `MARKERS_OLD` the cold remainder — at every checkpoint fold, markers that are *globally inert* (cleared, or absorbed by every live ref: `absorbed_R[ref_id] ≥ ref_seq`, deleted refs excluded; O(live refs²) ≤ 2,500 lookups) move there, where `doctor`, triage and history read them and no scan does ([70 S4]); `gc` drops `MARKERS_OLD` rows older than `gc.reflog-expire` | ~160 / 40 B per live row (est.; live leases number in the hundreds) |
| `REFS`, `PINS`, `HEADS` | folded tables (§4.2) | small |
| `ANCESTRY` | `(sha32, sha32) → bool` lazy facts | rare |
| `TERMS`, `POST` | tier-2 FTS (≥ 20k nodes, a store parameter; built per delta segment, merged at rollup); with R5's `DOCLEN` cold column (u16 tokens per field), per-segment document statistics and a tokenizer version byte ([50] F12) | ~135 B/node + 6 B/node |
| R4 link sections ([40] R-8, R-18) | versioned indexes `PATHIDX` (sorted (root, fold(path), path) → `#N` of present or planned file nodes), `ALIASIDX`, `ANCHORS` (sorted (src#, dst#, anchor#) → anchor record), `ANCHOR_UID`; runtime `FILEOBS` (per (file, tree): the tagged `OsFileId` with the parent-directory id (on Linux the inode plus a file-handle digest that carries the generation, [80 §2.11.2]), size, ns-precision mtime, ctime, creation and added time, attributes, `last_oid`, `verified_at`, state, ≤ 3 proposals), `PENDING`, `FSINTENT`, `FPRINT` (`oid` → fingerprint blob), `JOURNALCUR` (reserved; E2 is not built, §9), `DIRMAP` (derived-optional; the Linux changed-directory frontier, [80 §2.11.3]), `TREES` (per tree, plus the settle **epoch** list — `{scope_kind, scope_ref, scope digest, hlc}`, 32 B each, only the newest per (scope_kind, scope_ref) kept, [40 §2.6], A1 re-review A1P-04 — and the **dirty** row `{count, HEAD, hlc}` written only by settles, `links sync`, git hook blocks and the edit-evidence hook — no read ever scans a worktree, [70 S5, S8]), `PREFIXEV`, `GITRENAMES` (beside `ANCESTRY`), `ANCHORRES` (runtime, (anchor uid, file `oid`, resolver version) → state, span, score, ≈ 32 B, [70 S7]; rows only for the current or last-observed content of a live file node, A1P-16); versioned `GLOBIDX` (path globs of `applies_to`, `files_owned` and `path_globs` by literal prefix, so pack class C7 is a range probe, [70 S17]) | ~55 B per path + 16 B per anchor (indexes); 96–150 B per (file, tree) |
| R5 sections ([50] F4–F7, F11, F17) | `CREATOR` cold column (F4); section-tag ranges `FCOL.<field>` (dense typed column + absent bitmap) and `FIDX.<field>` (value → frozen bitset) for promoted fields (F5); `STATS` — per edge kind and direction: edges, max degree, log2 degree histogram; per (kind, field): presence and distinct estimate (F7); `CONFLICTS` — sorted unresolved conflict values (F11); `ALLOC` — dense `#N → (uid, ref_id, create_seq)` and `UIDX` — sorted `uid → #N` over every uid the store has ever allocated on any branch, both store-level runtime folded at checkpoints and rebuilt from the log by `repair` (F17, widened by [72 M7]) | 6 B + 24 B per node (cold) + ~1–5 B per promoted field; `STATS` ~4–8 KB |

A **frozen bitset** is hand-written: per 65,536-id chunk either a sorted u16 array (≤ 4,096 members) or an 8 KiB bitmap, with a 16-byte chunk index that also carries each chunk's cardinality (and the segment header each bitset's total, [50] F6, so the query planner gets exact counts in O(chunks)), queried in place from the mapping. Delta segments hold only touched rows (sorted `IDS` array), the complete forward/reverse lists of touched nodes (list-replacement semantics), bitset ± lists, new bodies/symbols/tombstones/leases/markers. A reader resolves a node by probing overlay → deltas newest-first → base. Hot bytes per node: 60 + 8 + 30 (3 edges × 2 directions × 5 B) + ~1 ≈ **99 B** [20 §1.1].

### 4.5 Write path (one command, one commit, three phases)

Every write verb — plain writes, `claim`, `complete`, `apply`, `TX`, `rm`, `merge`, `sync`, `revert`, `cherry-pick`, imports, settles — runs in three phases, so the writer byte is held only to scan, re-validate and append, and briefly to publish; the flush happens outside it (§2.8, [70 S2]). This generalises `TX`'s `DRY` + `IF TIP`; no leader is needed.

**Phase 1 — compute, before the lock.**

1. Open: `pread` both `HEAD` slots and take the valid one with the higher `slot_seq`; if its `boot_id` differs from the current boot, run phase 2's recovery first (§4.2). Map the segment set; replay `(replayed_lsn, committed_lsn]` into the process's **compact overlay** — a sorted `#N → newest record lsn` index (12–16 B per op) plus the derived-state ± lists, values decoded on probe from cached log pages, ≤ 60 B per op; lazy runtime records are indexed by key, not decoded ([71 RAM-M5]). Call the replayed bound L0.
2. Idempotency pre-check against L0 (a hit returns the stored result at once; the binding check is repeated in phase 2).
3. Resolve the client's branch (§5a.4), binding and git provenance; parse, bind and plan (`TX`); build the branch overlay on first use (§5a.3).
4. Compute the **candidate** against the snapshot at L0 in a copy-on-write layer over the overlay (only touched keys are copied, [71 RAM-M6]): validation (schema, branch kind write mask of `plan/*`, CAS guards `--if-rev`/`--if-status`/`--if-holder`/`--if-tip`, lease token (I17′), role write policy, status machine, restrict policies, Pearce–Kelly for every added precedence edge incl. implied ones (I5′), forest depth, `gates`, markers for `claim`, and the store's commit limit: a commit whose `seq` would exceed 2^32 − 1 is refused with exit 7 and nothing is written, because `created_tx`/`updated_tx` are u32 (§3.1; checked again under the writer byte before step 9 appends the commit); the M0 error table assigns its code and the reference model carries the rule, [60 §1.2] P5); ops with placeholder `#N`s; forward and reverse adjacency, counters, bitsets, the budgeted `suspect` closure, `mentions` re-parse; `affected` (complete, or `affected_complete = 0`, I42′); **markers from the net ops (CB3, [72 M4])** — every net `SetStatus{→ done|cancelled}` on a task yields `Marker{settled, #N, ref_id, ref_seq, commit}`, every `Delete` a `Marker{deleted}`, every net `SetStatus{done|cancelled → open}` and every `Undelete` a `Marker{cleared}` scoped to `(#N, ref_id)`, whatever verb, batch, merge, sync, cherry-pick, revert or import produced the op; `claim`/`release` a `Lease` record; for merges and syncs the whole of §5a.7 steps 1–6 and the dst ref's new absorbed vector; for `TX` the `MATCH` targets and `EXPECT`; the canonical form and `changeset_digest`. The working set of this phase is charged to the **write budget `wmem`** — min(1 MiB, RSS-gate headroom), never below 256 KiB, agent maximum 4 MiB — covering the candidate layer, the serialized record and the canonical sort; explicit bulk verbs above it switch to the bulk commit of §4.3, while `TX` and `apply` refuse with E501 naming the split ([71 RAM-M6]). File-system work of a settle — including the ≥ 50 ms quiescence wait — happens here and never under the lock (§5e.3, [72 M11]).

**Phase 2 — commit: append under the writer byte, then group commit through the flush byte** ([80 §2.4.3]; the same on every OS).

5. Acquire the writer byte: `LockBytes::acquire_within(lock.writer-wait-ms = 2 s)` ([80 §2.2]; an overlapped `LockFileEx` on Windows, a waiter thread in `F_OFD_SETLKW` on Linux and macOS; a second client of the same process gets `Busy` or waits in process, before any kernel call). On timeout, exit 7 naming the holder from the writer diagnostics at `LOCK` offset 2048 (G1).
6. `pread` the newest valid `HEAD` slot; if the boot changed, run boot-change recovery first (§4.2). Then **scan and validate** from L0 — the bound up to which this process validated the log, with the chain value there — to the end of the log, by the group-chain rule of §4.3:
   - groups up to the published `committed_lsn` are applied to the process's overlay; **pending groups** beyond it — complete groups appended by writers whose covering flush has not yet been published, or whose writer died — are replayed into a scratch layer that is discarded when the writer byte is released, so no read overlay ever passes `committed_lsn`. Replay applies each record by kind: a `Commit` with its implied ref move CAS-checked (on CAS failure it is parked on `orphans/<ref>`, I27′), a `RefUpdate`, `Lease`, `ClientHead`, `Pin`, `GitMap`, `Checkpoint`, `FsIntent*` or `Backup` record to its table; the `MARKERS` fold is recomputed from the commits' net ops ([72 M1]). The counters `next_id`, `next_anchor`, `fence` and `commit_seq` advance from the scanned groups exactly as recovery derives them;
   - if the chain value at L0 no longer matches the log, the process drops its overlay and replays from its segment set; an invalid group in `(durable_lsn, end]` is the end of the log — a lost lazy tail, or an unflushed group lost after an OS crash or another process's failed flush — and is overwritten; an invalid record below `durable_lsn` is corruption: exit 7 and `moirai repair` ([60 §2.5] decision (b), scoped so it never fires on a benign lazy tail, [72 B1]);
   - **only then** is the idempotency key evaluated (X2/F-B7 order), against the scanned log including pending groups; a hit on a pending group is returned only after that group passes step 10's identity check.
7. If the scan found nothing beyond L0 — no newly published and no pending group — the candidate stands (`committed_lsn` = L0 is not enough: pending groups lie beyond it, [81] M7). Otherwise replay `(L0, L1]`, L1 the end of the scanned log (normally a few records), and **re-validate by key**: if no node, edge, marker or lease the candidate read or wrote changed, the ref's tip is unchanged or the commit is a plain write whose parent may move (G20), and no commit in the window touched a kind, field or edge kind a `TX` `MATCH` reads, the candidate is re-parented in O(1) (new `ref_old`, `prev_on_ref`, `ref_seq`, `seq`, `hlc` and header hash over the unchanged `changeset_digest`); else it is recomputed once under the lock within `tx.max-work-in-lock` (a store parameter calibrated at M7 so that cap × ns per unit ≤ the hold p99 budget − the plain append cost; 5e5 units provisional, [50 §3.10] item 10, A1 re-review A1P-13), or the byte is released and phase 1 re-run (at most twice) before exit 4 with the current values. `EXPECT` therefore always holds against the tip it lands on.
8. Allocate `#N`s from `next_id` — a derived uid already in `UIDX` reuses its `#N` (I1, [72 M7]) — and record them in `ALLOC`; final idempotency check.
9. Append the group (the `Commit` or its bulk-commit record, then its `Lease`/`Marker` records, `group_end` with its chain trailer on the last) in one write at the end of the valid log; remember its end and chain value. A lazy group is published at once (`committed_lsn` = its end) when no durable group is pending; otherwise the flush that covers it publishes it. Release the writer byte.
10. **Group commit** ([80 §2.4.3]), for a durable group and for a lazy group appended behind a pending durable one (best-effort evidence records skip it and become visible with the next covering flush):
    1. `pread` `HEAD`; if a published flush already covers the group, go to 4.
    2. Take the **flush byte** (`lock.flush-wait-ms`, 2 s; on timeout exit 7 with outcome `pending` — the group is appended, and a retry with the same key completes it), then the writer byte (the lock order); if the group is covered meanwhile, release both and go to 4.
    3. Scan `(durable_lsn, E]`, E the end of the last complete valid group. If the own group is not in it with its own chain value, release both and go to 5. Otherwise **re-write** every byte of `(durable_lsn, E]` from the scan buffer under the writer byte, release the writer byte, flush once (`durable`: `NtFlushBuffersFileEx(DATA_SYNC_ONLY)`, `fdatasync` or `F_FULLFSYNC`) every extent holding bytes of the range, re-take the writer byte and **publish**: a read-modify-write of the newest valid slot into the other slot (no flush) with `durable_lsn` = max(slot, E), `committed_lsn` = the end of the valid log stopping before the first uncovered pending durable group, and the `HEAD` effects of every newly covered group folded in log order (counters to the maximum; `refs_lsn`, `pins_lsn`, `heads_lsn`, `markers_lsn` to the newest covered record; a covered `Checkpoint`'s segment set; a covered `GitMap`'s `image_cursor`; `seq_ring`) — no field decreases except `committed_lsn` after a lost lazy tail. Release the writer byte, then the flush byte. A flush failure aborts the process; the next holder re-writes and flushes.
    4. **Identity check:** `pread` the group's 8-byte chain trailer; if it equals the remembered value, acknowledge — the group is durable behind exactly the prefix it was validated against.
    5. Otherwise the group was lost before it became durable (its pages reverted, were invalidated or were evicted after another process's failed flush): it is not acknowledged; phases 1–2 re-run with the same idempotency key (at most twice), then exit 7 `outcome unknown: retry with the same key`.

    The writer byte is held for the scan, re-validation and the append — tens of µs for a plain write — and by the flush holder for its page-cache re-write and the publish; one flush covers every writer that appended before it, so a 16-writer burst costs ≈ 2–3 flushes.

**Phase 3 — after release.**

11. Print `{commit_id, seq, rev_seq per touched node, affected, newly_ready}` (and, for `complete`/`apply`, the pack-staleness notice of §7.4).
12. Maintenance, if a threshold is crossed and quiet mode is off: a **delta checkpoint** when the tail exceeds `store.checkpoint.ops` (4,096) / `.bytes` (4 MiB of records, bodies on the separate 32 MiB threshold) / `store.tail.max-overlay-bytes` (1 MiB of compact overlay), or a **runtime-only fold** into `FILEOBS`/`FPRINT`/`GITRENAMES`/`ANCHORRES` when lazy runtime records exceed `store.tail.runtime-bytes` (2 MiB) ([71 RAM-M5]); a **promotion** when a ref's counters cross their thresholds (§5a.3). Maintenance takes the maintenance byte, writes a segment under its final number, makes it durable (`durable+meta`, `durable-name`, §4.10) and sets it read-only, then appends the `Checkpoint` record (with per-ref lsn lists) under the writer byte for microseconds and makes it durable by group commit (step 10); the new segment set becomes visible with the publish that covers the `Checkpoint`, and readers keep the old set until then. The resident MCP server takes maintenance at 1× a threshold, as resumable ≤ 5 ms slices between requests; a CLI or hook process only above `maintenance.cli-threshold-multiplier` (2×) — except a sync that crosses the promotion threshold, which promotes in the syncing process — so agents' calls rarely carry it ([70 S9, S15]). A rollup is never run here: when deltas exceed `maintenance.rollup-threshold` (25 % of the base), the process spawns the detached `moirai gc --rollup --if-needed` child of §4.9 and returns. In quiet mode nothing runs until 8× the threshold or 2 MiB of compact overlay, then one bounded delta runs anyway (G10).

**Flush grouping** ([70 S13]): one flushed group per verb — `lane open` writes its `RefUpdate`, binding and lane-node commit as one group (one covering flush), a sync-first merge writes its sync and merge commits as one group; `file mv`/`file rm` flush twice by protocol (intent, then commit, §5e.5); the GC barrier's one `HEAD` flush is off the commit path and outside the writer byte. Engine cost excluding the flush: tens of microseconds for a small commit (est.). `apply` computes a whole batch in phase 1 and commits it with one flush.

### 4.6 Canonical form of a commit

`commit_id` = BLAKE3-256 over a length-prefixed canonical byte encoding (fixed in the M0 format specification, which freezes every on-disk layout of §4 at M0 exit, [60 §2.5]) of exactly these fields, in this order — item 10 entering the encoding as its own BLAKE3-256 `changeset_digest`, so a candidate re-parented under the lock (§4.5 step 7) re-hashes in O(1) and a bulk changeset is hashed while it streams ([70 S2], [71 RAM-B1]):

1. `kind` ∈ {ordinary, merge, sync, revert, cherry-pick} — the local `import` flag is *not* part of it: a verified native import keeps the original kind and id;
2. the parents' **stated** ids — one for ordinary/revert/cherry-pick, two for merge (dst tip, src tip) and sync (lane tip, `sync_base`);
3. `hlc` (u64);
4. actor, role, session as strings (empty allowed);
5. git provenance as strings: algorithm, head, branch, worktree, base (each possibly empty);
6. the message bytes, normalised at write time (LF, no trailing whitespace on any line, no trailing newlines; a message whose last paragraph begins with `Moirai-` is refused so the image's trailer block is always separable);
7. the schema version;
8. the origin commit id for revert/cherry-pick (empty otherwise);
9. `foreign_git` (algorithm, oid) for commits created by foreign or checkpoint import (empty otherwise);
10. the **net changeset**: one entry per key, sorted by (uid, key class, field or edge-kind name, dst uid, discriminator), every node named by `uid` — fields `(uid, field) → new value | absent`; status `(uid, status) → new status + resolution`; counters `(uid, field) → net delta`; body `(uid, body) → BLAKE3-128 of the new bytes | absent`; edges `(uid, kind, dst uid, disc, props) → present | absent`, where `disc` is R4's 128-bit anchor discriminator on `at` edges (empty for every other kind) and an `at` edge's `props` is its anchor selector block ([40] R-10; no new key class) in which quote, prefix, suffix and a range's `end` enter **only as BLAKE3-128 digests** (`quote_h`, `prefix_h`, `suffix_h`, `end_h`), so an image destination that omits the text (`image.dest.<name>.anchor-text = hash-only`) changes no moirai commit id ([72 M6]; `end_h` by the A1 re-review, S-04); the root of every `path` and `pathmove` value is encoded by name, never by its store-local symbol ([40] R-1, S-19); existence `(uid) → created(kind) | deleted(reason, replaced_by uid) | undeleted`; hierarchy `(uid) → (parent uid, order)`; schema items, including project named-query definitions in their portable form ([50] F3); conflict values `(key) → {class, base, ours, theirs}`.

There is no item 11: R4's link provenance (`relink`) and directory-move history (the root node's `path_moves`) are ordinary fields inside item 10, and R5 adds only schema items, so neither design adds a canonical item, a trailer or a commit annotation (§5e.6; Review log, integration entry).

**Not hashed**: `#N`, `lsn`, `seq`, `ref`/`ref_id`/`ref_old`/`prev_on_ref`/`ref_seq`, symbol numbers, offsets, the store id, `idem_key`/`idem_payload`, `import`/`verified`, absorbed vectors, `affected` and `affected_complete`, `stmt_origin`/`stmt_sym`/`stmt_hash` and `append_hlc` (R5), `actor_src` ([90 §10.1]), before-images (derivable from the parent state), `Violation` ops (which exist only on staging refs), the `ALLOC` index, a named query's canonical-AST hash (derived, recomputed on import), every R4 resolution and evidence table (file ids, volume serials, stat caches, fingerprints, proposals, intents, `ANCHORRES`; I-F4), and anchor quote, prefix, suffix and `end` text (their digests are hashed; the text is a verified cache of the preimage, absent after a hash-only import).

**Net changeset = state diff.** For every kind the canonical op list equals the typed diff between the state at the first parent and the state at the commit; the writer's per-key coalescing (§4.3) makes the stored list identical to it for ordinary, merge, revert and cherry-pick commits. A `sync` commit stores only the residue of §5a.3 (every key whose merged value differs from what `main`'s window alone yields: keys both sides touched, one-sided compositions and re-keys, [72 M5]), but its canonical op list is the **full** state diff against the lane parent — `main`'s window materialised plus the residue — computed at sync time from the same fold the sync already performs (O(window ops) of hashing, no extra I/O, no extra storage); the sync asserts that window ⊕ residue equals that diff, and `doctor --verify` re-checks it. Inside one uid, the order of key classes follows the `.moi` line order of §5b.2, so an importer whose tree order already equals uid order hashes a checkpoint node by node with O(1) memory; every other bulk producer sorts externally with ≤ 1 MiB runs in `<store>/tmp/` ([71 RAM-B1]). This one rule lets the importer reconstruct every kind the same way: diff the tree against the first parent's tree (§5b.6, CM2). Two stores that import the same image compute the same ids (I28′, I38′); this is what makes the git trailer `Moirai-Commit` verifiable, and it is checked for every commit kind before any round-trip corpus runs (§5b.7 gate 0).

**Reserved in format v1 for R4 and R5.** The on-disk format is frozen at M0 exit ([60 §2.5]) with every field the two designs need, before the first byte is written: R4's reservations R-1…R-18 ([40 §2.11], revision 2) and R5's F1–F18 ([50 §8.1], revision 2). Those tables are normative for byte layouts; this is where each lands.

| Area | R4 ([40] R-n) | R5 ([50] Fn) |
|---|---|---|
| Value types (§3.1) | `path`, `oid`, `pathmove` (R-1) | — |
| Schema as data (§2.12) | the `artifact` field set incl. `origin_path`, `origin_pred`, `observed_blob`; statuses `planned`, `removed`; merge classes `observation` (composite) and `identity`; `area` fields `root`, `path_moves` (R-2, R-5); `uid_derivation ∈ {random, file-key, root-key, anchor-key}` with the length-prefixed derivations, the predecessor order by greatest candidate uid on the registering view, the view-scoped dead-uid re-derivation and the edge-complete merge re-key rule (R-3; A1 re-review S-01) | edge rows `lq_name`, `src_kinds`, `dst_kinds`, `symmetric`, `reverse_names`, `reading` (F1); field rows `optional`, `default`, `index`, `sort_rank`, `coerce` (F2); the `QUERIES` item in portable form and `Schema{weaken, query}` (F3); violation classes `QueryInvalid`, `QueryCycle` (F18) |
| Edges and ops (§3.3, §4.3) | edge kind `at` (historical); the 128-bit discriminator in the edge key; the anchor record incl. its capture digest (with the widened prefix, suffix and `lines` window) and its predecessor term `pred`; the unhashed `aN` of the creating op; op `SetEdgeProps` (R-4; A1 re-review S-03, A1P-12) | — |
| `HEAD` (§4.2) | `next_anchor u32` (R-6) | — |
| Log records (§4.3) | durable `FsIntent`, `FsIntentDone`, `FsIntentAborted`; lazy `FileObs`, `Pending`, `FPrint`, `JournalCursor`, `DirMap`, `TreeReg` (incl. settle epochs and the dirty row), `PrefixEv`, `GitFacts`, `AnchorRes` (R-7, extended by [70 S5, S7, S8]) | unhashed commit-header fields `stmt_origin`/`stmt_sym`/`stmt_hash` (F10), `append_hlc` (F14), `affected_len u32` + `affected_complete` (F16); `append_hlc` per entry of `Checkpoint` per-ref lists (F9) |
| Segments (§4.1, §4.4) | `PATHIDX`, `ALIASIDX`, `ANCHORS`, `ANCHOR_UID`, `FILEOBS` (row layout R-18), `PENDING`, `FSINTENT`, `FPRINT`, `JOURNALCUR`, `DIRMAP`, `TREES` (with epochs and the dirty row), `PREFIXEV`, `GITRENAMES`, `ANCHORRES`, `GLOBIDX` (R-8, extended by [70 S5, S7, S8, S17]); the fingerprint blob class in `blobs.NNNN` (R-9) | `CREATOR` (F4), `FCOL`/`FIDX` tag ranges (F5), per-chunk bitset cardinalities (F6), `STATS` (F7), `hist` frame seq/HLC bounds (F8), `CONFLICTS` (F11), `DOCLEN` and the tokenizer byte (F12), `ALLOC` (F17); F13 is R4's R-8 |
| Canonical form (this section) | the anchor selector block as the props of an item-10 edge entry keyed with the discriminator, anchor text entering only as digests (R-10, [72 M6]); no item 11, no trailer, no commit annotation | named-query definitions as schema items of item 10; their canonical-AST hash unhashed and derived |
| Image (§5b) | `.moi` `anchor` lines with digests on every line and text in `full` mode, artifact and root-node fields, `pathmove` blocks, the hash-only anchor form (R-11) | `schema/queries/<q>.moi`, q = 32 hex digits of BLAKE3-256(name) (F3, [80] X-F9) |
| Invariants, config, constants, strings | I-F1…I-F14 (R-12); config keys `roots.*`, `files.*`, `image.dest.<name>.anchor-text` (R-13); the resolver-version constant table — thresholds, the `is_text` rule, the case fold, the window hash, the never-candidate patterns, the 50 ms quiescence, the git-window bounds (R-14); the binding-row extension (R-15); frozen state and header strings (R-16); the `relink` provenance vocabulary (R-17) | `affected` completeness (F15, I42′); the persisted bitset is the structural `unblocked` predicate and `ready` adds runtime clauses at read time (§3.5); the grammar v1, error table, output envelope and standard-library signatures frozen by LQ-Bench at M0 (§7.7) |

**Reserved in format v1 by the priority audits** ([70]–[74], 2026-09-26; each is frozen at M0 exit with the rows above and carried in [60 §2.5]): `HEAD` fields `durable_lsn`, `boot_id`, `config_gen` and flag `retired` (§4.2); `LOCK` session slots and slot table (§4.1; laid out anew by [80] X-F1); `RecHdr` flag `group_end` and the position check (§4.3); commit header `actor u32`, `changeset_digest`, `cs_ref`, and the `cs.NNNN` changeset segment kind with `store.commit.inline-max-bytes` (§4.3); byte-bounded `hist` frames (§4.1); the `derived-optional` section flag (§4.4); ref-table counters `overlay_ops`/`overlay_bytes` (§4.2); the `MARKERS`/`MARKERS_OLD` split and the marker key `(#N, ref_id, commit)` (§4.4); the `LEASES` holder anchor, deadline form and captured `files_owned` globs (§4.4, §6.2); `ALLOC` with uid and `UIDX` (§4.4); `ANCHORRES`, `GLOBIDX`, the `TREES` epoch list and dirty row, record kinds `AnchorRes`, `Backup`, `SessionMark` (§4.3–§4.4); anchor-text digests in the canonical form and on every `anchor` line (above, §5b.2); the per-uid key order of item 10 (above); the `lane` schema without a versioned dirty count (§3.2); the `sync_dir` operation of the `Vfs` and `ProjectFs` traits and the namespace rules of fault-model item (2) (§4.10); the `config` syntax, precedence and unknown-key rule — not the key set (§13); and R-14's E3d identity rule (§5e.3).

**Reserved in format v1 for the ports** (owner decision #32; [80 §3], carried in [60 §2.5]): `LOCK` v1 with lock bytes beyond EOF, the flush byte and 256 slot records of 128 B (X-F1); `ProcId`, the 32-byte anchor with kinds and nonce, the intent anchor of `FsIntent`, the boot-clock lease deadline, the boot-identity rule and Unknown-boot mode (X-F2); the group-commit protocol with the `group_end` chain trailer and chained group validity (X-F3) and the lock contract and order (X-F4); the durability class of each protocol point and fault-model items (2), (3), (5), (7)–(12) (X-F5); the mapping policy with `total_len` in sealed-file headers and the crash-gated environment guard (X-F6); the path canonical form P1–P10 and P12 (the machine-local `abs` form) with `fold_v1` and the per-component canonical root (X-F7); `OsFileId` (with the Linux file-handle digest), ns timestamps, `JOURNALCUR`/`JournalCursor`, `DIRMAP`/`DirMap`, the `TREES` additions and the per-OS R-14 rules (X-F8); hashed named-query file names and the ref-name rule (X-F9); numeric store file names (X-F10); the per-OS user-scope config locations (X-F11; `lock.flush-wait-ms`, `files.portable-names`, `hooks.session-start.path-export` and the `image.dest.<name>.path` default are registered in §13 at M0, not frozen — §13 freezes the syntax, not the key set); the shell transport rules T1–T10 (X-F12).

**Reserved in format v1 for the harness-agnostic interface** (owner decisions #43, #44; [90 §10.1], carried in [60 §2.5]): `LEASES` fields `kind` (task | role), `role`, `run`, `anchor` (session | session-ttl | none) and `bound` (the thread a session role lease or an environment lease is bound to), and the holder's root session for grouping (§6.2); the X-F2 amendment — the anchor hashes the namespaced process-lifetime identity (Claude Code: the session; Codex: the thread) with BLAKE3-128, held as 16 B in the anchor (kinds `session` and `session-ttl`) and in the slot record's primary and alias hashes ([80] X-F1, X-F2), slots are taken lazily, and a thread whose own server holds no slot gets anchor `none`; the unhashed commit-header byte `actor_src` beside `stmt_origin`; the output contract's byte units, both-ends rule, ASCII rule and `--ids` page rule (§7.1); the unknown-model write refusal code and the two exit-5 refusal texts (a declared agent that differs from the lease holder; an environment lease bound to another thread); the card's display spelling (§7.7.5); and the codec decided by §8.2 item 6 among pure-Rust options — the codec byte's values, the `hist` and `blobs` frame formats, and `dict.D` as a raw-content dictionary or absent, or, if M0 chooses option (3) of [90 §11.3] (an own zstd-format encoder decoded by `ruzstd`), a formatted zstd dictionary.

### 4.7 Read path and open path

Read: `pread` `HEAD` (10–170 µs; a `boot_id` different from the current boot sends the process through recovery once, §4.2) → map the listed segments if not already mapped, after checking each file's size against the `total_len` in its header ([80 §2.5]) (0.22 ms each [M], cached per process while the set is unchanged; a delete-pending miss means "re-read `HEAD` and retry", bounded) → replay `(replayed_lsn, committed_lsn]` into the compact overlay (µs per op; never beyond `committed_lsn`; a record that is invalid in `(durable_lsn, committed_lsn]` is the end of the visible log, never an error, [72 B1]; a reader that observes a new `slot_seq` checks the chain value at its replay bound and rebuilds its overlay if it no longer matches, [80 §2.4.3]) → resolve the branch and, on first use in this process, build its overlay (§5a.3) → serve from mapped columns + overlay; strings are borrowed from the map; each candidate body of a `pack` or `brief` decompresses into one reused 64 KiB buffer and is rendered or degraded to L1/L0 at once, so bodies never accumulate ([71 RAM-m7]); no node structs are materialised; readers take no locks. `SYMTAB` is probed in place (binary search over the map plus the tail's new symbols), never loaded into a per-process map ([71 RAM-m6]).

Open = floor (2 × 4 KiB `HEAD` reads + ≤ 8 maps at 0.22 ms each) + discovery and provenance (walk-up probes, `.git`/`commondir`, git `HEAD` and its loose ref; `packed-refs` only when the loose ref is absent) + one `pread` of the folded tables + tail replay ≤ `store.checkpoint.ops` ops: ≤ 1 / 1.5 / 3 ms warm at 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6 for the store part with a full tail at the M0 threshold (the threshold is chosen so this holds, [60 §5.2] item 10), plus ≤ 0.5 ms of discovery and provenance through the binary; the file opens per command are counted by the `Vfs` (≤ 10 on a `main` read, ≤ 14 on a lane, ≤ 18 with the tree gate, [70 S10]). No step is O(history) or O(nodes).

### 4.8 Indexes

| Index | Structure | Where |
|---|---|---|
| id → row | direct (dense `#N`) in base; sorted `IDS` in deltas; hash map in overlay | segments / process |
| uid → id | sorted `UID` column per view (import/export); store-wide `UIDX` over every uid ever allocated on any branch, probed by the write path and by import's `IdCollision` ([72 M7]) | segments + tail |
| adjacency fwd/rev | CSR + overlay ± vectors | segments + process |
| kind/status/unblocked/blocker/deleted/suspect/conflicted | frozen bitsets (with per-chunk cardinalities, F6) + overlay ± lists | segments + process |
| promoted field values; statistics | `FCOL`/`FIDX` (F5); `STATS` (F7) | segments |
| file path → file node; former paths | `PATHIDX`, `ALIASIDX` (R4) | segments |
| file node → anchors → referrers | `ANCHORS` + reverse `at` adjacency (R4; packs' C7 and `notes --path`) | segments |
| path glob → knowledge nodes | `GLOBIDX` by literal prefix (packs' C7, `notes --path`) | segments |
| anchor results | `ANCHORRES` (anchor uid, file `oid`, resolver version) | segments + tail |
| `#N` → allocating ref and commit | `ALLOC` (F17; notice N06 for ids created on another branch) | segments (cold) |
| precedence order | `TOPO` | segments / overlay |
| commit id → lsn | per-frame fan-out index in `hist`; for the active extent, the overlay's `(id16 → lsn)` of replayed records | history / process |
| per-ref commit chain | `prev_on_ref` links + per-ref lsn lists in `Checkpoint` records | log / hist |
| per-node op chain | `NODE.last_op_lsn` → `prev` links | log / hist |
| idempotency, leases, markers, refs, pins, heads | folded sections + tail records | segments + process |
| text | tier 1 scan; tier 2 `TERMS`/`POST` per delta segment | segments |
| git ancestry cache | `ANCESTRY` lazy facts; R4's `GITFACTS`/`GITRENAMES` (per-commit exact renames) | segments |
| git id map | `gitmap.NNNN` + `GitMap` records | own files |

### 4.9 Checkpoint, rollup, GC

- **Delta checkpoint**: automatic (outside the writer byte, §4.5 step 12): fold the overlay into `seg.dK` and seal the tail's bodies into one `blobs.NNNN` (compressing them here, §4.3); tiered: when d3 exists, fold d1..d3 into a new d1 — always a new file number, never a delete-pending name — and their blob files into one (CL3). 5–30 / 10–50 / 20–100 ms at 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6 (∝ tail, plus the column and bitset pages the fold rewrites, which grow with N; gated at ≤ 30 / 50 / 100 ms, §8.3), ≤ 8 MB private. At the same fold, globally inert markers move from `MARKERS` to `MARKERS_OLD` (§4.4) and every branch whose promotion counters crossed a threshold is promoted (§5a.3). In the MCP server a checkpoint runs as resumable ≤ 5 ms slices between requests.
- **Rollup** (new base, rebuilt bitsets, `TOPO`, dictionary retrain, tier-2 terms): **never inside the MCP server or a CLI/hook process** ([71 RAM-M3], [70 S9], [74 A09]). It runs only in a `moirai gc` process: explicitly, or — when deltas exceed `maintenance.rollup-threshold` (25 % of the base), quiet mode is off and `maintenance.rollup = auto` — as a detached `moirai gc --rollup --if-needed` child that a process spawns after its request (the MCP server) or after its write (the CLI, §4.5 step 12) at `BELOW_NORMAL_PRIORITY_CLASS`, `MEMORY_PRIORITY_LOW` and background I/O (on Linux `nice` 10 and the idle I/O class, on macOS the Darwin background policy; never from a CLI in a foreign PID namespace, [80 §2.6, §2.12]); the child takes the maintenance byte (a second spawn exits at once), streams the rollup and exits. The merge-into-`main` ritual runs `moirai gc --rollup --if-needed` explicitly. With `maintenance.rollup = explicit`, `brief` and `doctor` warn above the threshold.
