The rollup is **streaming**: CSR and columns merged row by row from base and deltas, bitsets rebuilt per 65,536-id chunk, `TOPO` renumbered from the Pearce–Kelly positions by one sort of (position, `#N`) pairs (Kahn stays the `doctor` check), the dictionary retrained on a capped sample (`store.dict.train-sample-bytes`, 4 MiB); ≤ 24 / 24 / 32 MB private at 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6, 10–30 ms / 0.1–0.3 s / 1–3 s [05 §7]; refused in quiet mode. After a rollup the child promotes every live branch still pinned to the superseded base, so at most two bases are resident in the page cache ([71 RAM-M1]).
- **History retirement**: a full `log.NNNN` is compressed into `hist.NNNN` (compressed frames of ≤ 256 commits and ≤ 1 MiB raw + commit index; a bulk commit keeps its `cs.NNNN` reference); nothing is dropped by default.
- **Pins**: every merge into `main` and every `tag --pin` pins the newest sealed checkpoint set (refcount per file); a branch fork pins the newest set with `upto_seq ≤ fork_seq`; a promotion moves `base_pin` to the newest sealed set.
- **GC** (`moirai gc`, explicit): reachability = refs ∪ reflog entries younger than `gc.reflog-expire` (90 d) ∪ pins; drop segment files with refcount 0 after 60 s delete-pending grace **and after the two-slot `HEAD` barrier of §4.2**; rewrite `hist` frames dropping unreachable commits older than `gc.cruft-delay` (14 d), keeping commit headers unless `--prune-headers`; blob GC for bodies unreferenced by any live row, retained history or ref; `gitmap` compaction; `MARKERS_OLD` rows older than `gc.reflog-expire`; R4's fingerprints no longer current or latest-observed content of a live file node, its git facts and anchor results, the `FILEOBS` rows of trees idle > `gc.fileobs-idle-expire` (30 d), and `trash/` older than `gc.trash-expire` (14 d); unreferenced `cs.NNNN` and `tmp/` files swept.
- **Bulk passes and the page cache** ([71 RAM-m5]): rollup, `backup`, full export, `hist` retirement, repair and `links check --all`/`--deep` set `MEMORY_PRIORITY_LOW` for their I/O thread and open their inputs with `FILE_FLAG_SEQUENTIAL_SCAN`; `backup` copies large files with `COPY_FILE_NO_BUFFERING` (Linux: `posix_fadvise` sequential and don't-need, never on a log extent with unflushed groups; macOS: `F_NOCACHE`; a backup never clones or reflinks, [80 §2.12]), so a 1 GB pass does not evict the agents' standby pages.

### 4.10 Crash safety and the OS layer

- Every record has length, LSN, epoch and xxh3, and is valid only at its own position; recovery scans forward from `min(durable_lsn, checkpoint_lsn)` and adopts whole flushed groups by re-writing them (§4.5 step 6). A commit is acknowledged only after a covering flush, its publish and the writer's identity check of its chained trailer (§4.5 step 10; I-G1 of [80 §2.4.3]). `HEAD` is a cache with a durable bound and a boot identity; two checksummed slots make a torn write harmless, and the barrier before deletion writes both (§4.2). Flush failure aborts the process; the next flush holder re-writes the unflushed range under the writer byte before it flushes, and the group chain makes whatever it reads either the true log or a shorter valid prefix (ATC'20 lesson [08 §8.1]; a failed flush proves nothing, [X17 §3.5]). The epoch rejects records from a *different* init (a restored older copy of the same store is handled by the adopt-and-republish scan; `restore`/`repair` re-roll the epoch and zero-fill recycled extents, G25).
- **Namespace durability** ([72 M8]): a data-only flush of the log does not persist a rename or delete of another file. Every create, rename or delete a durable record depends on is followed by `durable-name` on the parent directory before that record ([80 §2.3]): `FlushFileBuffers` on a directory handle opened with `FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS` and write access on Windows (0.07 ms [M, X17 §3.4]), `fsync` of the directory on Linux, and `fsync` plus an `F_FULLFSYNC` barrier on macOS. Sealed files written by maintenance are created under their final numbers; the only rename on the commit path is a bulk commit's `cs.NNNN`, moved from its temporary name by `rename_noreplace` and followed by `durable-name` before the `Commit` record that names it (§4.3, [80 §2.3.2]). On every OS R4's `file mv` is a no-replace rename followed by `durable-name` of both parents (on Windows also with `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, kept until the rig calibration of §8.2 item 17 — deferred to after the release — shows it unnecessary), and `file rm`/`--trash` puts the directory barrier before their commits (§5e.5); intent recovery re-establishes that barrier — `sync_dir` of both parents — before it commits any roll-forward, and a cross-volume `file mv` is refused, so moirai never copies a project file and then deletes the original ([40 §3.4]; A1 re-review A1P-01, A1P-02); replace-renames (the store `config`, git's `.lock` protocol) and the `restore` swap have their own operations ([80 §2.3.2]). The `Vfs` and `ProjectFs` traits expose this as `sync_dir` ([80 §2.1]'s `os::project` list omits it; this document wins, and the M0 `ProjectFs` trait carries no journal read — `journal_since` — because E2 is not built: `JOURNALCUR`/`JournalCursor` stay reserved and a later E2 adds the method additively; A1 re-review A1P-02, A1P-17); the simulator implements the amended fault-model item (2) for it — a metadata operation is durable only after `sync_dir` of its parent, and any subset of the unsynced ones may be lost ([80 §2.3.5]); the rig calibration (§8.2 item 17, deferred to after the release by the owner review of 2026-09-27) covers namespace operations and may then drop `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` on Windows, never the directory flush that every rename point requires — until then Windows uses both; after a boot change `doctor` compares recent `FsIntentDone` outcomes with the file system.
- `doctor --verify` recomputes every derived structure and every branch head (pin ⊕ ops vs promoted segments), the sync residue identity (§4.6) and the marker cache against the state definition of I26′, per 65,536-id chunk (≤ 16 MB at 1e5, ≤ 32 MB at 1e6, [71 RAM-m4]); `doctor --fsck` verifies column checksums and BLAKE3 footers. `repair --rebuild-from-log` rebuilds every segment, blob and `hist` file and every derived-optional section from the log (all are derived) by driving the ordinary checkpoint pipeline from lsn 0 and ending with a streaming rollup (≤ 32 MB), and re-rolls the epoch; it is the recovery path for a segment-writer defect (M1, [60 §3.2]).
- `restore DIR --into EMPTY_DIR` performs the swap into place itself, under the writer and maintenance bytes (an exchange-rename where the file system has one, else two renames guarded by an intent file, [80 §2.3.2]), and sets the old store's `HEAD.retired` flag, which every long-lived process checks per operation before re-discovering the store ([72 m6]); `backup` flushes every copied file and the directory before it reports success and appends a durable `Backup` record, so the backup-age warning is truthful ([72 M9]).
- **The OS layer** (§14, [80 §2]). Only the `moirai-os` crate contains OS-specific code (GT20 d). **Locks:** byte-range locks only on `LOCK`, beyond EOF, ownership decided in user space first, one lock order (`LockFileEx`; OFD locks on Linux and macOS); never process-associated `fcntl` locks, never `flock` or `File::lock`, which locks the whole range on Windows [05 §6.3]; lock release after a crash may lag on every OS, so the waiter is bounded and names the holder. **Durability:** through the classes of [80 §2.3] (`NtFlushBuffersFileEx(DATA_SYNC_ONLY)`, `fdatasync`, `F_FULLFSYNC`); never `FILE_FLAG_WRITE_THROUGH`, `F_BARRIERFSYNC` or plain macOS `fsync` as durability; `ENOTSUP` refuses. **Mappings:** read-only and whole-file, of sealed read-only files only, never resized, size-checked against `total_len`, with a fault handler that exits 7. **Files:** a dozen stable files + sealed blob/hist files + pinned sets, no per-node or per-commit files, no create/delete churn on the commit path; the commit path renames only a bulk commit's `cs` file, followed by `durable-name`; bounded retry on errors 5/32 for deletes and export renames (Windows). **Process and output:** a binary at a stable install path; bodies never on argv; UTF-8 output with LF regardless of code page or OS.

---
## 5. Version control (final)

### 5a. moirai's own VCS and branching

#### 5a.1 Object model

| Object | Identity | Content | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **commit** | BLAKE3-256 of the canonical form (§4.6); `id16` prefix in indexes; displayed `c<8 hex>` | header (parents, kind, gen, seq, ref, ref_old, prev_on_ref, hlc, actor, role, session, git provenance, idem, sync_base, message, schema version) + changeset (typed ops with before-images) | `log` / `hist` |
| **changeset op** | position in its commit | `Create`, `Delete`, `Undelete`, `SetField`, `SetStatus`, `Incr`, `SetBody`, `AddEdge`, `RemoveEdge`, `SetEdgeProps` (R4 anchors), `Move`, `Schema` (incl. named queries, R5), `Conflict`, `Violation`, `Resolve` | inside the commit |
| **blob** | BLAKE3-128 of raw bytes | dictionary-compressed body (the codec of §8.2 item 6) | `blobs.NNNN` / log tail |
| **ref** | name: `main`, `lane/<n>`, `plan/<n>`, `merge/<dst>/from/<src>`, `import/<n>`, `orphans/<n>`, `tags/<n>` (the path segments `main`, `lane`, `plan`, `merge`, `import`, `orphans`, `tags` and `from` are reserved as lane and branch names) | `{ref_id (never reused), kind: work \| plan \| merge \| import \| tag, commit_id, lsn, base_pin, fork_commit, fork_seq, ops_since_fork, ref_seq_next, promoted_seg, gen, absorbed[(ref_id, ref_seq)]}` | `RefTable` records → `REFS` section |
| **reflog entry** | `(ref, seq)` | commits carry their own ref move (`ref`, `ref_old`); `RefUpdate {ref, old, new, reason, actor, hlc}` records non-commit moves | log |
| **client head** | key: `blake3_16(canonical directory)` (per component, with on-disk names; found by the directory's file id first, [80 §2.10] P9), `--client <name>`, or `session:<harness>:<id>` ([90 §4.1]; expires with the idempotency window) | `ClientHead {key, ref \| detached commit, hlc}` | log → `HEADS` |
| **tag** | name under `tags/` | a ref of kind `tag` with an optional message; `--pin` pins the checkpoint set | as ref |
| **pin** | segment file | refcount + holders | `Pin` records → `PINS` |
| **runtime records** | `#N` (a role lease: `#N` = 0 and its lease id) | lease, settled, deleted, cleared (§5d) | log → `LEASES`/`MARKERS` |

A commit's parents may be on any refs; the DAG is store-wide, so a branch can fork from a branch, `main` can be merged into a lane repeatedly and the lane back into `main`. `gen = 1 + max(parent.gen)` prunes ancestor walks: `gen(A) ≥ gen(B)` ⇒ A is not an ancestor of B (git commit-graph generation numbers [04 §3.1]).

Branch kinds and write masks: `work` (default; `main`, `lane/*`) — everything writable; `plan` (`plan/*`) — `status`, `resolution`, `assignee`, leases/claims read-only, everything else including `blocks`/`parent`/`gates` writable and validated (I33′); `merge`/`import` — staging refs written only by the merge/import machinery; `tag` — immutable.

#### 5a.2 Refs, the reflog, and how a commit lands

- Every ordinary commit carries `ref` and `ref_old`; landing it is a compare-and-swap on the ref table entry (`old == ref_old`) performed under the writer byte in the same publish. **The parent of a plain write is the branch tip after tail replay under the writer byte** (G20), so two agents committing on one lane serialize on the writer byte exactly as on a shared trunk and never fail for "someone committed first"; only an explicit `--if-tip <commit>` guard can fail with exit 4 and the current tip.
- `RefUpdate` records exist only for non-commit moves (`branch` create/delete, `tag`, `undo`, `op restore`); they are written in the same flushed group as any commit they accompany.
- Every commit carries `ref_seq`, its position on its ref's chain, allocated from the ref entry's `ref_seq_next` and **never reused**: `undo` moves the tip back but does not reset the counter, so `(ref_id, ref_seq)` names one commit forever (CM1). `ahead/behind main` is arithmetic on these numbers (`ref_seq(tip main) − absorbed_L[main]` and `ref_seq(tip L) − absorbed_main[L]`, §5d.1).
- `moirai reflog <ref>` is a walk of the ref's chain (`prev_on_ref`) plus its `RefUpdate` records; the last 32 moves per ref are cached in `REFS` for O(1) `undo`.

#### 5a.3 How a branch lives on the engine

```
view(X) = SEG(pin_X)                          // sealed checkpoint set of main at seq P ≤ fork_seq(X)
        ⊕ ops(main, (P, fork_seq(X)])         // trunk ops from that checkpoint to the fork commit
        ⊕ fold over X's own commits in order:
              ordinary commit c  → ops(c)
              sync commit s_k    → ops(main, (M_{k-1}, M_k]) ⊕ resolutions(s_k)     // merge-by-reference (G17)
        ⊕ tail(X)                             // this process's overlay for X since its last replay
view(Y forked from X) = view(X)@fork_Y ⊕ ops(Y)   // recursive; promotion of X makes it cheap
view(main) = SEG(current) ⊕ tail(main)
```

- **Fork** (`moirai branch lane/l5np [--from main|<commit>]`): one durable `RefUpdate` (create) + one `Pin` increment on the newest sealed set with `upto_seq ≤ fork_seq`. Cost ~2 ms, zero copying. The pin keeps that set alive across later rollups of `main`.
- **Overlay build** (first read of X in a process): read the `Checkpoint` records since X's last promotion (a few KB each; ~1–2 per day) for X's lsn lists, `pread` X's own commits and, for sync windows, `main`'s commits `(M_{k−1}, M_k]` through `main`'s per-ref index; decode into the compact overlay of §4.5. Cost O(X's commits + `main`'s commits in synced windows **since X's last promotion**), independent of the other branches' traffic. The second term is `main`'s whole traffic for a lane that syncs — and lanes sync at every clean `SubagentStart` — so promotion is driven by it (next item): without that, a 14-day lane synced daily would replay 4.2–8.4k `main` commits (10–33k ops), 30–120 ms and 1.5–5 MB per first read [70 S1, 71 RAM-M1]. With it, the first read of any lane is ≤ 10 ms and its overlay ≤ 1 MiB, fresh lanes 1–3 ms; the builder streams through a fixed buffer, never reads extents whole.
- **Promotion** (physical in M1, policy in M3; the thresholds are store parameters): the ref table keeps, in O(1) per commit and per sync, `overlay_ops`/`overlay_bytes` = trunk ops from the pin to the fork + X's own ops + the ops of every synced window since the last promotion (§4.2). X is promoted — `seg.b<ref_id>.K`, named by X's decimal, never-reused `ref_id` (overridden rows, lists, bitsets, `TOUCH` bitmap) written and `base_pin` moved to the newest sealed checkpoint of `main` — when `overlay_ops` exceeds `store.promotion.overlay-ops` (set at M0 from the 10 ms lane-read gate at the measured build rate, est. 3,000–5,000 ops), `overlay_bytes` exceeds `store.promotion.overlay-bytes` (1 MiB), or X is more than `store.promotion.age-checkpoints` (16) checkpoints behind the head (G16). A sync that crosses a threshold promotes in the syncing process after it releases the writer byte, under the maintenance byte (sliced in the MCP server); at every delta checkpoint the maintenance holder also promotes every branch whose counters crossed a threshold; after a rollup every branch pinned to the superseded base is promoted, so at most two bases are resident. 10–100 ms and ≤ 8 MB, never in the writer byte. `doctor --verify` compares pin ⊕ ops with every promoted segment.
- **`sync`** (`merge main --into <lane>`): appends a `sync` commit on the lane with `sync_base = tip(main)` and **only the residue** — every key whose merged value differs from what `main`'s window alone yields at the lane: the keys both sides touched since the last sync, plus keys changed by a one-sided composition (a glob or path that only one side touched, composed through the other side's recorded `path_moves`) or by a derived-uid re-key (typically dozens) [72 M5]; `main`'s ops are never copied (G17). The sync asserts that window ⊕ residue equals its canonical state diff (§4.6), so the lane view, the canonical op list, the image tree and the reference model agree. A lane's history therefore grows by its own work plus residues, not by `main`'s churn (F-D2: ~0.1–0.2 GB/year at 50 lanes instead of 0.2–1 GB). A hook applies a clean sync automatically only when it touches ≤ `hooks.sync-auto-keys` (2,000) keys (so the hook stays within its 4 MB, §8.3; ≤ 8 MB is the gate of an explicit `sync` of a lane 14 days behind, [71 RAM-M6]). `sync --refork` (optional, unexported lanes only) instead re-forks the pin at `main`'s tip when the preview is clean and disjoint ([22 §9 graft 8]).
- **`merge lane → main`** copies the lane's folded ops into the merge commit on `main` (that is the data landing on trunk; O(lane ops) once per lane); cross-lane merges likewise.
- **Fold of a side since the LCA (F-D11, CM9).** The ops a branch contributes to a merge or to `diff A...B` are not "its own commits" but a **DAG walk over per-ref chain segments** bounded by the LCA: start at the side's tip; follow `prev_on_ref` within the ref; at the ref's first commit hop to `fork_commit` on the parent ref and continue along *that* chain; expand every `sync` commit met on the way by reference into `main`'s window `(M_{k−1}, M_k]` through `main`'s per-ref index; stop at the LCA. For a lane forked from `main` this is the lane's own chain (O(own commits)); for Y forked from X it is Y's chain plus X's segment from `fork_Y` back to the LCA, so `merge Y --into main` carries X's ops and uses the right base. Cost O(sum of the segment lengths involved), never O(store traffic since the fork). The same walk realises `view(Y)`.
- **Cost with ~50 live branches** (est.): ≈ 18 KB in `REFS` (50 refs × ≈ 68 B of fixed fields plus each ref's absorbed vector of ≈ 6 B × 50 refs, §4.2; CM1); pins retain the base files the branches' pins name — at most two after any rollup, because promotion re-bases every branch pinned to a superseded base (~12 MB each at 1e5, ~115 MB at 1e6) — plus their delta and promoted-branch files (30–120 files, ~10–30 MB at 1e5) — 25–60 MB of disk at 1e5, 0.25–0.6 GB at 1e6, zero private RAM until a branch is read, and at most two copies of the hot index in the page cache; `doctor` lists pins held by abandoned branches; `branch -d` releases them.

#### 5a.4 HEAD per client and checkout semantics

Client key resolution, first hit wins, in the **order of record** — [90 §4.1]'s Branch row, which §2.3, §7.2, [50 §3.9] and [60 §3.4]'s test driver cite and the reference model encodes as data: explicit `--branch <ref>` (MCP `branch`; a dispatch marker typed into it is explicit) → the branch recorded on the presented lease (`--lease L`/`lease`, or `MOIRAI_LEASE` under [90 §4.1]'s binding rule; a lease always carries its branch; a mismatch between an explicit branch and the lease is exit 5 unless `--move-lease <ref>`, which warns) → the Codex `sandboxCwd` (`_meta`) or the Claude stamp's `cwd`, through its directory binding → `MOIRAI_BRANCH` → the dispatch marker stamped into `ctx` at SubagentStart (`moirai:… branch=lane/l5np`) → `--client <name>`/`MOIRAI_CLIENT` → the registered binding for the current directory (`moirai worktree bind`, longest bound prefix) → the binding of the git worktree if a `.git` hint exists → the `default-branch` key (§13; default `main`). An inherited environment therefore never outranks the lease a caller presents ([91] M2), and an MCP call that passes `lease` but no `branch` resolves to the lease's branch (D3). The MCP server resolves per call in the same order (§7.2); unresolved reads use the session's checkout (`session:<harness>:<id>` key). Every result prints `branch: lane/l5np | rev 4471 | …` on its first line (§7.1).

For R4 a binding also records the tree's **expected git ref and base commit** ([40] R-15): each branch has at most one designated tree and each tree designates at most one branch, trees identified by their exact git top-level (a longest-prefix binding still resolves a command's *branch*, but never makes a nested worktree a designated tree), and only that tree, with its HEAD on the expected ref, writes file-link re-binds (I-F12, §5e.4).

`moirai checkout <ref|commit>` writes a durable `ClientHead` for the resolved key. Detached reads are allowed; writes on a detached head are refused unless `--branch-new <name>`. Many processes on different branches: each builds only its own overlays; writes serialize on the writer byte with `ref` = the client's branch; nothing about one client's head affects another. `moirai branch --list` prints tip, kind, fork point, ops-since-fork, ahead/behind `main` (absorbed-vector arithmetic, O(1)), unabsorbed markers it holds, last actor, bound directories, live leases.

#### 5a.5 Op log, reflog, undo, revert, cherry-pick

The commit log **is** the operation log (every agent write is one commit, jj's two levels collapse [04 §3.8]); `RefUpdate` + `ClientHead` records make it a complete jj-style op log, so any past *view* (set of ref tips) can be restored.

| Verb | Effect | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| `undo [--ref R] [N] [--expect <tip>]` | moves R back to the value N moves ago (default: the client's branch, N = 1); CAS on `--expect`, which defaults to the tip the client last read (N13f); appends `RefUpdate{reason: undo}` carrying R's recomputed absorbed vector (`absorbed_R[R]` = the restored tip's `ref_seq`; the other entries from the newest merge/sync/fork record at or below the restored tip, found by walking `prev_on_ref` — O(commits since that record), acceptable for an explicit, rare verb); **recomputes the markers of R over the moved range** ([72 M4]): `cleared` (scoped to `(#N, R)`) for every completion or deletion commit that leaves R's history, and a re-emitted `settled`/`deleted` for every such commit that re-enters it while the state still holds at the new tip — so undoing a `reopen` re-excludes the task everywhere — each with a triage line; nothing is rewritten; undone commits stay until GC | 1 durable commit; O(commits in the moved range) |
| `op log` / `op restore <seq>` | lists ref-set changes store-wide; restores all refs to their values at `seq`, recomputing markers for every ref it moves in both directions exactly as `undo` does | scan; 1 commit |
| `revert <commit> [--onto R]` | appends the inverse changeset (before-images make inversion exact) as a new commit on R after running the validators; a `RemoveEdge`/`SetField` whose target or before-image no longer matches is a `NotFound`/`DATA` case: `NotFound` stages the revert on `merge/<R>/from/<commit>`, `DATA` lands a `FieldEdit` conflict value (I34′); refused with the dependent set when a later commit on R *depends structurally* on the reverted one, defined as: it added a structural edge to, or a child of, a node the reverted commit created; or it completed a task the reverted commit reopened; or it resolved a conflict the reverted commit introduced. Reverting a **merge** commit is allowed with `--mainline 1` (CL8): the merge record stores the merged ops with before-images, so the exact inverse relative to the first parent exists and goes through the same validators (`--mainline 2` is refused); reverting a **sync** commit is refused (it stores only the G17 residue; use `undo` on the lane) | 2 ms + O(ops) |
| `cherry-pick <commit> [--onto R]` | 3-way apply of the commit's changeset with base = its first parent onto R's view; same typed rules, validators and `NotFound`/`DATA` handling; result is a new commit (`kind: cherry-pick`, origin in the trailer) | as revert |
| `reflog R` | the ref's move history with actor, reason, hlc | O(moves) |
| `rebase --onto` | **not built** (a scope decision, [60 §1.3]: history rewriting; the only verb that would change lane commit ids in the image) | — |

#### 5a.6 log, diff, show, blame, as-of

- `log [R] [--graph] [--node #N] [--actor] [--since seq] [--all-branches]`: walk from the ref tip through `prev_on_ref`/parents (gen-ordered priority queue); `--node` walks the node's per-view op chain (`last_op_lsn` → `prev`), switching into `main`'s chain segment for a sync window when the chain crosses a sync commit; per-node O(edits to the node); a cold `hist` frame decodes in ≤ 0.1–0.5 ms.
- `diff A..B` (A ancestor of B): fold of changesets along the path, grouped per key, rendered as typed hunks. `diff A...B` (symmetric, lane vs `main`): LCA by gen-pruned walk, then both sides' folds side by side with a `both` column for keys touched on both sides — the merge preview. Example rendering as [B §5.5].
- `show #N@<commit|seq|time> [--branch R]`: reverse-apply the node's chain from R's view until it passes the target; O(edits after the target). Membership of a chain op in the target's ancestry is gen-pruned.
- `blame #N [field]`: last op per field/edge with commit metadata.
- Whole-graph as-of (`USE <revspec>` in a query part, `--at REV` on every read verb, §7.7; revisions are `main`, `lane/x`, `c<hex>`, `s<seq>`, `REF~n`, `REF^n`, `REF@n`, `REF@<time>` and ranges `a..b`/`a...b`): reverse-apply from the nearest later pinned set within 50k ops, else replay forward from the nearest earlier pin (every merge into `main` and every `--pin` tag pins a set, so the distance is bounded by pin spacing). Output carries no derived fields unless `--recompute` (I18′).

#### 5a.7 Merge algorithm

`moirai merge <src> [--into <dst>] [--policy P] [--strict] [--base <commit>] [--message M]`; `moirai sync` = `merge main --into <current lane>`.

0. **Sync-first precondition for merges into `main`**: if `tip(main) ∉ ancestors(tip(src))`, the merge first performs `sync` (merge `main` into `src`) as step 0; both are computed before the writer byte is taken (§4.5 phase 1) and committed as **one flushed group** under it; if the sync stages, the whole merge stages and the message says which. After a successful sync, LCA(src, main) = `tip(main)` and the merge into `main` cannot conflict (every key: dst equals base → take theirs); it can still be refused for unresolved `conflicted` nodes on `src` (`merge-check` lists them). This makes the daily path a single-LCA path and criss-cross impossible there; a concurrent hooked `sync` cannot race because hooks only run `sync --check` (D5) and a `sync` of lane L is refused while *its own* staging ref `merge/<L>/from/main` exists — a staged sync of another lane, or a staged `merge/main/from/<L>`, never blocks it (I41′, CM5).
1. **LCA** of `tip(src)` and `tip(dst)` by gen-pruned bidirectional walk. Multiple LCAs → **the recursive virtual base** (I31′ as amended by [60 §3.4]): the LCAs are merged pairwise in generation order (ties by lowest commit id) by these same typed rules and the result is the base; a key whose virtual-base value is a conflict value is clean when both sides hold the same value and conflicts whenever they differ, so two sides that resolved a criss-cross differently always conflict (a single chosen LCA could take one of them silently) and two sides that resolved it identically never do; property-tested. `--base` overrides.
2. **Changesets**: fold each side since the LCA with the segment walk of §5a.3 (per-ref index, G15; the walk hops to the parent ref at a fork, so a branch forked from a branch contributes its ancestor's segment too — CM9); sync commits met on the walk expand by reference, except that `main`'s ops in synced windows are dst's own history when dst = `main` and are skipped there. Each fold is `key → final value`.
3. **Base per key**: the value at the LCA state, computed by reverse-applying the dst side's per-node chain for that key from `tip(dst)` back past the LCA (O(edits since LCA) per touched key; the LCA is a pin or within 4k ops of one on the daily path) (I25′, N1).
4. **Partition by key**; disjoint keys commute and apply directly; same-key changes go to the typed rule:

| Field type | Rule | On disagreement |
|---|---|---|
| status (per-kind lattice from the schema) | join if both are forward moves and comparable | `StatusFork` conflict value (side state vs forward; incomparable, e.g. `confirmed` vs `refuted`; `reopen` vs `done`) |
| enum / number scalar | equal → take; one side = base → take the other | `FieldEdit{base, ours, theirs}` |
| counter (`Incr`) | sum of both deltas over base | never conflicts |
| set | add-wins union with removals relative to base | never conflicts |
| text | line diff3 against the base blob; `doc.section` bodies also run the removed-text guard | `TextHunk` conflict value; `RemovedTextNotInBase` violation |
| `parent` / `order` | Kleppmann move in HLC order; a cycle-creating move is skipped and logged | `HierarchyCycle` violation |
| existence | delete vs modify → `DeleteVsModify`; `--policy delete-wins\|resurrect` per kind (default: tasks delete-wins, knowledge resurrect) | conflict value on the (resurrected) node |
| structural edge to a node deleted on the other side | `DanglingEdge` violation; suggested resolution = the edge's delete policy or `--replaced-by` | structural, staged |
| `supersedes` | second active superseder → `SupersedeFork` | conflict value |
| owner-authority fields (`authority = owner`, `owner_quote`) | `main` wins when `dst = main`; otherwise conflict | `OwnerFieldEdited` |
| schema | weakening changes union; strengthening on both sides → `SchemaConflict` | structural |
| `pinned_commit` on citations | edge props: theirs if ours = base, else conflict | `FieldEdit` |
| R4 observation composite (`path`, `oid`, `bytes`, `observed_git`, `observed_blob`, `relink` as one key, [40 §5.5]) | one side changed → take it; both, same path → dst's; both, different paths, and one side's root node gained since the LCA an `explicit`/`confirmed`/`committed` `path_moves` entry covering the other side's old and new paths → **compose** (the pre-composition path joins `aliases`) | `FieldEdit`, later resolved by observation in a writer tree fresh for every side (§5e.4) or by `links fix` |
| R4 existence of derived-uid kinds | created on both sides → one node (`created` = least (generation, commit id) among the creating commits the history holds; provenance only); created on one side while the other side removed or deleted that uid → **re-key, never resurrect**, to uid(root, `origin_path`, U), with every edge that side added since the LCA re-pointed ([40 §5.5]; A1 re-review S-01) | `DeleteVsModify` when the node existed at the LCA |
| R4 artifact status | `planned` vs `present` → `present` | `StatusFork` for `present` vs `removed` — never resolved by path presence |
| R4 `(root, exact path)` | — | `PathClaim` when two live nodes with different uids claim one exact path |
| R4 anchors, globs, `path_moves` | anchors add-wins by anchor uid; globs add-wins and composed through the other side's recorded moves — a composition changes a key only one side touched, so a `sync` records it in its residue (§5a.3); `path_moves` add-wins, composed in (hlc, from, to) order | anchor selectors repinned differently on both sides → `FieldEdit` |
| R5 named-query definition ([50 §4.4]) | one atomic value (signature, shape, budget, text); changed on both sides to the same canonical-AST hash → clean | `FieldEdit`; `DROP` vs modify → `DeleteVsModify` |

5. **Apply** to a candidate overlay on dst's view (TerminusDB "commit without advancing the label" [04 §3.3]).
6. **Validate** in this order (I37′): apply all `parent` moves → re-derive the implied exogenous edge set → check every added `blocks`/`gates` edge and every implied edge whose endpoints moved with incremental PK (full Kahn over the combined precedence graph when the merge touches > `store.kahn-fallback-edges` (1,000) precedence edges, 10–50 ms at 1e6 est.; implied exogenous edges derived on the fly from the parent CSR, never materialised, [71 RAM-m4]) → I2 dangling structural edges (reverse index ∩ deletions on either side) → I4 forest → I6/I7 cardinality → I-F1 path claims → schema → every named query the merge touched, or whose referenced schema it touched, parsed and bound (`QueryInvalid`) and its call graph checked (`QueryCycle`) → `plan/*` read-only → `Duplicate`/`Contradiction` hints. The merge engine never reads the file system or git (R4's conflicts that only the file system can settle land as conflict values).
7. **Emit** one merge commit with two parents, the merged ops, one `Conflict` op per value conflict and one `Violation` op per structural problem, `affected` for the change feed, the markers its net ops imply (§4.5 step 4; a staging ref emits none — the landing commit does, [72 M4]) and dst's new absorbed vector (`absorbed_dst[src] = ref_seq(tip src)`, every other entry the max of both sides, §5d.1); `rev_seq` of every touched node becomes the merge commit's seq (above both inputs).
8. **Advance or stage**: zero violations and (`--strict` ⇒ zero conflicts) → the merge commit lands on dst (CAS on the tip; nodes with conflict values get `conflicted` and leave `ready`). Otherwise it lands on the staging ref **`merge/<dst>/from/<src>`** (kind `merge`; a `sync` of lane L stages on `merge/<L>/from/main`, a merge of L into `main` on `merge/main/from/<L>`), dst is untouched, the CLI prints the violations with suggested resolutions and exits 6. Staging is per `(dst, src)` pair (I41′, CM5): two lanes with staged syncs coexist, and only a second merge of the *same* pair is refused. `resolve <key> --take ours|theirs|base|--value V` and `resolve --all --policy P` append `Resolve` commits on that staging ref; `merge --continue [SRC [--into DST]]` (the pair defaults to the current branch's single open staging ref, otherwise it must be named) re-runs steps 5–8 **against the current dst tip** with the staged resolutions as an overlay (the SQLite rebaser idea [04 §3.13]); `merge --abort` deletes the staging ref. Pins: a merge into `main` pins the resulting checkpoint set.

Merge cost (est.): a 2k-op lane vs 5k trunk ops since the LCA: two folds 1–3 ms + base as-of reads for ~200 touched keys 1–5 ms + typed merge + validators = 5–20 ms at 1e4, 10–40 ms at 1e5, 20–80 ms at 1e6 (+5–50 ms if full Kahn), all computed before the writer byte; the writer byte is held for scan, re-validation and append, and the flush runs outside it by group commit (§4.5 steps 5–10, [80 §2.4.3]). Private memory ≤ 8 MB at 1e5 and ≤ 16 MB at 1e6 including full Kahn ([71 RAM-M6]). CI gates: ≤ 50 ms at 1e5; writer-byte hold p99 ≤ 5 ms.

#### 5a.8 Conflict and violation taxonomy

| Class | Kind | Lands on the ref? | Default resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FieldEdit`, `StatusFork`, `TextHunk`, `DeleteVsModify`, `SupersedeFork`, `OwnerFieldEdited` (dst ≠ main), `DATA` (revert/cherry-pick before-image mismatch), `PathClaim` (R4) | value conflict | yes unless `--strict`; node `conflicted`, out of `ready` | agent `resolve`; per-kind auto-policies as schema data (`theirs` for findings, `ours` for rules are opt-in) |
| `DanglingEdge`, `Cycle`, `HierarchyCycle`, `IdCollision` (import), `SchemaConflict`, `RemovedTextNotInBase`, `ImageParse` (import), `NotFound` (revert/cherry-pick), `TombstoneRemoved` (import hint escalated when the node is referenced), `QueryInvalid`, `QueryCycle` (R5, [50] F18) | structural violation | **never**; staged on `merge/<dst>/from/<src>` or `import/<ref>` | suggested fix printed; `resolve --policy` |
| `Duplicate`, `Contradiction`, `ForeignMerge` | hint | yes, as a log line | none |

Conflict values render in `show` as `~conflict: base=… ours=… theirs=… (moirai resolve '#91.body' …)`; packs render a conflicted knowledge node as one line with the **base** text and the resolve hint, never with `<<<<<<<` markers (N15). `git`-style markers appear only in `show --full --markers`.

#### 5a.9 Tags, branch delete, GC

- `tag <name> [commit] [-m] [--pin]`: a ref of kind `tag`; `--pin` pins the checkpoint set for fast as-of.
- `branch -d <name>`: refused if not merged into `main` unless `-D`; releases the pin refcount; the reflog keeps the tip for `gc.reflog-expire`; **live leases on that branch are released with a triage note; `-D` re-attributes every `settled`/`deleted` marker the branch still held unabsorbed to a live ref that contains the marker's commit (a branch forked from it after the commit, `absorbed_Y[X] ≥ ref_seq`) and clears only the markers no live ref still holds, each with a triage line in `doctor`/`brief`** (N13d, I26′ by state, [72 M4]), so a completion that survives on a fork keeps blocking re-dispatch and a discarded one stops blocking it without being forgotten. `-D` warns how many commits, completions and deletions are dropped.
- `lane close|freeze <name>`: sets the lane node status and removes the directory binding; `apply` for a run bound to a closed lane resolves to the branch the lane merged into (N13e).
- GC: §4.9. Exported commits never disappear from the image; reflog expiry and cruft delay are the only places a moirai commit can vanish.

#### 5a.10 Per-branch cost summary (est.; inputs [05 §2], [20 §1.6])

| Operation | 1e4 | 1e5 | 1e6 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `branch` (fork), `checkout`, `undo`, `tag` | 2–3 ms (one durable commit) | same | same |
| first read of a fresh lane / 14-day lane synced daily / 60-day `plan/*` branch (G15 + overlay-driven promotion, §5a.3) | 1–3 / ≤ 10 / ≤ 10 ms; overlay ≤ 1 MiB | same | same |
| subsequent reads (long-lived process) | as `main` + overlay probe ~0.2 µs; catch-up scans new records and keeps its own | | |
| `sync` (merge-by-reference, dozens of resolution ops) | 5–20 ms | 10–40 ms | 20–80 ms |
| `merge lane → main` (2k-op lane) | 5–20 ms | 10–40 ms | 20–80 ms (+ Kahn) |
| promotion (at the overlay threshold) | 10–40 ms | 20–60 ms | 30–100 ms; ≤ 8 MB |
| `log --node` | 10–50 µs per shown edit (+ ≤ 0.5 ms per cold `hist` frame) | | |
| as-of at a pinned set | 5–50 ms | | |
| 50 live branches: disk | ~5 MB | 25–60 MB | 0.25–0.6 GB |
| 50 live branches: RAM | 0 until read; ≤ 1 MiB per branch read per process; MCP LRU ≤ `mcp.overlay-bytes` (4 MiB) and ≤ 8 overlays; ≤ 2 bases in the page cache | | |

---
### 5b. The git-compatible image (R3)

Goals in order: (1) every moirai commit, branch and tag maps to git objects by a pure function of moirai data and the object format, so two stores holding the same commit produce byte-identical objects (I28′); (2) a human or an agent can read and diff the image in any git tool; (3) an edit or merge made on the git side is importable as a first-class moirai commit validated by the same engine; (4) the store stays canonical — the image is derived, never the working copy (Beads' dual-source lesson [03 §8.2]). Git is a storage and transport target; it is never consulted to answer a query.

#### 5b.1 Tree layout

```
.moirai-image                        format marker (§5b.3): format version, object format, schema version — nothing store-local
schema/kinds.moi  fields.moi  edges.moi     schema-as-data, sorted rows, one file per table
schema/queries/<q>.moi               project named queries in portable form; q = 32 hex digits of BLAKE3-256(name), the name on the file's name: line (R5, [50 §4.4], F3, [80] X-F9)
nodes/<h1>/<h2>/<uid>.moi            h1 = uid hex[0..2], h2 = uid hex[2..4]; uid = 32 lowercase hex (R4 file and root nodes too)
refs/heads.moi  refs/tags.moi        checkpoint-granularity images only: the moirai ref table rows (name, kind)
```

Two levels of 8-bit fan-out over `uid` (65,536 leaf trees): at 1e4 nodes most leaves hold 0–1 entries, at 1e5 ≈ 1.5, at 1e6 ≈ 15; one commit touching k nodes rewrites root + ≤ k mid trees + ≤ k leaves (root ≈ 7.4 KB SHA-1 / 10.5 KB SHA-256 raw at ≥ 1e5 nodes) — the path-copy tax bounded by the fan-out, not by N. The fan-out is part of the format version. **Out-edges only** are serialized, in the source node's file: adding `A blocks B` rewrites `A.moi` only; in-edges are rebuilt on import (a rule cited by 10k nodes would otherwise change on every citation). **Tombstones are files** and stay forever; a tombstone keeps every out-edge the store retains for the dead node — flagged `blocks`/`gates` edges marked `flagged`, historical edges as-is — so the X4 protection and the reverse index survive the round trip (I39′, CM4); a file that *disappears* is a foreign hard delete. Bodies are the tail of the node file (one blob per node keeps object count ≈ N; `git diff` shows prose). No `#N`-named files, no kind-named directories (kinds change; `#N` is store-local).

Beside the hashed tree, each destination carries one **unhashed side ref per exporting store**, `refs/moirai/meta/<store-id>` (one name in every destination, the separate bare image repository included), whose tree holds `meta.moi` (`store-id`, `granularity` per exported ref, `moi-format`, `last-export-seq`) and `aliases.moi` (rows `uid #N`). Everything store- or destination-local lives there (CB2); it is never part of any commit's tree, and an importer reads it only as a hint. Git attributes (`*.moi -text diff`) go into the destination's `info/attributes`, never into the tree (CL1).

#### 5b.2 The `.moi` node file format (canonical text, version 1)

The exporter is the only writer of canonical bytes; the importer accepts a superset (CRLF, trailing whitespace, reordered lines) and normalises.

1. UTF-8, no BOM, bytes preserved (no Unicode normalisation). The **header** (everything before the body separator) is LF-only with no trailing whitespace on any line and exactly one LF after its last line; lines inside a block string (`<<` … `>>`) and the **body** are exempt from the whitespace rule and are byte-exact (I40′, CM3).
2. Line 1: `moirai-node 1`. Then header lines `key: value` in this fixed order, absent/empty values omitted: `uid`, `kind`, `title`, `status`, `resolution`, `priority`, `criticality`, `confidence`, `authority`, `parent`, `order`, `created`, `updated`, `deleted`, `flags` (source-truth flags only: `archived`, `frozen`, `pinned`; derived flags are never written). **There is no `id:` line** (N4): `#N` is store-local and would make identical data hash differently across stores. The unhashed side ref `refs/moirai/meta/<store-id>` (§5b.1) carries the exporting store's numbers in `aliases.moi` for humans; importers may read it as a hint (§5b.6 step 5).
3. Then, each group sorted bytewise: `field <name>: <value>` lines; `label <value>` lines (no colon); **`incr <field> <+k|-k> c<commit>` ledger lines** for counter-typed fields (one per `Incr` op, sorted by commit id; the current value is the sum and is never written as a `field` line; a foreign edit may write any token in place of `c<commit>`) (N6); `edge <kind> -> <uid> [pin=c<commit>] [flagged]` lines sorted by (kind name, dst uid, props); `conflict <key> class=<class> base=<v> ours=<v> theirs=<v>` lines sorted by key, where `<key>` is `field.<name>`, `status`, `body`, `parent`, `edge.<kind>.<uid>` or `existence` and every value is a single-line value — JSON-string-escaped whenever it contains a newline, so the three sides of a body conflict are three JSON strings on one line and the `---` body section is **omitted** while a body conflict exists (**the ordinary `field`/`edge`/body line for a key is omitted while a `conflict` line exists for it**, N12). `violation` lines never appear: violations exist only on staging refs, which are never exported (CB1).
4. Values: integers decimal; floats shortest round-trip (Ryu) with a mandatory `.` or exponent; booleans `true`/`false`; timestamps RFC 3339 UTC `Z` with millisecond precision; node references as 32-hex `uid`; commit references as `c<64 hex>`; `order` as a base-62 fractional-index string (`[0-9A-Za-z]+`, compared bytewise, generated between its neighbours the way Figma-style fractional indexes are, never renormalised); sets `[a, b, c]` sorted bytewise; strings bare when they contain no control characters, no leading/trailing spaces, do not start with `"`, `[` or `{` and are ≤ 4,096 bytes, otherwise JSON-string-escaped; multi-line text fields use block form `field why: <<` … two-space-indented raw lines … `>>`, chosen iff the value contains a newline.
5. `created`/`updated`/`deleted` are `c<commit-id>` followed by the commit's HLC timestamp for readability; importers use only the commit id.
6. Derived state (`open_blockers`, `ready`, `suspect`, `is_blocker`, rollups, `conflicted`, `rev_seq`, `lsn`, `claimed`, `settled`, `stale`) is never written (I36′).
7. The body, if any, follows a line consisting of `---` and is encoded as **the body bytes followed by exactly one LF**; the decoder takes everything after the `---` line and strips exactly one trailing LF. A body that ends with LF therefore yields a file ending in two LFs, a body without a final LF a file ending in one, and the file never ends without a newline (so `git diff` never shows `\ No newline at end of file`). Bodies are byte-exact: Markdown hard breaks (trailing double spaces), inner `---` lines and multiple final newlines survive; `SetBody{new_hash}` (BLAKE3-128 of the stored bytes) equals the hash of the decoded bytes (I40′). The only normalisation a body ever receives is in the **store**, at write time: CRLF and lone CR become LF (a documented store rule; the image never normalises inside a body, and a git-side file with CRLF is normalised by the importer exactly as the store would have). A node without a body has no `---` line.
8. A tombstone file contains `moirai-node 1`, `uid`, `kind` (at deletion), `title`, `deleted: c<commit> <time>`, `field reason: …`, `field replaced_by: <uid>` **and the out-edges the store retains for the dead node**: `edge blocks -> <uid> flagged` / `edge gates -> <uid> flagged` for structural edges left flagged by `rm` (neither re-pointed nor resolved), and historical out-edges (`cites`, `mentions`, `derived_from`, …, and R4's `anchor` lines) as-is. The importer re-creates them, sets `has_dangling` on the dependents of flagged edges and rebuilds the reverse index from them (I39′, CM4). Nothing else — no other fields, no body — is kept in a tombstone.
9. **R4 and R5 forms** ([40 §5.7], [50 §4.4]; part of the M0 ABNF). File nodes and root nodes are ordinary node files: `title` is omitted for artifacts (derived from `path`), `origin_pred` appears only when the derivation had one, and `path_moves` is a block of one JSON array per entry (`["<hlc as 20-digit zero-padded decimal>","<class>","<from/>","<to/>","<git oid>"]`) sorted by (hlc, from, to). Anchors are lines in the **referrer's** file — `anchor <anchor uid> -> <dst uid> kind=… mode=… watch=… [scope=…] [quote_h=…] [prefix_h=…] [suffix_h=…] [end_h=…] [quote=…] [prefix=…] [suffix=…] [end=…] [hint=…] [window=…] [span=…] [blob=…] [git=…] captured=… [pred=…] v=<resolver>` — sorted by (dst uid, anchor uid), values bare when safe and JSON-string-escaped otherwise, `window` in unpadded base64url; the `at` adjacency is implied by them, so no `edge at` line exists. The digests `quote_h`/`prefix_h`/`suffix_h`/`end_h` (BLAKE3-128, the canonical form's carriers; `end_h` and `pred` by the A1 re-review, S-03, S-04) are on every line; a destination with `image.dest.<name>.anchor-text = hash-only` omits the text, and in `full` mode the importer verifies each text against its digest (`ImageParse` on a mismatch). An anchor imported without its text is in the `text-unavailable` sub-state: it resolves by hint, window and scope only, never `fresh` by quote, until `links fix --repin --at …` recaptures it from a tree ([72 M6]). A project named query is a `moirai-query 1` file (name, grammar version, parameter signature, shape, budget class, `---`, the portable LQ text, LF only, byte-exact) whose text, re-bound, holds no store-local constant — no node- or revision-typed integer, `#N`, `s<seq>`, commit prefix, reflog revision or anchor handle; exporter and importer check it by re-binding, never by a character pattern ([50 §4.4], A1 re-review S-02). R4 and R5 add **no trailer and no commit annotation** (§5b.4).

Example — task `#12` of the walk-through after `#40` was deleted with `--replaced-by #52`, at `nodes/01/8f/018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0b9e77.moi`:

```
moirai-node 1
uid: 018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0b9e77
kind: task
title: Byte-range lock protocol
status: in_progress
priority: 1
criticality: normal
parent: 018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0b9e09
order: a0V
created: c9b2e6c1d4f0a7e3b5c8d1f2a9e4b7c6d3f0a1e2b5c8d7f4a3e6b9c2d5f8a1b4 2026-09-21T14:02:11.483Z
updated: c4470a11e2f3b4c5d6e7f8091a2b3c4d5e6f708192a3b4c5d6e7f8091a2b3c4d 2026-09-25T14:02:40.011Z
field acceptance: writer and flush bytes locked per range; a dead holder's range freed by the OS
field assignee: dev#1
field estimate: 3
field files_owned: [src/lock.rs, src/vfs/lock_bytes.rs]

field work_kind: impl
label l5np
label storage
incr reopen_count +1 c4470a11e2f3b4c5d6e7f8091a2b3c4d5e6f708192a3b4c5d6e7f8091a2b3c4d
edge blocks -> 018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0b9e51
edge cites -> 018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0bd3a5 pin=c4410f0e2d3c4b5a69788796a5b4c3d2e1f0a9b8c7d6e5f4a3b2c1d0e9f8a7b6
edge implements -> 018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0b9e40
edge mentions -> 018f3c2e7a117b3c9d5e4c2f1a0b9e52
---
Writers lock byte ranges of LOCK, never the whole file.
See #52 for the reader registry this depends on.
```

(The file ends with the body's own newline plus the encoding newline of rule 7.) `git diff` of the commit that deleted `#40` shows, in `…9e40.moi`, the header collapsing to a tombstone whose remaining lines are its historical out-edges (had `--replaced-by` been omitted, it would also keep `edge blocks -> …9e77 flagged`, and `#12` would stay out of `ready` after any import), and in `…9e52.moi` one line added, `edge blocks -> …9e77` (the re-pointed `#52 blocks #12` lives in the file of its source); `…9e77.moi` above does not change. Nothing else changes: in-edges are derived. Uids are random; the example ends the uids of `#9`, `#40`, `#51` and `#52` in their numbers for readability only, and every other suffix, `#12`'s own included, is arbitrary. **Grammar (CL1)**: the M0 format specification includes an ABNF for `.moi` and golden fixtures for every node kind, a tombstone with flagged and historical edges, a scalar conflict, a body conflict, ledger lines and block strings; the encoder, the decoder and the fuzzer are generated from the same grammar.

#### 5b.3 Format marker `.moirai-image`

```
moirai-image 1
object-format: sha1
schema-version: 3
```

Only these three lines are hashed content: the format version and the schema version are moirai data, and the object format is the destination's algorithm, which I28′ already lets differ. `store-id` and `granularity` — one store-local, one destination-local — live in the unhashed side ref `refs/moirai/meta/<store-id>` (§5b.1, CB2); an importer records that ref's `store-id` as `origin` and learns the granularity of each commit from its `Moirai-Kind` trailer, so no global granularity marker is needed. Two stores holding the same commit therefore write the same root tree, and the two-store property test asserts it.

#### 5b.4 Commit mapping (moirai commit → git commit)

| git field | value |
|---|---|
| `tree` | the image tree at that commit; unchanged subtrees keep their ids |
| `parent` × n | git ids of the moirai parents through `gitmap`; merges **and syncs** have two parents (first = dst or lane tip, second = src tip or `sync_base`); revert/cherry-pick results are ordinary commits with trailers naming the origin |
| `author` | name `moirai/<actor>`, email `<role>@moirai.invalid`, time = `hlc >> 16` ms → seconds, tz `+0000` |
| `committer` | identical to `author` (deterministic; the exporter's identity or wall clock would change the hash on re-export) |
| message | the moirai message (normalised as §4.6 item 6), a blank line, then the trailer block in the fixed order given below; the importer strips the final paragraph starting at its first `Moirai-Commit:` line (`Moirai-Kind:` for checkpoint commits) and takes everything before the separating blank line as the message; **no `Moirai-Seq`** (N4) |

**Canonical field → carrier (CB1, I38′).** Every field hashed into `commit_id` (§4.6) has exactly one place in the git commit; the importer reads these and nothing else to rebuild the canonical form.

| §4.6 field | Carrier in the git commit | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 1 `kind` | `Moirai-Kind: ordinary\|merge\|sync\|revert\|cherry-pick` | checkpoint commits carry `Moirai-Kind: checkpoint` and **no** `Moirai-Commit` — they have no native id (N13a) |
| 2 parents' stated ids | the git parents, each mapped to its own `Moirai-Commit` trailer (`parent_actual` recorded locally when a parent was demoted) | order preserved: first = dst/lane tip, second = src tip or `sync_base`; the second parent of a sync is repeated as `Moirai-Sync-Base: c…` for readers |
| 3 `hlc` | **`Moirai-Hlc: <u64 decimal>`** | the author/committer time is `hlc >> 16` in seconds, which is *not* enough to rebuild the hash; the trailer is authoritative |
| 4 actor, role, session | `Moirai-Actor`, `Moirai-Role`, `Moirai-Session` | omitted when empty; author name/email are derived from them and never read back |
| 5 git provenance | `Moirai-Git-Head: <algo>:<hex>`, `Moirai-Git-Branch`, `Moirai-Worktree`, **`Moirai-Git-Base: <algo>:<hex>`** | each omitted when empty |
| 6 message | the paragraph(s) before the trailer block | byte-exact after the write-time normalisation of §4.6 |
| 7 schema version | `Moirai-Schema: <n>` | |
| 8 origin (revert/cherry-pick) | `Moirai-Origin: c…` | |
| 9 `foreign_git` | `Moirai-Foreign-Git: <algo>:<oid>` | only on commits created by foreign or checkpoint import; when such a commit is re-exported to a *different* destination it is written natively with this trailer |
| 10 net changeset | the tree diff against the first parent's tree, parsed through the `.moi` codec (§5b.6 step 2) | `Moirai-Ops: <n>` is the entry count, a cheap pre-check; conflict values come from `conflict` lines; `Resolve{choice}` ops need no carrier because the canonical form records the resulting value, not the choice |
| not hashed | `Moirai-Ref: <landing ref>` (informational: tells an importer the ref kind and lets `image show` name the branch), `Moirai-Idem: <hex>` (informational) | changing either never demotes a commit; a re-export after an `import/<ref>` staging keeps the same oid, so `gitmap` stays truthful |
| never exported | `Violation` ops, before-images, `#N`, seq/lsn, absorbed vectors, markers, leases, R5's unhashed commit-header fields (F10, F14, F16) and `ALLOC`, R4's resolution and evidence tables (I-F4) | staging refs (`merge/*`, `import/*`, `orphans/*`) are never selectable for export |
| R4 and R5 | no carrier of their own: link provenance (`relink`), directory-move history (`path_moves`) and named queries are part of the tree diff (item 10), so the one reconstruction rule covers them for every commit kind, checkpoint commits included [41 B4] | — |

Trailer order: `Moirai-Commit`, `Moirai-Kind`, `Moirai-Ref`, `Moirai-Hlc`, `Moirai-Actor`, `Moirai-Role`, `Moirai-Session`, `Moirai-Git-Head`, `Moirai-Git-Branch`, `Moirai-Worktree`, `Moirai-Git-Base`, `Moirai-Origin`, `Moirai-Sync-Base`, `Moirai-Foreign-Git`, `Moirai-Schema`, `Moirai-Ops`, `Moirai-Idem`; checkpoint commits carry `Moirai-Kind: checkpoint`, `Moirai-Head: <head moirai commit id>`, `Moirai-Ref` and `Moirai-Folded: <n> commits from c… to c…`. **Sync commits (CM2)** are exported as two-parent git commits (lane tip, `sync_base`), which is why `commit` granularity covers every commit reachable from the selected refs through both parents (the `main` commits a lane referenced are exported with the lane); their canonical op list is the full state diff against the first parent (§4.6), so the importer reconstructs them with the same rule as every other kind. **Foreign commits** get deterministic ids: kind `ordinary` (or `merge` for two parents), `hlc = max(committer_time << 16, max(parent.hlc) + 1)`, actor `git:<author email>`, the git message, `foreign_git` = the oid, and the reconstructed net changeset — a pure function of the git commit and the parents' ids, so two stores importing the same foreign commit agree (I28′). **Import-checkpoint commits** likewise: kind `checkpoint` in the local record, parents = the previous checkpoint's id, `foreign_git` = the checkpoint's oid, actor `image:checkpoint`.

Refs: `main` → `refs/moirai/heads/main`, `lane/x` → `refs/moirai/heads/lane/x`, `tags/v` → `refs/moirai/tags/v` (lightweight; `--annotated` uses the author rule for the tagger). In a **separate image repo** the prefixes are `refs/heads/` and `refs/tags/`, so an ordinary `git clone` fetches everything. In a **project repo** the `refs/moirai/` namespace keeps the image out of `git branch` and requires the fetch refspec `+refs/moirai/*:refs/moirai/*` (documented; `doctor image` checks every clone config it can read and warns about `push --mirror` from clones that lack it — Dolt's `refs/dolt/data` caveat [08 §7.1]). Optional `refs/moirai/ops/<store-id>` (`--with-oplog`, in the release): `RefUpdate`/`ClientHead` events for reproducing reflog history elsewhere.

#### 5b.5 Determinism rules

1. Tree entries sorted by git's rule (bytewise, directories compared as `name/`); modes `100644`/`040000`; no executable bits, no symlinks.
2. Object format = the destination repo's `extensions.objectFormat`; never mixed; `gitmap` records the algorithm and destination.
3. Every blob byte is produced by §5b.2; the exporter re-parses each file it writes and asserts equality.
4. Commit objects follow §5b.4; no `gpgsig`, no `encoding`, no non-standard headers (jj's `jj:trees` header is exactly what tooling drops [D §12 S1]).
5. `gitmap` is derivable: walking the image and reading `Moirai-Commit` trailers rebuilds it (`image doctor --rebuild-map`), reporting commits whose recomputed canonical hash ≠ trailer.
6. **Every past `.moi` encoder is retained**; an image records the format version each destination uses, and re-exporting an old commit uses that version's encoder (O5). A format bump is a new destination, never a rewrite of an existing one.
7. Hashed content contains no store-local datum: no `#N`, no `seq`, no lsn, no store id, no granularity (I28′; the `.moirai-image` marker holds only the format version, the object format and the schema version, CB2); named-query text is stored in portable form (`#N` → `#u:<uid>`, sequence numbers and commit prefixes → full commit ids, reflog forms refused, [50 §4.4]); R4 identity derivation reads no machine-local input (exact path bytes, never folded; predecessors ordered by (generation, commit id), [40 §2.3]). Property test: two stores that imported the same bundle export byte-identical objects, root tree included.
8. Every commit kind is reconstructable from its trailers plus the tree diff against its first parent (I38′); the M5 "reconstruct and re-hash" unit test (its fixtures are the M0 gate-0 carrier table, [60 §2.5]) runs over every kind — ordinary, merge, sync, revert, cherry-pick, foreign, import-checkpoint — before any round-trip corpus (CB1, CM2).

#### 5b.6 Export and import

**Object I/O.** One in-process git object layer (M4, [60]) reads and writes loose objects, pack v2 + idx v2 (reading OFS/REF deltas), commit-graph files incl. split chains, loose refs and `packed-refs` through git's `<ref>.lock` protocol with read-after-write verification (G21), and bundles, in SHA-1 and SHA-256; reftable repositories are read, and ref updates in a reftable destination are delegated to `git update-ref --stdin` when git is present and refused otherwise (G22). Export writes packs above the M0 loose/pack threshold (G23), closing a pack every `store.pack-objects-max` (65,536) objects so idx staging stays bounded (full export ≤ 2 / 6 / 10 MB private at 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6, [71 RAM-m3]); `image gc` repacks. The object layer keeps a byte-bounded delta-base LRU (`git.delta-cache-bytes`: 256 KiB in CLI and hooks, 1 MiB in the MCP server), streams objects larger than it, processes E6 windows commit by commit and maps pack, idx and commit-graph files read-only ([71 RAM-m1]). There is no backend trait and no second implementation: the git CLI (`git fsck`, `cat-file`, `rev-list`, `verify-pack`, `merge-base`) is the layer's independent test oracle, and `git` is spawned for network transport only (`image push/pull`), printing the exact command when git is absent. The semantic layers (canonical `.moi` codec, tree diffing at the path level, trailers, verification, validation, `gitmap`, staging) are hand-written on top.

**Export** (`moirai image export [--to <gitdir|bundle>] [--refs 'main,tags/*,lane/*'] [--granularity checkpoint|commit] [--object-format sha1|sha256] [--create] [--if-older <duration>]`; `--if-older` exports only when the last checkpoint export of the selected refs is older than the duration, default `image.export.max-age`, and is otherwise a no-op, §2.15):

1. Open the destination (recognised by `HEAD` + `objects/` + `refs/`; `--create` writes a bare skeleton with `objectFormat` and `refStorage = files` explicit, plus `gc.auto = 0`, `gc.autoPackLimit = 0`, `pack.threads = 2`, `pack.windowMemory = 64m`, and `info/attributes` with `*.moi -text diff` — never a tracked `.gitattributes` inside the image tree, CL1).
2. Frontier: first verify that the newest `gitmap` entry per (destination, algorithm, ref) names an object present in the destination and re-emit from the previous entry if not, so a bugcheck that lost a pack after its `gitmap` commit can never leave the image silently broken ([72 M9]); then, for each selected ref, walk moirai commits from the tip down to one already in `gitmap` for this (destination, algorithm) — `image_cursor` gives the starting seq; at `commit` granularity the walk follows **both** parents, so the `main` commits a lane's `sync` references are exported with the lane; topologically order the new commits. Staging refs (`merge/*`, `import/*`, `orphans/*`) are never selectable.
3. Per commit (`commit` granularity) or per export run and ref (`checkpoint`: one commit for `main` and one per selected lane, parent = that ref's previous checkpoint): build the tree by applying the net changeset (for a `sync`, the expanded window plus residue) to the exported tree of the first parent, reading only root + touched mid + touched leaf trees from the destination (lazy, G24); encode touched `.moi` files from the branch view at that commit (as-of, O(edits) per touched node); stream to the backend; record `(commit_id, dest, algo, oid)`.
4. Update refs through the git object layer: in a moirai-owned bare image repository, **every ref of the run — the side ref `refs/moirai/meta/<store-id>` (`meta.moi`, `aliases.moi`) included — in one `packed-refs` transaction** (one lock file, one rename, loose refs deleted, [70 S11]); in a live project repository git's `<ref>.lock` protocol per ref with read-after-write verification (G21); for a reftable destination `git update-ref --stdin` when git is present, otherwise refused (G22); each with a CAS against the last-seen oid for that ref on that destination; mismatch → exit 6 with `moirai image import` as the remedy.
5. **Durability order** ([72 M9]; the `Vfs` classes of [80 §2.3.2], the same on every OS): pack and idx data flushed (`durable+meta`) → moved into `objects/pack/` by `rename_noreplace` → `durable-name` on both parents → the `packed-refs.lock` (or `<ref>.lock`) written and flushed → `rename_replace` onto `packed-refs` (or the loose ref; git's lock protocol) → `durable-name` → only then the `gitmap` records appended under the writer byte in one durable commit. The per-OS calls are those of [80 §2.3.2]; `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` is also used on Windows until the rig calibration (§8.2 item 17, deferred to after the release by the owner review of 2026-09-27) shows it unnecessary. A crash anywhere leaves the destination at most one step ahead of `gitmap`, which the next export detects and re-maps (idempotent: object ids are content-addressed), never behind it. An incremental checkpoint export of `main` + 2 lanes costs ≤ 50 ms against the measured rename floor ([70 S11], M0 item 8).
6. Exports do not hold the maintenance byte; they read sealed segments and the log and rely on pins plus the 60 s delete-pending grace (G26). Refused in quiet mode unless `--force`.

**Import** (`moirai image import [--from <gitdir|bundle>] [--refs …]`):

1. Read refs and objects through the git object layer; walk commits from each ref tip until an oid in `gitmap`; order topologically; record each destination ref's oid as "last seen" (N13b).
2. **Native** commit (`Moirai-Commit` trailer present, unknown to the store): diff the tree against the tree of its **first parent** — the one rule for every kind, including `sync` (CM2) → parse touched `.moi` files → the net changeset (absent→live `Create` + fields + edges; changed lines `SetField`/`SetStatus`/`AddEdge`/`RemoveEdge`/`Move`/`SetBody`; `incr` lines added since the parent → `Incr` with the summed delta; `conflict` lines → conflict values; tombstone edge lines → flagged or historical edges (I39′); live→tombstone `Delete`; **tombstone→live `Undelete`**; live→absent foreign `Delete{image:file-removed}`; tombstone→absent `TombstoneRemoved` hint) → rebuild the canonical form from the §5b.4 table (kind, the **parents' stated ids** from their trailers, `Moirai-Hlc`, actor/role/session, the four provenance trailers, the message, schema, origin, `foreign_git`, the changeset) → verify BLAKE3 = `Moirai-Commit`. Match → append with the same id and canonical kind, `import = native`, `verified = 1`; for a `sync` the store keeps the G17 residue (the importer subtracts `main`'s window, which it already holds because the second parent was imported first) with `sync_base` = the second parent. Mismatch → this commit alone is demoted to foreign with a new id and `verified = 0`; its children still verify against their stated parent ids and record `parent_actual` (N5, I29′). Checkpoint commits (`Moirai-Kind: checkpoint`, no `Moirai-Commit`) are imported as `import = checkpoint` with the deterministic id of §5b.4 and their `Moirai-Head`/`Moirai-Folded` origin, never reported as tampered (N13a). Merge and sync records update the local ref's absorbed vector exactly as a local merge would, and the imported ops write their markers (§4.5 step 4), so an imported `status: done` is `settled` everywhere (CB3).
3. **Foreign** commit (no trailer, unknown trailer, hash mismatch): same tree diff → ops; the deterministic id of §5b.4 (`import = foreign`, canonical kind `ordinary` or `merge`, `actor: git:<author email>`, `hlc = max(committer_time << 16, max(parent.hlc) + 1)` (N13c), `foreign_git: <oid>`, message = the git message). **A two-parent foreign commit is imported as a moirai merge computed by the typed 3-way over the two parents' imported states** (base at their LCA); the foreign tree is consulted only to resolve `TextHunk` conflicts the human resolved on the git side (a line present in the foreign file and in exactly one side is taken as that side's resolution); counters come from the ledger union, never from a text merge; any remaining disagreement is a conflict value (I30′, N6).
4. **Validate** every imported commit with the merge validators against its parent view: `ImageParse` (a `.moi` that does not parse, e.g. leftover `<<<<<<<` markers, or a malformed `anchor` line), `SchemaConflict`, `DanglingEdge`, `Cycle`, `HierarchyCycle`, `IdCollision` (a `uid` live with a different `created` commit — not applied to R4's derived-uid kinds, where a `Create` of an existing uid is equal existence plus a field merge; a file, root or anchor uid that does not match its derivation over its stored inputs is accepted as foreign, treated as random and flagged by `image doctor`), `PathClaim` (I-F1), `QueryInvalid`/`QueryCycle` (every imported named query is parsed and bound; a text containing a store-local datum is `ImageParse`); an `anchor` line whose destination uid is unknown is a tombstone reference (`at` is historical). Imported commits **always keep their stated parents** ([72 M10]). Clean and the local tip is an ancestor-or-self of the imported chain → append and move the local ref. Clean but the local ref has moved on since the imported chain's base (the colleague's hand edit of last night's checkpoint while `main` gained local commits) → the chain lands on `import/<ref>` and the importer runs the typed `merge import/<ref> --into <ref>` with its LCA at the last common exported commit: a clean merge lands a local merge commit with its markers and absorbed vector, violations stage as below; `image.import-merge = stage` stages every divergent import instead. Violations → append on `import/<ref>` (staging) and stop the local ref there; `resolve` + `merge --continue` finish it like a local merge. A checkpoint commit that carries a whole store (the first import into a fresh store) is appended as a bulk commit (§4.3), hashed node by node, ≤ 8 / 16 / 32 MB private at 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6 ([71 RAM-B1]).
5. **Ids**: `uid` is identity; an alias hint from the side ref's `aliases.moi` (`refs/moirai/meta/*`) is honoured only if `N ≥ next_id` (then `next_id = N + 1`), otherwise the alias map `(origin store-id, foreign #N) → local #N` records the remap and `show` prints `#40 (was #17 in store 6f2a…)` (N11, I35′). **Mentions (CL2)**: on import, `mentions` edges come only from `edge mentions` lines — the write-time `#N` re-parse never runs on imported bodies, because a literal `#N` in foreign prose numbers the *origin* store's nodes; bodies imported from another store are marked `origin:<store-id>` and rendered with `#N` remapped through the alias map (`#40 (was #17 in store 6f2a…)`). Importing another store's image is in the release and this rule is part of the M5 corpus; live cross-machine writers stay out of scope (owner decision #11).
6. `gitmap` gets `(commit_id, dest, algo, oid)` for native and foreign commits alike, so the next export of a foreign commit is a no-op and the round trip closes.

Both directions take the writer byte only to append commits, so agents keep working during a 1e6-node export.

#### 5b.7 Round-trip guarantees

| Data | Lossless? | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| nodes, fields, sets, labels, counters (as ledgers), bodies (byte-exact, I40′), edges with props, flagged dangling edges and the out-edges of tombstones (I39′), tombstones (reason, replaced-by), conflict values, schema; R4 link intent (file nodes with their observation composite, aliases, removal intent, anchors, `path_moves`, derived uids — which recompute identically from their stored inputs); R5 named queries | **yes**, at every granularity for link intent (`path_moves` is head state, so a checkpoint export carries the full directory-move history) | `.moi` canonical text; tombstones are files that keep their retained out-edges |
| R4 resolution state (per-tree link states, file ids, stat caches, proposals, fingerprints) | **no, by design** (I-F4) | the importing store resolves against its own trees and rebuilds fingerprints from readable content |
| commit DAG (parents, merges, sync bases, revert/cherry-pick origins) and commit metadata (actor, role, session, hlc, message, project-git provenance incl. base, foreign origin, idempotency key hash, ops count) | **yes** at `commit` granularity, for every kind including `sync` (CM2) | trailers + parents (§5b.4 table); `Moirai-Commit` verifies the canonical hash (I38′) |
| violations, `Resolve` choices | **no, by design** | violations live only on staging refs, which are never exported; a resolution is exported as the value it produced |
| moirai commit ids | **yes** (`verified`) | recomputed from the canonical form |
| branch/tag names and kinds | **yes** | ref names; kind in `refs/heads.moi` (checkpoint) or the `Moirai-Ref` trailer |
| `#N` numbers | **best effort** | unhashed alias ref; alias map on collision |
| reflog / op log / client HEADs | only with `--with-oplog` | `refs/moirai/ops/<store-id>` |
| leases, `settled`/`deleted`/`cleared` markers, absorbed vectors, idempotency results, cursors, `next_id`, fencing tokens, pins, segments, lsn/seq | **no, by design** | runtime state (I36′); re-established on the importing store (markers and absorbed vectors are re-derived from the imported ops and merges) |
| derived state | recomputed | never serialized |
| intermediate commits between checkpoints | **no** at `checkpoint` granularity | folded; the head state is exact, the path to it is not |
| git object ids across object formats | different by construction | `gitmap` keeps both |
| store file bytes | no | the image is logical |

**Gates (M5 exit).** Gate 0, before any corpus: for every commit kind — ordinary, merge, `sync`, revert, cherry-pick, foreign, import-checkpoint — export one commit, reconstruct its canonical form from trailers + tree diff, and assert hash equality (CB1, CM2). Gate 1, `commit` granularity: `export(store) → fresh import → export` **byte-identical** (objects and refs) for 1e5 nodes and 1e5 commits (SHA-1 and SHA-256), with lanes that synced, tombstones without `--replaced-by`, bodies with hard breaks, CRLF at write time and no final LF, conflicted nodes, file nodes, anchors, `path_moves`, dual-creation re-keys and named queries in the corpus. Gate 2, `checkpoint` granularity (the default): **state-identical** — after `export → fresh import`, every exported ref's head state (its `.moi` set) equals the original's, the dependents of flagged blockers are not `ready`, and a re-export of the fresh store to a new destination reproduces every head tree byte-identically (commit objects differ only in the import-checkpoint ids and parents, which are themselves deterministic). Gate 3: `import(export(St1)) ⊕ import(export(St2))` on a third store equals `merge` of the two; two stores importing the same bundle export identical objects including the root tree (CB2), derive the same file and anchor identities and bind every named query to the same uids with the same canonical-AST hash, and a merge after a checkpoint-granularity round trip composes directory moves identically in both stores; the encoder/decoder fuzzed over random op sequences including `revert`, `cherry-pick`, `undo`, block strings, `---` inside bodies, CRLF at write time and bodies without a final newline.

#### 5b.8 Destinations, granularity, object format, failure modes

| Destination | Refs | Cloned by default? | Working tree touched? | PR-reviewable? | Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **separate bare repo** `<parent of the main worktree>/<project>-moirai.git` (default) | `refs/heads/*`, `refs/tags/*` | yes | no | yes (branch view) | isolates history size from the project repo; pushed to a private remote by the owner |
| project repo, `refs/moirai/*` (not built: excluded by decision until #16 lists the project remote or #11 changes; naming reserved, [74 A17]) | `refs/moirai/heads/*` | no (refspec) | no | via `git log refs/moirai/heads/main` | backup + cross-machine sync riding the project remote; checkpoint granularity only |
| project repo, orphan branch `moirai/image` (not built, [60 §1.3]) | `refs/heads/moirai/image` | yes | no | yes | when the image must be visible in every clone |
| tracked directory `docs/moirai/` (not built, [60 §1.3]) | none | yes | yes | **yes** | checkpoint granularity only; git-side merges expected and imported as foreign merges |

Defaults: refs `main` + `tags/*` + `lane/*`; granularity `checkpoint` (for `main`: one per merge into `main`, one per day and on explicit export; for each live lane: one per day and on explicit export, parent = the lane's previous checkpoint — the off-store copy of unmerged work, CM8, one small commit per lane per day); `commit` granularity opt-in per destination (it then exports every commit reachable through both parents, so `sync` commits carry their `main` parent). SHA-1 for anything that leaves the machine (the hash-function transition document still discourages SHA-256 on public servers [D §12 S4]); SHA-256 allowed for local-only repos; `doctor image` warns when a SHA-256 image is pushed to a remote that has not advertised support.

| Failure | Answer |
|---|---|
| hand edit of a `.moi` on the git side | foreign commit, validated; a parse error stages `ImageParse` with the path |
| `git merge` of two exported branches on the git side | imported as a typed moirai merge over the parents' states (I30′); leftover markers stage `ImageParse` |
| file deleted on the git side | foreign `Delete{image:file-removed}` with a tombstone; a real moirai delete always writes a tombstone file, so an absent file is always a foreign act |
| history rewritten on the git side (rebase/squash/filter-repo) | trailers survive (message trailers, unlike jj headers); commits whose tree equals the recomputed tree are re-mapped in `gitmap`; others are foreign; the exporter's CAS refuses to move a ref it does not recognise until an import has run |
| `push --mirror` from a clone without `refs/moirai/*` | `doctor image` warns; the store is canonical, a fresh export restores the refs; the separate-repo default avoids it |
| image repo `gc` prunes unreferenced objects after a partial export | the exporter re-emits them (idempotent) |
| two stores export to one destination | the second's ref CAS fails; it imports first (native ids verified), then exports (Dolt's fetch-then-`--force-with-lease` loop) |
| object-format mismatch | each format is a distinct destination with its own `gitmap` column; never mixed |
| CRLF / autocrlf on a tracked directory | `info/attributes` (bare repo) or a `.gitattributes` *outside* the image subtree (tracked directory) with `*.moi -text diff`; the importer normalises CRLF in headers and bodies exactly as the store does at write time; the exporter always writes LF |
| reftable repository | the git object layer reads reftable; ref updates in a reftable destination are delegated to `git update-ref --stdin` when git is present and refused otherwise (G22); `image export --create` writes `refStorage = files` |
| AV filter driver rolls back a loose-ref write (git-for-windows #6396 class) | read-after-write verification with one retry; `image doctor` compares `gitmap` against refs (G21) |
| `git gc` during a quiet window | never automatic (config written by moirai); `moirai image gc` refuses in quiet mode (G23) |

#### 5b.9 Cost (est.; SHA-256 1.94 GB/s [M], in-process deflate on one core; inputs [20 §1.7])

| Quantity | 1e4 | 1e5 | 1e6 |
|---|---|---|---|
| full export objects | ~1e4 blobs + ~9k trees | ~1e5 + ~66k | ~1e6 + ~66k |
| full export raw / packed | 5–6 / ~2 MB | ~58 / 20–25 MB | ~565 / 200–250 MB |
| full export time (one pack) | 0.1–0.3 s | 1–2.5 s | 10–25 s |
| full export private RSS (explicit command) | ≤ 2 MB | ≤ 6 MB | ≤ 10 MB (packs closed every 65,536 objects) |
| incremental checkpoint export, `main` + 2 lanes | ~32 KB raw / ~17 KB packed per changed checkpoint; one pack + idx by temp + no-replace rename and `durable-name` ([80 §2.3.2]), one `packed-refs` transaction, directory barriers, the `gitmap` commit ≈ 20–50 ms (est. from rename p50 3.9–9.5 ms [M, 13 §1.4]; confirmed by M0 item 8), ≤ 1 MB private | same | same |
| checkpoint per merge + daily, 1e5 nodes | ~4 MB/day changed files → ~1.5 GB/year undeltified, ~0.2 GB after repack | | |
| `commit` granularity, 365k commits/year | ~6 GB undeltified (SHA-1), ~0.3–0.6 GB after repack | | |
| full import | 0.3–1 s; ≤ 8 MB | 2–7 s; ≤ 16 MB | 25–70 s (+ a final rollup); ≤ 32 MB |
| incremental import, one commit | 5–20 ms; ≤ 4 MB | same | same |
| `gitmap` | 41 B per commit per (destination, algorithm) | | |
| R4 link intent in a full export | ~0.45 KB per file node + ~0.52 KB per anchor line; the owner's scale (≈ 2.2k cited files, 10–35k citations) ≈ 1 + 18 MB raw [40 §7.3] | | |

### 5c. Git independence (R2)

Discovery, location, binding and provenance are fixed in §2.14; this section states what runs without git and what never does.
