**Runs with no `git` binary, no git library and outside any repository**: `init`, every read and write verb, branches, merge, sync, tags, revert, cherry-pick, undo, reflog, GC, doctor, hooks, the MCP server, packs and briefs, `image export`/`image import` to and from a local directory or bundle, `check`/`stale` (ancestry in-process through the git object layer, CM7), every LQ query and `TX`, and file links (without git there is no tree gate and no git rename evidence: a missing file always runs the evidence cascade and is never treated as deleted, §5e.7). Provenance fields are empty when no `.git` is discoverable; `stale` degrades to "unknown" and says so.

**Reads git *files* in-process, never spawns**: the discovery hint (`.git` file/dir → `commondir`), provenance (`HEAD`, `refs/heads/*`, `packed-refs`, ~60 lines read textually, or reftable through the git object layer), and ancestry for `stale`/`check` from commit-graph generation numbers when the graph contains both commits [04 §3.1], else a generation-pruned commit walk through the git object layer — cached as lazy facts in the log so every process shares the answers (C's cache); and, for R4's tree gate and evidence, HEAD-tree lookups by path, per-commit exact renames on the first-parent chain and ancestry of heads newer than the commit-graph (settles cache them as `GITFACTS`; reads compute them for the command only, §5e.3).

**Spawns `git` only when present and only in four explicit, optional places**: network transport of the image (`image push/pull`), printing the exact command otherwise; ref updates in a reftable image destination (G22); `doctor lanes --refresh-graph`; and `moirai file mv --git` (R4, only on request; `file mv` never touches the git index otherwise). None is on a pack, brief, ready, blocking, get, claim, check, stale, query, link-resolution or hook path, and none is in the core crate. **Gate** (GT20, §8.3): a spawn lint admits a `git` spawn at exactly these four call sites and no other process spawn except the detached `moirai gc` child (§4.9); a `Cargo.lock` lint forbids every git library and embedded-database crate; the GT2 and GT4 streams also run with `git` removed from `PATH` and no `.git` present, where `stale` answers "unknown" and every other verb behaves as above.

**Branch independence**: moirai refs have their own namespace and lifecycle; nothing is created, switched or deleted because git did something. Conveniences, all explicit: `lane open <name> --worktree <dir> [--git-branch <b>] [--base <sha>]` (= `branch lane/<name>` + `worktree bind` + a `lane` node recording the git facts), `worktree bind|unbind|--list`, `hook git-post-merge` (runs `merge-check` for the bound lane and offers `moirai merge`), `hook git-post-checkout` (refreshes the binding's provenance), `doctor lanes` (bindings whose directories are gone; worktrees with no lane). One store at `<git-common-dir>/moirai/` serves all of the owner's worktrees (44 `git worktree list` entries in [13 §1.3], 46 a day later in [41 §1]) through the hint with no pointer files (§2.14 placement, CM6); directories outside any repository use pointer files.

### 5d. Coordination under branching and the "node 40" story

#### 5d.1 Versioned vs runtime state

| State | Versioned per branch | Store-level runtime | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| nodes; all typed fields incl. `status`/`done`/`resolution`/`assignee`/`phase_state`; all edges incl. `blocks`/`gates`/`parent`; bodies; schema; conflict values; `rev_seq` | ✔ | | merge rules §5a.7; `reopen` vs `done` at merge = `StatusFork`, never silent |
| refs, tags, reflog, client heads | (version pointers) | ✔ | exported with the image (`--with-oplog` for heads/reflog) |
| **lease** `{#N, lease id, kind, role, holder, anchor, token, expires, run, branch, bound, root session, files_owned}` | | ✔ | "an agent is working on this node now" (a task lease) or "this holder acts in this role" (a role lease, `#N` = 0, [90 §4.3]): keyed by `(#N, lease id)`, visible from every branch; liveness by the holder anchor, never by the pid of the process that wrote it (§6.2, [72 B2]); the leased task's `files_owned` globs are captured at `claim` and at any `set files_owned` under the lease, so every pack's do-not-touch list is read from `LEASES` alone ([70 S5]); `claim` requires live + ready **on the claimer's branch** and records it; `complete`/`set --lease` write on the lease's branch (`--move-lease <ref>` explicit, warns); `release`/`reclaim`/expiry store-level; a lease on a branch that is deleted is released with a triage note; `rm` refuses under any live lease unless `--release` |
| **`settled`** `{#N, ref_id, ref_seq, commit, holder, outcome, hlc, seq}` | | ✔ | written by the op applier for every `SetStatus{→ done\|cancelled}` on a task, on whatever branch and by whatever path (CB3); on every branch R that has not absorbed it (`absorbed_R[ref_id] < ref_seq`), `ready`/`claim`/`blocking`/`brief` treat `#N` as `done on <branch> (c…, unmerged)`: not claimable, not listed as a live blocker, rendered with the branch; **absorbed** (inert for R) once R's vector covers it — its own branch immediately, `main` at the merge, other lanes at their next `sync`; **superseded** by a `Marker{cleared}` scoped to `(#N, ref_id)` written by `reopen` or `Undelete`, and **recomputed** — cleared, re-emitted, or re-attributed to a live fork that contains the commit — by `undo`, `op restore` and `branch -D` (each with a triage line); the markers are a cache of I26′'s state definition, which the reference model evaluates directly ([72 M4]) |
| **`deleted`** `{#N, ref_id, ref_seq, commit, seq}` | | ✔ | written by the op applier for every `Delete` op; same treatment: no branch dispatches or lists as a live blocker a node another live branch deleted before it has absorbed the delete |
| **absorbed vectors** per ref: `absorbed_R[ref_id] =` the highest `ref_seq` of that ref reachable from `tip(R)` | | ✔ | maintained in the ref table (folded `REFS` + tail records): a commit on R sets `absorbed_R[R]` to its `ref_seq`; a fork of Y from X at `ref_seq f` copies X's vector and sets `absorbed_Y[X] = f`; a merge or sync of src into dst sets `absorbed_dst[src] = ref_seq(tip src)` and every other entry to the max of both sides (so a marker absorbed by `main` reaches every lane at its next `sync`, transitively); `undo` restores the vector recorded on the newest merge/sync/fork record at or below the restored tip and sets `absorbed_R[R]` to the restored tip's `ref_seq`. Lookup O(1); ≤ 6 B × live refs per ref; the same numbers give `ahead/behind main` without a DAG walk (CM1) |
| fencing tokens `HEAD.fence` | | ✔ | monotonic store-wide; expiry never bumps the token; same-holder renewal of an expired unreclaimed lease (I17′) |
| idempotency results | | ✔ | key + payload hash + **branch**; replay on another branch is exit 9 with the original result, except when the original branch was merged into the caller's or deleted after merge, which returns the original result as success (N13e) |
| change-feed `seq`, per-session cursors | | ✔ | `seq` store-wide; every entry carries `ref`; `changes --since S [--branch R\|--all]` defaults to the caller's branch ∪ `main`; ref moves (merges, undo, branch delete) are entries; per-session cursors live in the MCP server's memory when the hooks run there, else in lazy records appended with a try-lock that skips the advance when the writer byte is busy ([70 S12]) |
| `next_id`, `uid` generation | | ✔ | one allocator; `#N` unique across branches |
| client HEAD bindings, quiet flag, pins, `gitmap`, alias map, ancestry cache | | ✔ | not versioned; `session:<harness>:<id>` heads expire with the idempotency window; R4 bindings add the expected git ref and base commit (§5a.4) |
| R4 resolution and evidence: `TREES`, `FILEOBS`, `PENDING`, `FSINTENT`, `FPRINT`, `JOURNALCUR`, `DIRMAP`, `PREFIXEV`, `GITFACTS` ([40 §2.6]) | | ✔ | "where is this file in this worktree now": keyed by `#N` and tree, never versioned, merged, hashed or exported (I-F4); `FsIntent` records are durable (an interrupted `file mv`/`rm` is recovered, never lost), the rest lazy and derivable; written only by settles, file verbs and evidence hooks, never by reads (I-F5) |
| R5 `ALLOC`: `#N → (uid, ref_id, create_seq)` ([50] F17) and `UIDX`: `uid → #N` | | ✔ | which branch and commit allocated each id, so a query for an id created on another branch says where it lives (notice N06); the uid index makes one `#N` per derived uid hold across unmerged branches (I1, [72 M7]); rebuilt from the log by `repair` |

Why leases and markers are runtime, not history (R1's "justify" clause): a lease merged from a lane would be a claim by a possibly dead process; a lease on `plan/*` is meaningless; and the owner's stated fear is two lanes building the same task [07 §7.4] — a store-wide lease prevents it *while the lease lives*, and the `settled` marker prevents it *after* `complete` releases the lease and before the merge, which is where D's "double work is impossible" claim failed (N2/D1). Both are "who is doing / has done what now", exactly like leases, and are never exported (I36′); an importing store re-derives them from the imported ops. Cost: ~30k markers/year at 40 B, of which only the unabsorbed ones (dozens) stay in `MARKERS`: at every checkpoint fold the globally inert ones move to the cold `MARKERS_OLD`, so no scan is O(history) — `brief_triage` stays ≤ 50 µs with 90k inert and 100 active markers ([70 S4]); the reader-side check is one probe and one vector lookup per candidate, so `ready` with 50 stale unmerged completions costs the same as `ready` with none (a CI workload, §8.3).

#### 5d.2 Task status and `done` across branches and at merge

- `status` is versioned; a task's virtual `done` is `status ∈ {done, cancelled}` (§3.1). A task completed on `lane/x` is `done` on `lane/x`, `settled` everywhere. `main` learns it at `merge lane/x --into main` (lattice `done > in_progress > open`; `cancelled`/`deferred`/`frozen` vs `done` → `StatusFork`). A lane learns `main`'s completions at `sync`.
- `blocks` across branches: `ready(B)` on branch R uses R's view of A's status **and** the markers. If A is done on `lane/x` and B lives on `lane/y`, B is ready on `lane/y` after `lane/x → main → lane/y` (or a direct cross-merge); meanwhile `blockers #B --across` prints `A: done on lane/x (c4470, not merged into your branch)`, and `ready`/`blocking` on `main` never list A as a live blocker or re-dispatch it (I26′). Hooks add a one-line `behind main by N commits (k completions, m rules, j deletions touching your nodes)` notice for the bound branch, printed only when its counts changed since the session's last hook output ([73 F12]).
- `reopen` is an explicit op; merging a reopen against a `done` on the other side is a `StatusFork`.
- Verdict gating (`gates`) is versioned like `blocks`: a critic's `fail_fixable` verdict written on `lane/x` gates completion on `lane/x`; dispatch happens on the lane branch (`ready --branch lane/x`), so the verdict is visible where the work is; the orchestrator sees it from `main` through `--across`.
- Rules and owner rulings written on `main` reach lanes at `sync`; until then every pack shows unmerged critical rules with a `~main` marker (owner rulings are never hidden by branching, §7.4).

#### 5d.3 "Node 40 deleted": within a branch and across branches

Within one branch, one commit (§4.5): the writer walks #40's reverse list (O(degree)); restrict kinds refuse unless `--cascade`/`--reparent`; `blocks`/`gates` out of #40 are re-pointed with `--replaced-by` or flagged (X4); historical in-edges stay and now point at a dead id; `cites`/`derived_from` sources become `suspect`; the row keeps `deleted`; the tombstone `{40, tx, reason, replaced_by}`, the `deleted` marker and the change-feed entry with `affected = [12, 77, 203, …]` are in the same flushed group. L1: every other process reads `HEAD` before its next operation and replays the record (µs) — any read of #12 afterwards shows the flagged blocker, any reference to #40 renders `#40 (deleted c812 by dev#2: "dup of #52" → #52)`. L2: `changes --since` and the hook deltas (`UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStart`) carry the line, relevance-filtered to nodes the agent claimed, is blocked on or cited, capped at 600 B. L3: any write that assumed #40 (`set 40`, `link --blocks 40`, `--if-rev` on #12 with an old rev) fails with exit 3/4 and the current value; a `SubagentStart` pack never includes #40.

| Case | On the deleting branch (`lane/x`) | On other branches before any merge | At merge |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rm 40` on `lane/x`; `main` and `lane/y` reference it | as above | **unaffected** (git-like isolation): #40 is live there, but the `deleted` marker keeps it out of `ready`/`blocking`/`claim` on every branch; `show 40 --across` prints `deleted on lane/x c812 by dev#2 (not merged)`; packs add an advisory line when the caller's branch is behind | `merge lane/x --into main`: `main` only read #40 → delete wins, `main`'s referrers get the same atomic treatment; `main` modified #40 → `DeleteVsModify` conflict value; `main` added a structural edge to #40 → `DanglingEdge` violation, raised by the sync of step 0 (§5a.7) and staged on `merge/lane/x/from/main`, so the whole merge stages, with the suggested `resolve … --take repoint:#52`; historical edges become tombstone refs, their sources `suspect` |
| `rm 40` on `main`; lanes reference it | as above on `main`; every process reading `main` sees the tombstone at its next read | lanes unaffected until `sync`; the hook notice says `behind main: 1 deletion touching your #12`; `blockers 12` on the lane prints `#40 (deleted on main c812; sync to apply)`; the marker keeps #40 out of lane dispatch | `sync` applies the same rules with the lane as dst; a flagged blocker keeps #12 out of `ready` until `resolve` |
| #40 leased by `dev#2` on `lane/y`, deleted on `lane/x` | **refused**: `rm` prints `leased by dev#2 on lane/y (L-19)`; `--release` releases the lease with a triage note attached to #40's tombstone and proceeds (I32′) | — | `DeleteVsModify` if `lane/y` modified #40 |
| #40 deleted on `lane/x`, then `lane/x` deleted unmerged | the deletion never reaches `main` through `lane/x` (a live branch forked from it after the deleting commit still carries it) | `branch -D` re-attributes the `deleted` marker to a live ref that contains the deleting commit and clears it only if none does (§5a.9, [72 M4]); either way it leaves a triage line in `doctor`/`brief`, and its warning names the dropped deletion | — |
| #40's file removed by hand in the git image | — | — | import creates a foreign `Delete{image:file-removed}` on the imported ref; the merge rules above apply when that ref is merged |
| `revert` of the deleting commit | `Undelete #40` with the before-image; re-pointed edges restored if their targets still exist, else `NotFound` staged | — | the image sees a tombstone→live transition (`Undelete`) |

Engine-level guarantee on every branch head at every commit: no structural edge points at a dead node except a flagged blocker that keeps its dependent out of `ready`; every referrer of a tombstone renders it; every derived counter equals a recompute (I2, I12, I9). What "maximally synchronous" means under R1: a delete on one branch never *mutates* another branch, but every branch that receives it applies it atomically, every other branch can see it on request, and no branch can dispatch or list as a live blocker a node another live branch has deleted or completed.

### 5e. Move-proof file links (R4)

R4 (owner, 2026-09-26): a node can reference a project file as a whole or a place inside it — a line span, a symbol, a Markdown section, a quoted passage — and the reference survives renames, moves, directory moves, case-only renames and edits, whoever makes them (moirai, the owner in Explorer or an IDE, an agent's `mv`, `Move-Item`, `os.rename`, `Edit`/`Write`, or git's `checkout`, `switch`, `merge`, `rebase`, `stash`, `reset`, `apply`, `cherry-pick`); when a referenced file is deleted every referrer knows. This section is the integrated summary; [40] (revision 2, which answers every finding of its review [41]) is normative for every rule, threshold, pattern list and fixture, and wins wherever this summary is silent.

#### 5e.1 Mechanism and guarantee

- **Hybrid, because explicit verbs alone cannot work.** Explicit file verbs record intent precisely; lazy, deterministic re-binding catches everything else. Agents made 148 raw `mv` calls against 7 `git mv` and 63 Python renames, and tree-rewriting git operations (`apply` ×189, `merge` ×85, `stash` ×43, …) outnumber explicit moves about 3:1 [M, 13 §1.1–§1.2]. No watcher, daemon, timer or polling (DR3): `ReadDirectoryChangesW` is lossy [08 §4 W10] and a watcher holding subdirectory handles makes renames of ancestor directories fail with error 5 [M, 13 §1.4].
- **Reads compute, settle points write** (P1). `show`, `pack`, `brief`, `get`, `find`, `q`, `links check` and MCP reads resolve link states live and append nothing to the log (I-F5): a moved or vanished file is visible to every referrer at its next read — the "node 40" rule for files. Versioned re-binds are written only at settle points (§5e.3), after a ≥ 50 ms quiescence re-check that drops a re-bind whose source path came back (atomic saves rename the original away for milliseconds).
- **Only exact evidence re-binds automatically** (P2, I-F13): a single exact candidate, ties refused; equal content is not exact by itself; absence is never deletion (P3, I-F7); a path reused by unrelated content is `replaced`, never silently `ok`.
- **The guarantee** [40 §0.2]: moves and deletions made through moirai in a bound worktree land in one commit that every referrer sees, with the deletion intent (`reason`, `replaced_by`) recorded; moves made by anything else show correctly at the next read and are recorded at the next settle in the branch's writer tree; **no link ever points silently at the wrong file or the wrong text** — every link that is not `ok` carries a state and a one-line next step, and a guess accepted by an agent stays marked until another role confirms it; a worktree that has not received a move says so.

#### 5e.2 Data model

- **Two layers** [40 §2.1]. *Link intent* — file nodes, root nodes with their `path_moves`, `at` edges with anchors, removal intent — is versioned per branch, merged and exported. *Resolution and evidence* — per-tree states, tagged OS file ids and parent-directory ids with their volume keys ([80 §2.11.2]), the stat quadruple, the latest observed `oid`, proposals, pending observations, file-operation intents, fingerprints, journal cursors, the directory map, git-derived facts — is store-level runtime (§5d.1), never versioned, merged, hashed or exported (I-F4). Git versions the files; moirai versions the links [13 §2.1].
- **The file node** is the widened `artifact` kind (§3.2; the kind count stays 13): immutable identity inputs `origin_path` and `origin_pred`; `root`; the **observation composite** `path`, `oid`, `bytes`, `observed_git`, `observed_blob`, `relink` (one merge key: a move changes path, provenance and usually content together); `aliases` (former paths; they resolve old mentions and never capture a new file); status `planned < present`, side `removed` with `reason`/`replaced_by`.
- **Derived identity** [40 §2.3]: `uid = BLAKE3-128(lp("moirai-file-v1") ‖ lp(root) ‖ lp(origin_path) ‖ lp(origin_pred))`, every field length-prefixed, over the exact path bytes as git's HEAD tree spells them (else as the directory enumeration returns them), never case-folded; the predecessor is, among the registering view's nodes that held that path (removed there, or moved away and aliased), the one with the greatest uid — a value of the view's versioned state at every image granularity — and a derived uid that already names a node of that view (live elsewhere, removed or a tombstone) is re-derived with it as the predecessor, so the uid is a function of the view alone and every store derives the same one (A1 re-review S-01, which replaced revision 2's order by (generation, commit id) and its store-wide dead set). Dead uids are never re-created (I-F14). Two lanes that link the same file create the same node, and a known uid reuses its `#N` (I1). The stored inputs make every uid verifiable (`doctor --verify`, image import). Root nodes derive their uid from the root name; anchor uids from a stored, length-prefixed capture digest over every selector capture used for uniqueness, plus a stored predecessor term when that uid is taken on (src, dst), so a repin never changes them and two captures never share one (S-03).
- **Content identity** [40 §2.5]: `oid` is a git-blob-shaped hash over the bytes normalised by git's own `convert_is_binary` statistics (CRLF → LF for text), SHA-1 or SHA-256 per root, fixed at `init` (the `project` root takes the store repository's object format, every other root SHA-1; `oid`s of different algorithms never compare equal, A1 re-review S-18), read through one handle in two checked passes (A1P-05). It equals git's blob id for normally converted text files, but correctness never assumes that: every git comparison uses git's own blob ids read from git trees (`observed_blob`). A ~300 B fingerprint per content version (bottom-64 line sketch, sizes, weights) is runtime and derivable.
- **Roots and paths** [40 §2.4]: `project` (the worktree), named roots mapped per machine in `config roots.<name>`, and `abs` (a machine-local absolute path in the form of [80 §2.10] P12 — Windows `X:/…` with the drive letter upper-cased, the leading `/` kept on Linux and macOS — existence and `oid` checks only, never compared across machines or OSes); root-relative `/` paths, exact UTF-8 bytes, with no Unicode normalisation except that an untracked name on a normalization-insensitive macOS volume is stored as git records it (NFC, [80 §2.10] P3); `\`, control characters and non-UTF-8 names are refused, and names some supported OS cannot hold are refused at creation (P4, P5); walks are relative, with `\\?\` for long paths (P10). Case and normalization equivalence are properties of a directory or volume, decided at resolve time: paths that another OS holds as distinct files but this directory merges (twins) resolve only by content, the others `missing (not representable on this OS)` ([80 §2.11.4] rule 2); outside twin sets a case-only or normalization-only difference on disk is `ok (spelling differs on disk)` and is recorded only when a writer tree's committed HEAD spells the new case or through `file mv` (git keeps the old spelling under `core.ignorecase=true` [M, 41 §1]). Session scratchpads are refused as link targets by default (`files.scratchpads`).
- **Directory-move history is versioned state** [40 §2.4]: the root node's `path_moves` set of `{hlc, class, from/, to/, git}`. Classes `explicit` (`file mv` of a directory), `confirmed` (`links fix --prefix`) and `committed` (a settle saw every tracked file under `from/` renamed to `to/` inside one git commit) rewrite glob fields whose literal prefix lies under `from/` (`task.files_owned`, `applies_to` globs, `area.path_globs`) in the same commit and drive merge composition; `observed` entries (inferred lazily, which an in-tree quarantine would also produce) only feed aliases and evidence. Being an ordinary add-wins set field, it survives checkpoint folding, git-side edits, revert and cherry-pick with no op, no canonical item and no trailer [41 B4]. A glob that matches nothing renders `[glob matches nothing]`.
- **Anchors** [40 §2.7]: selector bundles captured at write time from what agents already type — `path`, `path:L-M`, `path::Type/fn` (Rust items, TOML keys), `path#H1/H2` (Markdown), `path@<commit>:L-M` (a pinned historical citation), or literal text through `--quote-file`. An anchor holds its kind (`file|heading|symbol|quote|range|lines`), mode (`live|pinned`), watch (`header` for symbols and headings, `span` otherwise), scope (from hand-written Rust, Markdown and TOML scanners), a W3C-style quote with prefix and suffix widened until unique (then scope, then an occurrence index), an end quote for ranges, a ±16-line window of line hashes, a line hint, the file `oid` and git commit at capture, a span hash, the capture digest and the resolver version — ≈ 345 B typical. A bare line number is never an identity (I-F9); a basename that matches several files is refused at write time (15.9 % of the owner's citations were ambiguous when written [M, 11 §2.1]).
- **Edge `at`** (any node → `artifact`, historical, §3.3) carries ≥ 1 anchor (I-F3); the anchor uid is the edge key's discriminator, so one node cites several places in one file and identical captures on two lanes merge into one anchor.
- **Link states** [40 §2.9], frozen strings (R-16): the primary states an agent sees are `ok`, `moved-auto`, `moved-needs-confirm`, `ambiguous`, `deleted`, `replaced`, `stale-anchor`, completed by the tree-relative `missing`, `absent-in-tree` (`behind` — folded into a header count — or `diverged`, marked per link), `pending`, `planned`, `unverified` (details from a closed set: `budget`, `cloud-only`, `commit not in this repository`, `no tree`, `git`, `size`); anchor states `fresh`, `moved`, `edited`, `ambiguous`, `orphaned`; in LQ also `none` (`link_state(n)` of a node without links) and `unresolved` (`a.state` when the file did not resolve), so the built-ins are total (A1 re-review S-05, S-08). Over a node's anchors the most severe state is taken in the order `missing, replaced, deleted, ambiguous, moved-needs-confirm, stale-anchor, unverified, pending, planned, absent-in-tree, moved-auto, ok`. Two mechanisms affect referrers: a file node that is `removed` or deleted makes them `suspect` (graph state, maintained in the write path, §3.5); everything else is a read-time, tree-derived predicate that never gates `ready`, exists only at a branch tip with a resolved tree, and is shown in packs.

#### 5e.3 Resolution and settle

- **The tree gate comes first** [40 §5.2]: before any search, the resolver reads the worktree's own committed tree τ(HEAD) through the in-process git object layer (M4). G1 — the stored path is in τ(HEAD) but not on disk: this tree moved or deleted it, uncommitted → search. G2 — `observed_git` is an ancestor of HEAD → search, with per-commit renames over `observed_git..HEAD`. G3 — an alias is in τ(HEAD): the move happened on a line this tree has not received → `pending`, no search. G4 — otherwise, per-commit exact renames over the integration window from the stored path and from every alias (this repairs patch integration, rebase and squash: `git apply` ×189 in the census), else `absent-in-tree`. Ancestry answers ≤ 1 ms with a commit-graph and ≤ 5 ms without (the owner's commit-graph misses every fresh HEAD [M, 41 §1]); no R4 resolution path spawns git (the only R4 git process is the opt-in `file mv --git`, §5e.5). **Git work is counted** ([70 S6]): every object decoded costs 1 `fs` unit plus 1 per 4 KiB inflated, E6 per commit by its objects; a **read** path uses the settled `GITFACTS`/`ANCESTRY` caches plus at most one uncached ancestry pair and at most 32 E6 commits per command, and reports anything beyond as `unverified (git)`, whose next step names `moirai links sync` or `moirai check` — never wrong, ≤ 10 ms p99; settles and `check` run the full windows and fill the caches.
- **The evidence cascade** [40 §4.3] over *unbound* candidates only (paths that are no other live file node's current path), inside the root, never into ignored output, never-candidate names (`*.tmp`, `*.bak`, `*~`, `*.orig`, `*.swp`, `4913`, `.#*`, `~$*`, MSYS `sed??????`, the OneDrive conflict-copy form, and any name that is the old basename plus a suffix), cloud-only entries or the Recycle Bin and the OS trash directories ([80 §2.11.1]): **E1** captured intent and hook evidence; (**E2**, a USN-journal rename chain, is not built: D: holds every repository and has no journal, and C:'s journal keeps 1–2 h, so it would never contribute on the owner's trees; its reservations stay and its revisit trigger is a bound tree on a journaled volume whose journal outlives the median settle interval, [74 A13]); **E3d** the parent directory's file id — NTFS keeps a directory's id across renames and moves of it or any ancestor, so one `OpenFileById` re-binds every linked file below a moved directory (0.17–0.40 ms [M, 40 §0.3]; `fsgetpath` on macOS; on Linux the changed-directory frontier, a hit counting only with the stored file-handle digest, since ext4 reuses inode numbers, [80 §2.11.3], [81 B2]) — but only when `D′/basename(p)` carries the recorded file id, or equal size and mtime, or an `oid` in `{o, last_oid}`; otherwise the `replaced` test runs there and a mismatch is `replaced` or `moved-needs-confirm (directory moved, file replaced)` (R-14, [72 M13]); **E3** the file's own id (verified by size and mtime or `oid`, because MFT slots are reused aggressively [M, 09 §2.1]) — per OS ([80 §2.11.4]): `OpenFileById` on Windows, `fsgetpath` on macOS, and on Linux, which has no unprivileged open-by-id, the changed-directory frontier over `DIRMAP`; identity is always the whole tagged file id, never an inode number alone; **E4** nearby directories and alias targets; **E5** recorded `path_moves`; **E6** per-commit exact renames from git trees (identical-blob groups are ambiguous, never paired by order); **E7** a tree-wide `oid` search over files created since the last settle (never on a tree's first settle); **E8** a same-basename new file whose sketch containment against the last content is ≥ 0.8 both ways → `moved-needs-confirm (edited+moved)`. **Copy rule** (I-F13): an equal-`oid` candidate is exact only if a git rename inside one commit, captured intent or — for near candidates only, only where creation times cannot be copied (Windows), and only when the candidate's creation time is unique in the scanned scope — an equal creation time corroborates it (on Linux and macOS it is at most strong evidence, [80 §2.11.4] rule 1, [81 M5]); a candidate that coexisted with the original while it was verified is a copy and never a target; otherwise it is an `identical copy` proposal. **Similarity** (bottom-64 sketch, then symmetric similarity and containment; ≥ 0.5 with a margin ≥ 0.2, git's 50 % default and above the p90 background of unrelated files [M, 10 §5.8]) runs only under `--deep` and only proposes.
- **Classification** [40 §4.4]: exact and unique → `moved-auto`; exact ties → `ambiguous`; identical copies, strong and weak evidence, module splits and merges into a host file → `moved-needs-confirm` with the detail; nothing → `missing`; budget exhausted → `unverified`, never a guess; `replaced` when the path is present but both its `oid` and file id changed and sketch containment is below 0.29 in both directions (`generated` artifacts exempt); at a settle, a present path whose file id differs from `FILEOBS` also triggers one lookup of the recorded id — per OS ([80 §2.11.4] rule 8): `OpenFileById` on Windows (0.24–0.57 ms), `fsgetpath` on macOS, and on Linux the `DIRMAP` frontier, a hit counting only with the stored file-handle digest, else `unverified (budget)` — and if the original is alive elsewhere with a size, mtime or `oid` match the state is `ambiguous (path reused; original at q)`, recorded in `FILEOBS` so reads render it too — a directory promote-replace (`mv storage storage_old; mv storage_v2 storage`) can no longer keep a link `ok` on the wrong file ([72 M13]). Every threshold, pattern, the case fold `fold_v1` and the per-OS rules of [80 §2.11.4] (the twin rule, the unique-creation-time copy rule, whole-`OsFileId` identity, denials as `Unknown`) are constants of the resolver version (R-14), so `resolve(versioned link, tree snapshot with its git objects, the tree's runtime rows, resolver version, budget)` is a pure function (I-F10) whose caches never change an answer and whose only budget-dependent output is `unverified` (A1 re-review S-13); a version bump is announced.
- **Anchor cascade** [40 §4.5], cached in the runtime `ANCHORRES` table per (anchor, file `oid`, resolver version) — written by settles and by the edit-evidence hook, read by every process — ([70 S7]): hint → exact quote inside a uniquely resolved scope, then in the whole file, duplicates broken by prefix/suffix agreement, then window alignment, then the recorded occurrence — never nearest-to-hint → Myers fuzzy match (similarity ≥ 0.75 with a margin; a renamed symbol or heading matches only same-kind headers with margin 0.1, so `acquire` never silently pins to `acquire_shared`) → scope only (`edited`) → `orphaned`. `header` watch keeps a growing function or section `fresh`; `span` watch turns `edited` when the span changes. Measured basis: quote + context resolves 96.3 % of 1,180 real citations and 90.1 % of those whose files changed, against 27.2 % for line numbers [M, 11 §2.3].
- **When it runs** [40 §4.2]: every read verb, within LQ's `fs` units (then `unverified (budget)`; `files.read-budget-ms`, 20 ms, is only the wall-clock safety net, A1 re-review A1P-15); settle points — `SessionStart` (scope: the brief's items and the bound lane's `files_owned`, and the rest of the tree only when its last full settle is older than `files.settle.others-after`, 24 h, or through `links sync`; a hard 150 ms cap including the quiescence wait, [70 S8]), `links sync` (2 s from the CLI; ≤ 200 ms per MCP call with a continuation cursor), the file verbs and `links fix`, `complete` (links of the completed subtree, **as a separate commit after `complete`'s own**, resolved outside the writer byte, [72 M11]), the merge ritual, the optional git `post-commit` block; evidence hooks write runtime rows only. What a settle writes is only `SetField(observation)` (+ `aliases`), `planned → present`, and qualifying `path_moves` entries with their glob rewrites — **each re-bind CAS-guarded on the file node's `rev_seq` as read when its resolution started**, dropped on a mismatch and left to the next settle, so a stale observation never overwrites a newer one ([72 M11]). A settle writes `TreeReg` epoch records `{tree, scope_kind, scope_ref, scope digest, hlc}` and `FILEOBS` rows only for files whose stat quadruple, state or proposals changed; `verified_at(F)` = max(its row value, the newest epoch that covers F — a `full-tree` epoch, or a `lane-owned` epoch whose lane's current `files_owned` globs match F and have the epoch's digest), a `partial` epoch never advancing it, and only the newest epoch per (tree, scope_kind, scope_ref) is kept (A1 re-review A1P-04); so an unchanged tree costs < 16 KB of log and 5–30 ms ([70 S8]); on hook paths and in the MCP server a settle never sleeps — a re-bind still inside its quiescence window waits for the next settle, and every lock wait is bounded by the remaining cap (A1P-06); links sharing a directory (≥ 4) are checked by one enumeration of it. Project files are read through one fixed 128 KiB buffer per thread: `oid` in two passes (statistics, normalised length, line hashes and sketch, then the hash), anchors by a chunked scan with overlap, line-hash arrays capped at `files.max-line-hashes` (65,536; beyond it window-only anchors are `unverified (size)`), at most 2 content readers under `--deep` — so a 16 MiB file costs ≤ 4 MB of private memory, not 32 MiB ([71 RAM-M4]). Quiet mode (§6.6): hooks exit after reading the flag, `SessionStart` does stat only, `--deep`/`--all` are refused unless `--force`.

#### 5e.4 Worktrees and branches

- **Tree identity and eligibility** [40 §5.1]: a tree is its exact git top-level (a nested `.claude/worktrees/*` checkout is its own tree) or, without git, a bound directory; a command's tree is `--tree` → cwd (the hook's `cwd` field) → lease → run → lane → `lane.worktree_path` → the branch's designated tree → `files.main-tree` for `main`. Only an eligible tree gets per-link work (a designated tree, a worktree of the store's repository, a tree with existing observations, or `--tree`); otherwise — for example after an image import on a machine without the files — the header says `files: no tree bound` and nothing is stat'ed. Every file-bearing result names its tree by its display label (§7.1): `files @ lanes/l5np (u/l5np 7c1e0a, dirty 3 4m ago)`, the dirty count read from the runtime `TREES.dirty` row with its age (omitted when none was recorded) — no read scans a worktree, and `links check --dirty` recomputes it on request ([70 S5]).
- **Who may write a re-bind** (I-F6, I-F12) [40 §5.3]: only the branch's single **designated tree** (`lane.worktree_path`, `files.main-tree`, or one `worktree bind`), and only while its HEAD is on the binding's expected git ref (a `git switch` elsewhere turns it into a reader, and line 2 of each result says `reading only: tree on u/other, branch expects u/l5np`); only when it is **fresh** for the node — it has received the observation the current value rests on (`observed_git` empty or an ancestor of HEAD, the stored path in τ(HEAD), or a rename chain from it in the window); only after quiescence; on `main` only for observations committed in the writer tree's HEAD (an uncommitted observation on `main` would reach every lane at `sync` and be re-bound back). Every other tree is a reader whose observations become `PENDING` rows, promoted at a later settle in the writer tree when that tree sees the same move exactly — so moves observed in harness worktrees reach a branch once the code does, never before.
- **Lane lifecycle** [40 §5.4]: a move in the lane's tree lands on `lane/x` with `observed_blob` empty until the agent commits; `merge-check` lists re-binds that rest on uncommitted observations ("commit the move first"), and `--strict-links` also refuses `missing`, `ambiguous`, `replaced` and `stale-anchor` links; between `moirai merge` and `git merge` the trunk tree classifies the moved link as `pending` (G3) and never re-binds it back; after the git merge commits, `moirai links sync --tree <trunk tree> --since <pre-merge HEAD>` settles, and a composite conflict is resolved by observation once the trunk tree is fresh for every side.

#### 5e.5 Explicit verbs and the intent protocol

- **Verbs** (§7.1; namespaced because `add`, `rm`, `move`, `link` already name node verbs): `link ID --at SPEC`, `unlink ID --at aN|PATH`, `file add|mv|rm|relink --after|revert|where [--evidence]`, `links check|sync|fix|mentions|import`, `hooks install --git`. `links check` never writes; `links sync` is a settle point; `links mentions` reports textual path mentions resolved through aliases and `path_moves` (the owner's 188 dangling references [M, 12 §3.5]) and never rewrites a file. Filesystem-changing verbs (`file mv|rm|revert`) are CLI-only and run only in the writer tree of the caller's branch; elsewhere they refuse (exit 5; owner decision #20), naming the raw-`mv` fallback or "ask the orchestrator" to an agent and the bind command only to the orchestrator and owner roles (A1 re-review A-m4); a raw `mv` stays possible and is caught lazily.
- **`file mv` / `file rm` protocol** [40 §3.4–§3.5]: plan (read-only: writer tree, sources, destination absent, affected nodes by a `PATHIDX` range scan and an `ALIASIDX` probe, affected globs) → a durable `FsIntent` record in its own flushed group → a no-replace rename (`MoveFileExW` without replace or copy flags on Windows; `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` or `renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)` on Linux and macOS) followed by `durable-name` on both parent directories, so the rename is durable before the commit that records it ([72 M8], [80 §2.3.2]; a directory is one rename: 9.5 ms for 1,000 files [M, 13 §1.4]; bounded retry on errors 5/32 with a diagnosis; a move across volumes — a file's as a directory's — is refused with exit 7, because a copy followed by an unlink has no namespace order that survives a power loss, A1 re-review A1P-01) → one commit carrying the observation re-points, the old paths as aliases, the `explicit` `path_moves` entry for a directory, the glob rewrites and `FsIntentDone`, each node CAS-guarded. `file rm` deletes (or moves to `trash/`) and flushes the parent directory before its commit. An intent records an intent anchor — a liveness slot the CLI holds for the intent's life (§6.2, [80 §2.7.2]) — not a pid; recovery at the next writer's open (or `doctor`) of an intent whose anchor is Dead decides from the file system — roll forward with provenance `explicit/intent-recovered` after re-establishing the directory barrier (`sync_dir` of both parents, so a rename the crashed CLI never flushed cannot be lost behind a durable roll-forward, A1P-02), abort, or `ambiguous` when both paths exist — so an intent is never lost and the graph never half-applied (crash-point enumeration, §8.2). moirai never touches the git index unless `--git` (the only R4 git process). `file rm` is a dry run without `--yes`, records `removed{reason, replaced_by}`, re-points whole-file anchors to the replacement and span anchors that resolve there, and makes referrers `suspect`: one commit, every referrer known through the reverse index.
- **`links fix`** records human or agent evidence [40 §3.7]: `--accept --expect PATH` (only if the top proposal, re-evaluated, is still PATH), `--to PATH`, `--confirm` (another actor; orchestrator or owner by default, `files.confirm-roles`), `--accept-replacement`, `--drop`, `--same-as ID`, `--split`, `--repin` (after a fuzzy match only with `--at SPEC`), `--pin`, `--restore`, `--prefix FROM TO`. An agent-accepted non-exact re-bind (provenance `agent/…`, or `policy/…` under `files.policy.auto = strong`, from [40 §2.2]'s closed `relink` vocabulary, A1 re-review A-M1) renders `[accepted guess c4480 | confirm: moirai links fix 815 --confirm]` until confirmed, and no printed hint ever accepts a guess: proposals print the evidence command (`moirai file where 815 --evidence`).

#### 5e.6 Merge, history and the image

- **Merge** [40 §5.5], rows in the §5a.7 table: the observation composite composes through recorded directory moves or lands as `FieldEdit`; a derived uid created on both sides is one node; created on one side while removed on the other it is **re-keyed, never resurrected**, to uid(root, `origin_path`, U), with every edge that side added since the LCA re-pointed to it; that is the uid a registration on the merged view derives when U is the greatest predecessor candidate there, and otherwise the re-keyed node holds the path, so no registration competes with it (A1 re-review S-01); `present` vs `removed` is `StatusFork`, never resolved by path presence (the file there may be unrelated); two live nodes at one exact path are a `PathClaim`; anchors, globs and `path_moves` are add-wins sets. The merge engine never reads the file system or git, so merges stay pure functions of history and imported foreign merges recompute identically in every store (I28′, I30′); conflicts only the file system can settle are resolved by a later settle, with provenance `merge-observation/<evidence>`.
- **History verbs never touch the working tree**: after `undo`, `revert` or `cherry-pick` of a file-moving commit the next settle re-binds to wherever the file actually is (links follow files, P5); `file revert COMMIT` is the file-system-aware inverse, and a plain `revert` warns.
- **Image** [40 §5.7]: file and root nodes are ordinary `.moi` files and anchors are `anchor` lines in the referrer's file (§5b.2 rule 9); tombstones keep their anchor lines (I39′); R4 adds no trailer and no commit annotation; nothing machine-local is hashed or exported. Link intent round-trips at every granularity; the importing store resolves against its own worktrees and rebuilds fingerprints from content it can read (a file that vanished before the importer saw it can be re-bound there only by exact evidence and git history). `image.dest.<name>.anchor-text = hash-only` keeps quote text out of a chosen destination.

#### 5e.7 Git independence, accelerators, cloud roots

- **Without git** everything works except the tree gate, E6, git old-blob similarity and settle-time split detection: a missing file then always runs the cascade and is never treated as deleted; ignore rules come from `.gitignore`-format files if present, else `files.ignore` (`target/`, `node_modules/`, `build/`); `moirai init` binds its own directory as `main`'s designated tree.
- **The in-process git object reader is a hard dependency, not an accelerator** [41 B2]: HEAD trees, per-commit renames and ancestry over split commit-graph chains with generation data plus the commits beyond the graph are core resolver inputs (M4); a spawn fallback would be an interim stage and would break the 150 ms `SessionStart` budget (130–750 ms per spawn [M, 10 §5.3]).
- **Accelerators, never needed for correctness**: the `PostToolUse` evidence hook on `Bash(mv *)`, `Bash(rm *)`, `PowerShell(Move-Item|Rename-Item|Remove-Item *)` (default on; ~0.9 % of shell calls); the `Write|Edit` evidence hook (`files.hooks.edit-evidence = auto`: on whenever the hooks run as spawn-free `mcp_tool` handlers, 0.3–0.7 ms per edit, where it refreshes the file id, stat quadruple, `last_oid` and `ANCHORRES` of an edited linked file — which makes edit-then-move exact and keeps reads from re-hashing edited files; off under command hooks, where each of the census's 23,754 edits would cost a 25–73 ms spawn, [70 S3, S7]); marker-delimited git hook blocks (`post-commit`, `post-checkout`, `post-merge`) installed only by the owner with `moirai hooks install --git`. Each is a `config` key (§13). USN-journal replay (E2) and the `PreToolUse` move nudge are not built (§9, [74 A13, A17]).
- **Hands off the user's files** (DR9, I-F11): project files are opened only with `FILE_SHARE_READ|WRITE|DELETE` on Windows, and read-only, no-follow and without access-time updates on Linux and macOS, closed before the next file, never mapped, locked or written; no IDs are written into files, no NTFS object IDs or alternate data streams are created, no text is rewritten (owner decision #21); cloud-only placeholders (OneDrive `RECALL_*` on Windows [M, 41 §1], `SF_DATALESS` on macOS, checked before every read) are never opened or enumerated by an automatic path — answers that need content are `unverified (cloud-only)`, and explicit verbs need `--allow-hydrate` (`files.cloud`).

#### 5e.8 Invariants ([40 §2.10], R-12)

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| I-F1 | On every branch view, at most one live `present`/`planned` file node per (root, exact path); after a merge a duplicate is a `PathClaim` conflict value; case collisions are a resolve-time state |
| I-F2 | Every file, root and anchor uid equals its derivation over its stored inputs (for an anchor, `captured` and `pred`); a `Create` of a uid the store knows reuses its `#N` |
| I-F3 | Every `at` edge carries ≥ 1 anchor; every anchor belongs to exactly one `at` edge; anchor uids are unique per (src, dst) |
| I-F4 | OS file ids, volume keys, mtimes, creation times, stat caches, resolution states, proposals and every runtime table of §5d.1's R4 row never appear in versioned, hashed or exported data |
| I-F5 | Read verbs (`show`, `pack`, `brief`, `get`, `find`, `q`, `links check`, MCP reads) append nothing to the log |
| I-F6 | An automatic re-bind is written only with exact evidence (or under `files.policy.auto = strong`), only by the branch's writer tree, only when that tree is fresh for the node, only after the quiescence re-check, and on `main` only for an observation committed in the writer tree's HEAD |
| I-F7 | `removed` is never inferred from absence: only `file rm`, `links fix --drop`/`--same-as`, or (with `files.deletion-inference = main-tree-commits`) a deletion commit seen on the main tree |
| I-F8 | Stored paths are root-relative, `/`-separated, with no empty, `.` or `..` segment and no leading `/`, valid UTF-8, exact bytes, no Unicode normalisation except [80] P3 (NFC for untracked names on normalization-insensitive volumes); the one exception is root `abs`, a machine-local absolute path in the form of [80] P12 (Windows `X:/…` with the drive letter upper-cased; the leading `/` kept on Linux and macOS), checked for existence and `oid` only and never compared across machines or OSes |
| I-F9 | No live span anchor has a bare line number as its only selector |
| I-F10 | `resolve(versioned link, tree snapshot with its git objects, the tree's runtime rows, resolver version, budget)` is a pure function; thresholds and pattern lists are resolver-version constants; caches never change an answer, and `unverified` is the only budget-dependent output (A1 re-review S-13) |
| I-F11 | Project files are opened only with full sharing, never mapped or locked, closed before the next; no automatic path opens cloud-only content |
| I-F12 | Each branch has at most one designated tree and each tree designates at most one branch; trees are identified by exact git top-level |
| I-F13 | No automatic re-bind rests on equal content alone; a candidate that coexisted with the original is never a target |
| I-F14 | A derived uid removed or deleted on a view is never live again there through registration or merge; only explicit, recorded doors bring it back: `links fix --restore`, `Undelete`, and a `revert`, `undo`, `cherry-pick` or `op restore` of the commit that removed or deleted it (A1 re-review S-14) |

#### 5e.9 Placement, size and budgets

Built where its hosts are built ([40 §8.1] in [60]'s order; no interim mode, DR13): **M0** FL-0 (reservations R-1…R-18 in format v1, the ABNF additions, the resolver constant table, the link logic of the reference model with a brute-force anchor resolver) and FL-1 (path rules, `is_text`/`oid`, sketches and similarity, anchor capture and resolution, the Myers matcher, the histogram diff shared with M3's diff3, the scope scanners, the gitignore matcher) validated on the owner's replay corpora before the freeze; **M1** the reserved record kinds and sections through the section registry; **M2** FL-3 graph layer (file and root nodes, derived identity, `at` edges and anchors, the indexes, the `suspect` extension); **M3** FL-7 merge rules; **M4** the R4-facing git API; **M5** FL-8 image carrier; **M6** the runtime — FL-2 `ProjectFs` seam with its deterministic simulator, FL-4 resolver, settle, tree gate, evidence cascade with git renames (E6; USN evidence E2 excluded, §9), writer-tree and freshness rules, bindings, the `FsIntent` protocol and recovery, and the file operations as `Store` API commands; **M7** FL-6 built-ins (§7.7); **M8** FL-5 CLI verbs; **M9** FL-9 pack rendering, hooks, and `links import` (owner decision #23: it runs at the cutover, after the owner reviews its ambiguous list); **M10** the MCP operations. Size ≈ 22–30k lines of product Rust plus 10–14k of tests, ≈ 68–93 units across those milestones (§9) — M6's range already excludes E2 ([60 §3.7]), and M9 keeps `links import`'s 1–2 units because #23 decided that it is built; [60 §7.1] adds the audits' and the cross-platform deltas per milestone (M6: + 1–2.5 units each), and velocity re-issues the calendar at the M0 exit. Budgets (§8.3): link work per 50-link pack ≤ 3 ms p50 idle and ≤ 5 ms loaded when all links are present, ≤ 5 ms p50 when all 50 sit in one moved directory, ≤ 5 ms p50 when 10 of the files were edited since the last settle; read-path git work ≤ 10 ms p99; `SessionStart` settle ≤ 150 ms and ≤ 16 KB of log when nothing changed; zero log bytes from reads, zero idle CPU; ≤ 4 MB for a read verb resolving anchors in a 16 MiB file; ≈ 12 MB of anchors on disk at the owner's scale and 0 B resident at idle.

## 6. Concurrency, leases, change feed, durability classes

### 6.1 Processes and locks

| Process | Lifetime | Store access | Locks |
|---|---|---|---|
| `moirai` CLI (agent Bash calls, command hooks, orchestrator scripts) | ms | direct (§4.5, §4.7) | writer byte for the commit phase only; maintenance byte for maintenance above 2× a threshold (§4.5 step 12) |
| `moirai mcp` (one per Claude Code session, serving all its subagents across worktrees [08 §2]; one per thread under Codex, which starts a server for every subagent and whose `codex` profile releases every mapping and overlay at the end of each request, [90 §4.5]; opens the store lazily at its first tool call; also the handler of every `mcp_tool` hook, §7.5) | session | direct; keeps maps, the `main` overlay and a byte-bounded LRU of branch overlays (`mcp.overlay-bytes` 4 MiB, at most 8, G19) warm, every per-view and per-request structure in a region arena of ≥ 256 KiB `VirtualAlloc` chunks (anonymous `mmap` on Unix) released whole on eviction, on a segment-set change and at request end ([71 RAM-M2]); runs delta checkpoints, promotions and settles as resumable ≤ 5 ms slices between requests and caps `links_sync` at 200 ms per call ([70 S9]); never runs a rollup (it spawns the §4.9 child); at most two OS threads, both blocked, and 0 % CPU when idle (no timers) | same as CLI, plus its liveness-slot byte (§6.2); leader byte only if the optional leader is built (§2.2) |
| leader (optional; built only if the M0 measurements require it, §2.2) | session | the endpoint of [80 §2.8] (a named pipe with a per-user DACL, `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE` and `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS` on Windows; a pathname Unix socket in a 0700 per-user directory on Linux and macOS), published in `LOCK`; broadcast; sandboxed clients use the direct path; every forwarded write carries a client-generated 128-bit key (G13) | leader byte + the writer and flush bytes of group commit |

Writer protocol: the writer byte through `LockBytes::acquire_within` with the G1 blocking wait (2 s bound; a timeout prints the holder's diagnostics and anchor liveness), then the flush byte for group commit (§2.8, §4.5 step 10). Readers take no locks; correctness comes from immutable segments, `committed_lsn` and the monotonic `seq`. Maintenance (delta checkpoint, promotion, rollup, GC) holds the maintenance byte and takes the writer byte only to append its durable records, which use the same group commit (§4.5 step 12). Under a 16-agent burst the appends serialize in tens of µs each and ≈ 2–3 flushes cover all of them, so the last acknowledgement costs ≈ 2–3 flush times — est. 4–8 ms on the owner's NVMe, ≈ 25–35 ms p99 on current Apple silicon ([80 §2.4]) — instead of 16 flushes, without convoy (F-A2). Every write is three-phase (§4.5), so the writer byte is held for the scan, re-validation and the append only (p99 ≤ 5 ms for every verb class, merges, syncs, settle-bearing `complete` and `TX` included, [70 S2]). Per-process caches: mapped segments (shared pages), the compact overlay since the segment set's `upto_lsn`, the branch overlays the process read (symbols are probed in place); invalidation is by `committed_lsn` ("replay the tail"), never "drop everything", because sealed segments never change.

### 6.2 Leases and claims

- `claim 12 [--branch R] --agent A [--ttl 15m|run] [--start]` is one durable commit that writes a **`Lease` record only** (CL5): the task must be live and `ready` on R (no live lease by another holder, no unabsorbed `settled`/`deleted` marker, `gates` do not constrain `claim`); the versioned status is untouched unless `--start` is given, which additionally performs `open → in_progress` on R; otherwise the first `set`/`complete` under the lease performs it (`complete` from `open` is the compound `open → in_progress → done` in one commit). The lease row captures the task's `files_owned` globs (refreshed by any `set files_owned` under the lease), so a pack's do-not-touch list reads `LEASES` only ([70 S5]). Result `{lease: L-<n>, token: <fence>, branch: R, expires}`. Claiming again as the same holder is idempotent.
- Fencing: `complete`, `set --lease`, `release` must present the lease; a stale token fails with exit 5 and names the current holder (Kleppmann's argument [07 §7.4]). Every mutation takes CAS guards (`--if-rev` against `rev_seq`, `--if-status`, `--if-holder`, `--if-tip`); a failed guard returns the current value (exit 4) with what changed and who changed it.
- Liveness: TTL (15 min self-claims; **run-scoped** for dispatcher claims: `--ttl run` — 60 minutes is wrong for the owner's multi-hour runs [22 §2.2]). A run-scoped lease is released **only** by `apply` (for the leases its batch names), `run close`, or an explicit `reclaim --run <id>`/`reclaim --older-than`; nothing in moirai observes a harness background task's liveness, because that would need polling (CL5). `heartbeat` is a lazy record, and any write that presents a TTL-bound lease (`--lease`, MCP `lease` or `MOIRAI_LEASE`) renews its deadline when more than half the TTL has elapsed — one lazy record, the renewal rule of Codex's `session-ttl` anchors applied to every TTL lease ([90 §4.4]) — so a self-claim with anchor `none` (generic harnesses, no MCP) stays live while its holder writes; a holder that works longer than the TTL without a moirai write runs `moirai heartbeat L`, which C0's `AGENTS.md` block teaches ([90 §2.3]). **Holder liveness** ([72 B2]; a check at read time, never a poll). The CLI exits within ~0.2 s of a `claim`, so a lease never names the claiming process. It names a **holder anchor** (32 B, [80 §2.7.2]) `{kind, os, slot hint, session_hash (BLAKE3-128, 16 B), boot_hash}` of kind `session` or `session-ttl`: the session's MCP server holds one liveness-slot byte of `LOCK` from the moment it knows its identity until it exits and writes a slot record at offset 4096 `{kind, nonce, primary session hash, alias hash, ProcId}`; `SessionStart(clear)` runs on that server and records the new session id as the alias, and the lease stores the primary hash; a CLI or hook reads the slot table once and matches its harness identity — the one whose lifetime the server tracks: `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` for Claude Code's per-session server, `CODEX_THREAD_ID` for Codex's per-thread servers (a Codex hook's `session_id` is the thread); every id namespaced `<harness>:<id>` before hashing — against the primary and alias hashes; a Codex server, whose environment carries no id, takes its slot at its first call carrying `_meta.threadId`, and its leases are `session-ttl` anchors, Alive while its slot is held and the deadline its own calls renew has not passed; a lease of a thread whose own server holds no slot, or of a caller with no harness identity, has anchor `none` ([90 §4.4]), and a lease taken where no server is connected has kind `none` and lives by TTL or run scope alone. Liveness is three-valued and needs no PID, so it holds across PID namespaces, sandboxes and PID reuse: **Alive** when the boot matches (or is unknown to the checker) and a held slot's valid record names the primary session; **Dead** when the boot differs or no held slot's record names it; **Unknown** when `LOCK` cannot be read or probed (e.g. `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` for a command running as another principal, `EPERM` from a sandbox, [X20 §0 item 5]). **Unknown never ends a lease early**; only its deadline, run scope or an explicit `reclaim` does. `expires` = `{wall, boot_hash, mono}` ([80] X-F2): a wall-clock deadline plus the hash of the boot identity and the boot-clock deadline, the boot clock being monotonic and including suspend on every OS ([80 §2.7.1]): on the same boot the boot-clock deadline decides, so wall-clock steps change nothing (the boot identity is itself invariant under clock steps, suspend and hibernation; a process in Unknown-boot mode judges by the wall deadline); after a reboot every non-run-scoped lease is Dead and is released at the first read with a triage line, while run-scoped leases keep their rule. `ProcId` (pid, start, boot, PID namespace) is kept only as diagnostics. `FsIntent` records an anchor of kind `intent`: the CLI running `file mv`/`file rm` holds its own slot from before the intent until `FsIntentDone`/`Aborted`, so the intent is Dead, and recoverable, as soon as that slot is free or carries another nonce ([80 §2.7.2]). The MCP server ends with its parent process as well as on stdin EOF (an event-driven watch, no timer), so its slot lives as long as the session. TTL values are `lease.*` config keys. **Lease kinds and anchors** ([90 §4.3, §4.4]): a lease is a task lease, a run-scoped role lease (`claim --role R --run ID`) or the orchestrator's session role lease (`claim --role orchestrator --session`); its anchor is `session` (Claude Code's per-session server), `session-ttl` (a Codex thread's server: Alive while the slot is held **and** the deadline that the thread's own calls renew has not passed, so a leaked server's leases expire) or `none`; a session role lease and an environment lease are bound to one thread where the harness names threads. `SubagentStop` releases or flags leases held by the stopping `agent_id` (only for hooks that fire; the run-scoped rule covers Workflow agents).
- `complete 12 --lease L --outcome done|failed|abandoned --summary - [--evidence …]` writes `done` on the lease's branch, releases the lease **into `settled`**, returns newly ready ids on that branch and — when the caller passes the digest its pack printed — a notice listing the critical rules, owner rulings and blockers that changed since that pack (`pack.staleness-notice`, [72 m2]); the link settle of the completed subtree follows as a separate, CAS-guarded commit (§5e.3), so `complete` and MCP `write{tx: CALL tx.complete(…)}` produce the same commit; refused (exit 6) while a child is open or an open `fail_*` verdict `gates` the task.
- Mutexes (benchmark slot, merge queue): `task{work_kind = mutex}` with the same lease semantics.

### 6.3 Change feed

Every commit has `seq`; each record lists ops, `affected` ids (dependents unblocked, referrers of a tombstone, sources marked suspect, newly ready) and lease/marker events; ref moves are entries. `changes --since <seq> [--branch R|--all] [--about #..] [--for-agent A]` reads records from `HEAD.seq_ring` (last 32) or the `hist` commit index (older), filters by relevance (nodes the agent claimed, is blocked on, authored or cited) and prints one line per change; every command header prints the current `seq`. A hook delta reads at most `hooks.delta.max-commits` (2,000) commits and then prints `N older changes: moirai changes --since S` ([70 S12]). No file watcher (lossy on Windows [08 §4 W10]), no polling process; the feed is pulled at hook boundaries (`UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStart`; the `PostToolBatch` delta is not built, §9) and, only with the optional leader (§2.2), pushed by it to followers and to `watch` (leader-only, never a polling process — F-B5).

### 6.4 Idempotency and Workflow resume

Every write verb and `apply` take a key (MCP: `idempotency_key`); the writer hashes it and the payload (BLAKE3-128 each) and binds the record to the branch. Because every write verb, `apply` batch and MCP `write` compiles to one LQ `TX` block (§7.7), the payload is the **canonical bound AST** of that block with its parameter values: formatting, comments, keyword case, Cypher-versus-GQL spellings, reverse aliases, parameter order and variable names are normalised away; only committed results are recorded, so a refused block can be retried with the same key ([50 §3.10]). Hit with equal payload → the original result, `replayed: true`; different payload → exit 9 with the original result; different branch → exit 9 unless the original branch was merged into the caller's or deleted after merge (N13e). Entries live 30 days (a Workflow resumed after > 7 days re-runs every completed agent [07 §4.1]) and ride the segment pipeline (40 B each). `apply` batches with local `$refs` are all-or-nothing, keyed once per run (`run:<id>`), and land on the branch derived from the run (`run --runs_in--> lane --moirai_branch`) unless `--branch` overrides; a batch containing a lease whose branch differs from the batch branch is refused before any write (D6). Workflow agents that write directly use content-hash keys, so a re-run critic that emits different findings does not get the old results back (X3). A write that carries no key gets a **default key** = BLAKE3(namespaced session, the attested thread or agent where one exists — Codex `threadId`, the Claude stamp's `agent_id` — else the resolved actor, canonical bound AST; [90 §4.1]) valid for `idempotency.default-window` (10 min; `--no-dedupe` opts out), so an agent that retries after its writer died with the commit already adopted never creates a duplicate; a process that dies abnormally prints `outcome unknown: re-run with the same key or check moirai changes` ([72 m5]).

### 6.5 Durability classes

| Class | Members | Guarantee |
|---|---|---|
| `durable` | every graph mutation, `claim`/`complete`/`release`, `rm`, ref moves, `ClientHead`, `settled`/`deleted`/`cleared` markers, `Idem`, `Pin`, `GitMap`, `Checkpoint`, `apply` batches (one flush per batch) | acknowledged only after a flush that covers it has returned and been published, and its writer's identity check (`NtFlushBuffersFileEx(DATA_SYNC_ONLY)`, `fdatasync` or `F_FULLFSYNC`, [80 §2.3]); survives power loss |
| `lazy` | the kinds in `durability.lazy-kinds` (heartbeats, read cursors, session marks by default) and R4's runtime evidence | appended; published at once when no durable group is pending, else by the covering flush's publish; flushed by the next covering flush; may be lost after power loss, an OS crash or a failed flush (documented) — recovery and readers treat a lost lazy tail as the end of the log (§4.2); graph mutations are always durable, a rule no key can change |

A flush failure aborts the process (never retried on the same handle); the next flush holder re-writes and flushes. Extents read as zero beyond the tail and are made by the per-file-system method of [80 §2.3.3] (G11). Group commit is leaderless and always on (§2.8), with no accumulation timer: a flush covers whatever was appended when it starts. The classes `durable+meta` and `durable-name`, and the rule that an unavailable class refuses the store, are in [80 §2.3].

### 6.6 Quiet mode

`moirai quiet on|off` sets `HEAD.flags.quiet`; any lane with status `measuring` implies it (the explicit flag wins). In quiet mode: no automatic delta checkpoints until the tail exceeds 8× the threshold, then exactly one bounded delta runs (G10); no rollup, promotion, GC, export or import (unless `--force`); the MCP server stays resident at 0 % CPU (it has no timers, its maintenance slices and the rollup child are suppressed, and its only extra thread is blocked on stdin); R4's evidence hooks and git hook blocks exit after reading the flag, `SessionStart` does stat only (no hashing, enumeration, git windows or writes), and `links check|sync --deep|--all` are refused unless `--force`. Because no moirai process has background work in any mode, the idle rule of [02 §9] is satisfied by construction; the flag only suppresses the foreground maintenance a busy writer would otherwise trigger.

---

## 7. Agent interface (final)

### 7.1 CLI surface

Conventions: compact line-oriented text by default (ids first, deterministic order), `--ids` (never prints a header, pages at `output.ids-max-bytes` (24,000 B; `0` = unlimited for scripts), and puts the count, the cursor and any budget footer on stderr with exit 10, so a harness's cut never drops middle ids silently, [90 §2.1]), `--json v1`/`--jsonl` with one frozen, versioned envelope for every verb `{"v":1,"branch":…,"rev":…,"data":…,"next":…,"dropped":…}` extended only by additive keys (`commit`, `view`, `use`, `live`, `tree`, `reads`, `cols`, `notices`, `warnings`, `budget`, …; [50 §6.4]), empty results exit 0, bodies and texts via `--stdin` or `-f FILE` (never argv: PowerShell 5.1 strips quotes [07 §5.2]; `@file` is not part of the contract, because an unquoted `@x` is a splat in PowerShell 5.1 [M, 16 §6.10]), **ids bare in argv** (`show 40`, `--scope 88`: an unquoted `#40` starts a comment in Git Bash and PowerShell 5.1; `#N` is accepted inside a token or in query text), no non-path argument starting with `/` (MSYS path rewriting), and no argument starting with `#`, `~`, `=`, `@` or `!`; values with spaces and free text on stdin or `-f`, and a value that carries `*`, `?`, `[` or `{` (a glob list for `--refs`, `--applies-to` or `--path`) single-quoted or on stdin, never bare (zsh aborts with "no matches found", bash substitutes file names) — the shell rules T1–T10 of [80 §4], valid in bash, zsh, dash, fish, PowerShell and cmd, with byte-identical output on every OS; `--agent` and every other context field resolved in [90 §4.1]'s order — rights only from the presented lease; the actor from the lease holder, then attested identity (Codex `_meta`, the Claude stamp), then the declared `--agent` (refused if it differs from the presented lease's holder), then the environment (`MOIRAI_*` and the harness's variables, which a child process inherits and which therefore rank last) — with the source recorded as `actor_src`, and `session:<harness>:<id>` when nothing names the agent, no ANSI or prompts off-TTY, every verb accepts `--branch R`, `--lease L`, `--client NAME`; every result's first line is `branch: <ref> | rev <seq> | <n> rows`, the `branch` field always first (the D2/D3 safety signal), followed only when they apply by the view (`as-of`, `staged`, `live`), `behind main N` and, for file-bearing results, `files @ <tree> (<git branch> <head>[, dirty N <age>])` or `files: no tree bound`. **Header rules** (A1 re-review A-M3; revision 2's own example was 104 B against a 100 B limit with a 16-byte placeholder for the tree): `<tree>` is the tree's **display label** — its path relative to the parent directory of the main worktree (for a tree outside it, its last two path components), and the git branch its name — each cut from the left with `...` to ≤ 20 B and ≤ 16 B; the full canonical path goes only to `--json` (`tree`) and `file where`. A reader tree's note has one ASCII spelling, R-16's `reading only: tree on <ref>, branch expects <ref>`, and stands on line 2 (≤ 80 B), like the reading echo. The limits are counted **per part**: the base fields (`branch`, `rev` or `rev <old> -> <new>`, the view flag, `<n> rows`, `committed <commit>`) ≤ 60 B; the `files @` segment with its separator ≤ 80 B; the extras (`behind main N`, search, diff range, composite parts, recomputation, `view moved`, a write's `key`, `lease` and dry-run parts) ≤ 60 B; `dropped`/`more` ≤ 30 B; the goldens count each part, and the examples of [40] and [50] are re-counted there. A write prints `rev <old> -> <new> | committed <commit>`, and the commit id of a read appears in `--json`, `show` and write results only ([73 F13]); separators and arrows are ASCII (`|`, `->`), link markers name the evidence as `verify #N` with one legend line per result, and success paths print no explanatory prose (the skill carries it). A result with a non-zero exit carries ≤ `output.nonzero-exit-max-bytes` (8,000) bytes on stdout, because Claude Code's Bash tool shows only ≈ 10,000 characters of a failed call (Codex shows ≈ 40,000 bytes with a middle cut, [90 §6.1]); a store write refused by a sandbox exits 7 with one line for the agent (the equivalent MCP call; "do not request escalated permissions"), one fallback line (if the moirai tools are unavailable, put the write in the final `result.v1` or tell the user) and one for the owner (the harness's fix), [90 §5.3]; a longer `--ids` cut continues behind a cursor ([74 A07]). Every read verb is a named query of the LQ standard library and every write verb a named mutation (§7.7): `--show-query`/`--show-tx` print the LQ behind them, and every read verb takes `--at REV`, `--tree DIR` and `--budget …`. Exit codes: 0 ok (incl. empty and paginated) · 1 internal · 2 usage, parse or bind error · 3 not found (tombstone printed) · 4 guard conflict (current value printed; `EXPECT`, `IF TIP`) · 5 lease lost/stale token/branch or tree mismatch (incl. a file verb outside the writer tree) · 6 precondition (blocked, gating verdict, staged merge, I13/I14, assertion, role policy, `--strict` with non-`ok` links) · 7 store unavailable/locked/file busy/no git for an image verb/refused location/store I/O fault in a mapping/durability outcome pending or unknown · 8 partial batch · 9 idempotency payload/branch mismatch · 10 incomplete result (budget, pre-flight refusal, cancellation, `fs` budget exhausted).

```
# context
moirai brief   [--scope ID] [--role R] [--budget 8000] [--more] [--across]
moirai pack    ID --role R [--phase P] [--budget N] [--since-round k] [--more] [--rules] [--record-run] [--explain] [-o FILE]   # N defaults to pack.budget.<role>, capped by pack.cli.max-bytes (24,000 B) unless -o
# read (named queries of the LQ standard library; all take --at REV, --tree DIR, --budget ..., --show-query, --json)
moirai ready   [--scope ID] [--role R] [--limit 20] [--cursor c] [--ids] [--explain ID] [--across [REFS]]
moirai blocking [--scope ID] [--ids]            # ids of all blocking tasks (is_blocker ∧ kind:task), markers honoured
moirai blockers ID [--transitive] [--explain] [--across]
moirai show    ID.. [--full] [--neighbors N] [--across] [--uid] [--markers]
moirai find    [TEXT] [kind:task status:open prio:..1 area:net done:false suspect:true conflicted:true] [--bodies] [--ids]
moirai tree    ID [--depth N]      moirai notes --path src/net/x.rs      moirai stale [--scope ID]
moirai changes --since SEQ [--branch R|--all] [--about ID,..] [--for-agent A]
moirai stats   loop P | refuted-share --role R [--round k]      moirai lane conflicts L1 L2      moirai conflicts [REF]
# query language (R5, §7.7)
moirai q NAME [k=v|k:v].. | q - (quoted heredoc) | q -f FILE.lq   [--explain|--profile|--check] [--cursor K]      moirai q --list | --describe NAME
moirai tx - | tx -f FILE.lq [--dry-run] [--if-tip COMMIT] [--lease L] [--idempotency-key K]      # one TX block = one commit
# write (named mutations; all: --idempotency-key K, --agent A, --if-rev N, --if-status S, --if-holder H, --show-tx)
moirai add     task|doc|note|rule|decision|question|finding|verdict|measurement|artifact|run|lane|area 'title' [--parent ID] [--blocked-by ID,..] [--blocks ID,..] [--field k=v].. [--body -|-f FILE]
moirai rule|note|decision|finding|verdict|measurement --stdin|-f FILE [--critical] [--about ID,..] [--applies-to 'role:tester,path:crates/phys/**'] [--authority owner --owner-quote-file FILE]
moirai set     ID [k=v].. [--status S] [--done] [--resolution R] [--lease L]
moirai link    A --blocks|--gates|--parent|--cites[@COMMIT]|--supersedes|--refutes|--confirms|--verifies|--addresses|--implements|--derived-from|--answers|--scoped-to|--depends-on B
moirai unlink  A --<kind> B        moirai move ID --parent P        moirai reopen ID --reason T
moirai doc patch SECTION --remove FILE --add FILE [--depends-on S1,S2]     # refuses (exit 4) if the removed text is not a substring
moirai supersede OLD --with NEW    moirai retract ID --reason T     moirai answer Q --by owner --verbatim-file FILE
moirai rm      ID [--reason T] [--replaced-by ID] [--cascade|--reparent] [--release] [--dry-run] [--yes]
moirai resolve KEY --take ours|theirs|base|repoint:ID|--value V | --all --policy P
moirai apply   FILE|- | --from claude-journal:RUN|codex-exec:DIR|jsonl:FILE [--idempotency-key run:ID] [--branch R] [--dry-run]   # result.v1 records ([90 §7.2]); --from-journal RUN = --from claude-journal:RUN
moirai check   ID                  # appends only lazy ANCESTRY facts: a write verb outside the reads-append-nothing set
# file links (R4, §5e; file mv/rm/revert only in the writer tree of the caller's branch)
moirai link    ID --at SPEC [--at SPEC].. [--watch header|span] [--planned] [--quote-file FILE] [--allow-hydrate]      moirai unlink ID --at aN|PATH
moirai file add PATH.. [--kind source|doc|asset|generated|dir] [--root NAME]      moirai file where PATH|ID [--evidence]
moirai file mv SRC.. DST [--git] [--dry-run] [--retry-ms 1000]      moirai file rm PATH.. [--reason T] [--replaced-by PATH|ID] [--trash] [--recursive] [--yes]
moirai file relink PATH|ID --to PATH --after      moirai file revert COMMIT
moirai links check|sync [--scope ID | --path GLOB | --all] [--tree DIR] [--deep] [--budget-ms N] [--strict]
moirai links fix ID|aN --accept --expect PATH | --to PATH | --confirm | --accept-replacement | --drop | --same-as ID | --split | --repin [--at SPEC] | --pin | --restore
moirai links fix --prefix FROM TO      moirai links mentions [PATH | --moved]      moirai links import --from-markdown GLOB [--dry-run]      moirai hooks install --git
# coordination
moirai claim   ID.. | --next [--scope ID] [--role R] --agent A [--ttl 15m|run] [--start] | --role R --run ID [--branch B] | --role orchestrator --session   # role leases ([90 §4.3]); --lease L presents the caller's own lease      moirai heartbeat L     moirai release L
moirai complete ID --lease L --outcome done|failed|abandoned --summary - [--evidence commit:sha|ID..]
moirai reclaim --older-than 30m | --run ID      moirai run open|close ID [--lane L]
# branches, refs, history (orchestrator rituals; CLI only)
moirai branch [NAME [--from REF|COMMIT] [--kind work|plan]] | --list | -d|-D NAME
moirai checkout REF|COMMIT [--branch-new NAME]      moirai worktree bind DIR REF | unbind DIR | --list
moirai lane open NAME --worktree DIR [--git-branch B] [--base SHA]      moirai lane close|freeze NAME
moirai sync [--check] [--refork]                    # merge main into the current branch
moirai merge SRC [--into DST] [--policy P] [--strict] [--base COMMIT]   moirai merge --continue|--abort [SRC [--into DST]]   moirai merge-check SRC [--into DST]
moirai cherry-pick COMMIT [--onto REF]   moirai revert COMMIT [--onto REF] [--mainline 1]   moirai undo [--ref REF] [N] [--expect COMMIT]
moirai tag NAME [COMMIT] [-m MSG] [--pin]   moirai reflog REF   moirai op log
moirai log [REF] [--graph] [--node N] [--actor A] [--since SEQ] [--all-branches]
moirai diff A..B | A...B [--node N] [--stat]      moirai blame N [FIELD]      # any read verb at a past view: --at REV (LQ: USE REV)
# git image
moirai image export [--to DEST] [--refs 'main,tags/*,lane/*'] [--granularity checkpoint|commit] [--object-format sha1|sha256] [--create] [--if-older DURATION]
moirai image import [--from DEST|FILE] [--refs ...]      moirai image push|pull [REMOTE]     moirai image doctor [--rebuild-map]     moirai image show COMMIT     moirai image gc
# store, integration, maintenance
moirai init [--here | --link STORE] [--default-branch main] [--force --shadow]      moirai doctor [store|lanes [--refresh-graph]|image|agents|hooks|sandbox|--verify|--fsck]
moirai backup DIR [--force]      moirai restore DIR --into EMPTY_DIR      moirai repair --rebuild-from-log      # backup: transaction-consistent copy of the store; restore and repair re-roll the epoch (G25)
moirai gc [--rollup [--if-needed]] [--prune] [--reflog-expire 90d] [--cruft-delay 14d]   moirai quiet on|off   moirai migrate
moirai config get|set|unset KEY [VALUE] [--user|--store] | list [--effective|--defaults] | check     # §13
moirai export md --to DIR | memory-md | agents-md | rules --format claude|agents-md [--to DIR]      moirai schema result-v1
moirai integrate --detect | claude|codex|generic.. [--scope user|project] [--hooks none|min|full] [--tools read|core|all] [--store-writes writable-root|execpolicy-store|execpolicy|mcp] [--dry-run|--print] | --check | --remove <harness>..   # [90 §3]; Tier B harnesses, `package` and other export formats only under #45
moirai hooks install [--git]        # = moirai integrate claude --hooks full; registers only the hooks enabled in config, with the transport hooks.transport chooses (§7.5)
moirai hook session-start|prompt|subagent-start|subagent-stop|agent-launched|stamp|fs-evidence|git-post-commit|git-post-merge|git-post-checkout      moirai mcp [--legacy|--modern|--auto|--read-only] [--tools read|core|all] [--client C]
```
