The owner's realistic scale (0.3–0.5 M nodes after three years [02 §12.6]) sits between the 1e5 and 1e6 columns; the RAM target holds across all three because data is shared through read-only mappings.

**Budget gates and where they run.** Every budget above that a gate enforces, and every correctness and token gate, is listed once, per owner priority, in §8.3, which is normative: each row names its gate and the milestone from which it is mandatory, and stays mandatory afterwards ([60 §3.13, §5.4]). Gates are measured under the M0 measurement protocol [60 §5.1] — nightly on the owner's laptop in owner-granted agent-free windows (the laptop-only profile L, #34, §9) with Defender on, idle and under the replayed 16-agent load fixture, and at every milestone exit on the same machine; PR-level runs on hosted GitHub Actions **Windows** runners (free for the public repository, #36; synthetic data only; never Linux or macOS in M0–M11, #32) build, lint, type-check and run the unit, property and differential suites — a correctness signal, never a timing, RAM, floor, crash or kill-loop gate, because the hosted images are Windows Server, not the gated Windows 11 + Defender profile; regressions beyond the measured noise band block. The owner's realistic scale (0.3–0.5 M nodes) is measured weekly in a laptop window and at M11.

### 8.2 Benchmark and verification plan

**M0 measurements on the owner's machine** (under the M0 measurement protocol [60 §5.1]: Defender on; idle and under the replayed 16-agent load fixture; sample sizes tiered by duration — n ≥ 10,000 below 1 ms, ≥ 1,000 at 1–50 ms (p99), ≥ 200 at 50 ms–1 s (p95 and max), ≥ 20 above 1 s (max) — with 5 repetitions below 1 s and 3 above, gated on the median, [74 A04]; floors re-measured interleaved in the same run; peak private bytes at exit and peak working set via `GetProcessMemoryInfo`, flush counts, page faults; `hyperfine -N --warmup 5` for spawn-to-exit):
1. open → append → `DATA_SYNC_ONLY` flush → close on a 64 MiB log file, n per the protocol (the Defender close cost, G8) — decides, before M1, whether the CLI must forward to a leader.
2. The group-commit protocol under contention: 16 writer processes through the writer and flush bytes (overlapped `LockFileEx`): last-acknowledgement p50/p99, device flushes per burst, fairness and the flush-byte hand-off latency (G1, [80 §2.4]). The vehicle (the toy log) writes the product's `RecHdr`, chained groups and a commit-size distribution taken from the specification (typical 0.3–0.6 KB, a tail of large inline commits up to `store.commit.inline-max-bytes`), and the run sweeps an injected in-lock CPU cost from 0 to the writer-byte hold budget (p99 5 ms, max 20 ms): the leader stays out of M1 only if last-acknowledgement p99 ≤ 50 ms holds **at the maximum in-lock cost**, and M1's writer gates re-check the decision on the product (A1 re-review A1P-03).
3. Overlay build vs branch age: branches forked 1k / 7k / 14k / 60k commits ago with 50 concurrent writers, with and without the per-ref index (G28), **plus a lane synced at every `SubagentStart` for 14 days while `main` took ~500 commits a day and three lane merges** — fixes G15/G16 thresholds and `store.promotion.overlay-ops` from the 10 ms lane-read gate at the measured build rate ([70 S1]).
4. `sync` bytes per lane per day under merge-by-reference vs copying (G28).
5. Pinned file count and disk with 50 branches forked over 40 checkpoints (G28).
6. The codec decision of [90 §11.3] (owner decision #44): size, encoder speed, decoder speed and RSS of `lz4_flex` blocks with and without a raw-content dictionary, `ruzstd` at its Fastest level, and the `zstd` CLI at level 1 with the same dictionary as the proxy for an own zstd-format encoder, on the owner's real notes and plan sections and on `hist`-sized frames (U12; nothing leaves the machine); bytes per token of four synthetic fixture classes (English prose, LQ/code, id-dense lists, 20 % Cyrillic mixed) for Claude's tokenizer (Claude Code's reported input tokens in headless mode with and without the fixture, because no API key exists for the token-counting endpoint, the owner review of 2026-09-27, V1; every Claude count this document calls "by the real tokenizer" is taken this way) and o200k (offline), and the owner's Codex model's reported usage (W-all-6, [90 §9.1]).
7. `git` presence on PATH, exec-form PATH resolution of `moirai.exe` for hooks (W-all-3), whether `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}` is substituted in an exec-form hook `command` and in `.mcp.json` (re-checked in M8; if it is not, `moirai hooks install` writes the expanded absolute path, [80 §2.12]), the 5-minute experiment on whether `SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop`/`PostToolUse(Agent)` fire for Workflow `agent()` calls (W-all-2), and the 5-minute `mcp_tool` experiment: an `mcp_tool` `PreToolUse` hook's `permissionDecision` honoured, `${tool_input.idempotency_key}` substituted, and whether `updatedInput` and whole-object `${tool_input}` substitution work — deciding the stamp route (§7.2); the Workflow experiment is re-run in M9 and the `mcp_tool` experiment in M10 ([70 S3]); the Bash tool's inline and failure caps of the then-current Claude Code ([73 F1], [74 A07]); and the Codex probes P1–P7, P10 and P11 of [90 §10.5] with a test-only stub server — the handshake and cold start against Codex's 1,000 ms optional-server grace, where the instructions appear, `_meta` and hook `session_id` from the main thread and subagents, truncation of single and batched results, the code-mode print idiom, hook firing, trust, sandboxing and `additionalContextLimit`, approval modes in `codex exec`, the elevated Windows sandbox against the store (which decides `integrate.codex.store-writes`), and PowerShell 5.1 argv and stdin under Codex. **Without Codex access** (V1 says only Claude Code by subscription is available, so V9 asks the owner to confirm Codex access): the Codex probes wait until access exists, and every decision they feed keeps its documented default — `integrate.codex.store-writes` stays `writable-root` (§13); P10 reports the Claude and o200k columns and skips Luna's; GT12's Codex conformance is reported as not run until then ([90 §10.5]).
8. Loose-object create + rename cost under Defender, n = 1,000, and full `git gc` time/peak RSS on a 1e5-commit image repo (decides the git object layer's loose/pack threshold and the `image gc` guidance, G28).
9. deflate throughput of `zlib-rs` vs `miniz_oxide` on this CPU (chooses the git object layer's codec, U33).
10. Tail replay of a full tail at candidate checkpoint thresholds (U13) — the threshold is chosen so that open at 1e6 with a full tail meets ≤ 3 ms (before the threshold is chosen, the 4,096-op / 4 MiB default was estimated at 3–5 ms; §4.7 states the gate at the chosen threshold). The probe decodes every record **and applies it into an overlay built to the segment and runtime-table chapters** (the sorted patch lists and id maps of the product), with a tail mix that includes the expected share of lazy runtime records at the quiet cap, because decode alone is half of a tail's replay cost ([20 §1.3], [70 S8]); the chosen threshold must hold loaded, and M1's open gate re-checks it (A1 re-review A1P-03).
11. Physical floors: data-only flush on a zero-filled extent vs append (G11), the directory flush (`durable-name`) and `FlushFileBuffers` (`durable+meta`), open + map of 8 sealed files, `HEAD` pread, spawn-to-exit of an empty Rust executable from the stable install path, directly and through the agent's Git-Bash wrapper, signed and unsigned (#39) — the baselines of the floor-relative gates ([60 §5.3]) — and a VMMap breakdown (private, page tables, shareable) and the heap-only high-water mark per process kind ([71 RAM-m9]), with the CLI's private-bytes baseline at 1e5 on `main` and on a 14-day lane against the 4 MB gate ([50 §5.12]; A1 re-review S-23).
12. Lock-release delay after `TerminateProcess` of a holder, p50/p99/max, idle and loaded (W2) — confirms the 2 s bound and parameterises lock-delay injection in the simulator and the kill loops — for the writer, flush and slot bytes.
13. BLAKE3 (feature `pure`, owner decision #44) and xxh3 throughput on this CPU, idle and loaded, through the Rust build (so far measured only under 97–100 % load, [10 §0]).
14. Layout probes — CSR probe, frozen-bitset AND/popcount, column scans and overlay probe at 1e5/1e6 — plus the T1 and T2 trigger quantities (a point read after a full tail and a delta checkpoint at 0.5 M nodes; the 16-writer wait and MCP overlay catch-up), so T1's structure and the leader are decided before M0 freezes the layouts.
15. File-system costs for R4 through the `Vfs`: stat, directory enumeration with file ids, rename of one file and of a 1,000-file directory under Defender **as the `file mv` protocol performs it** — `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` and both parents' `FlushFileBuffers` — on the project volume, idle and loaded ([09], [13]; A1 re-review A1P-07), and whether a same-volume rename updates a file's `ChangeTime` (input to the copy rule, [40 §4.3], S-16); extended by [40 §8.3.6] with `OpenFileById` on directories, the creation-time behaviour of `mv`, `cp`, Claude Code's tools and NTFS tunneling on the owner's D: volume, directory-id stability on D:, and the attribute bits of a OneDrive placeholder read without hydration.
16. The 16-agent load fixture — a recorded CPU, disk and memory profile of a real 16-agent campaign (no content; owner decision #35), replayed with free RAM held at ≈ 1.8 GB — and the run-to-run noise band of every gated quantity on the owner's laptop and on the hosted runners ([60 §5.1]).
17. **Deferred to after the release with the rig** (the owner review of 2026-09-27, V7): calibration of the OS-crash rig, **on the rig host (the owner's laptop in profile L, #34) with the exact guest image that runs GT15** (a Windows Server 2025 Core evaluation guest by default, [74 A06]): across ≥ 100 hard power-offs, a deliberately unflushed write, rename or delete is lost at least once and a barrier-protected one never is (host I/O cache off, guest flushes honoured); recorded per rig: (a) guest page-cache loss seen, (b) flushed writes never lost, (c) whether loss of issued-but-unflushed writes was ever seen (expected never — GT1 alone certifies flush placement, [74 A18]); namespace operations included, which decides whether `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` (used until then) can be dropped besides the directory flush that every OS's rename point requires ([72 M8], [80 §2.3.2]).
18. Flush-failure injection on a VHDX taken offline mid-flush: what Windows returns and what later reads see — parameterises fault-model item (3); the re-write rule of §4.5 is safe either way ([72 M1]).
19. The MCP runtime shape over stdio: private bytes and thread count of `moirai mcp` with rmcp on the configured tokio runtime, against a hand-written synchronous JSON-RPC loop; above baseline + 2 MB or 2 threads, the loop is used ([71 RAM-m2]). A decision input is a pending settle during a 16-subagent burst: the chosen shape must defer a settle's quiescence instead of sleeping ([40 §4.2]; A1 re-review A1P-06).
20. The token baseline: per-dispatch context tokens of ≥ 20 recorded dispatches today (HDR, role prompt and the plan reads of the first turns, by the real tokenizer, counted as item 6 states), and the moirai text a BoykoEngine-style session would place in agent contexts under the default config (the token ledger's first run) ([73 F8], [74 A08]).
21. One moirai build lane's and both lanes' peak RAM and CPU during a build-and-test cycle on the laptop, and the owner's intended overlap with other campaigns — sets the build cap (`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`) and the build windows of the two lanes, which both build on the laptop (#2), or shows that lane B must pause (the one-lane calendar), and re-issues the calendar ([74 A03]).
22. The OS-layer conformance probes on Windows ([80 §2.2, §2.5–§2.7]): lock bytes at 2^62 + 0…4 and 2^62 + 2^16 + 255 with one handle per role, the in-process two-client case and the probe cost; `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY` on sealed files with GC's clear-then-delete; the in-page-error handler on a mapping whose VHDX is taken offline; the `total_len` check; the environment guard on NTFS, ReFS, exFAT, a `subst` drive, a UNC path and a OneDrive folder; the boot clock (`QueryInterruptTimePrecise`) across a sleep; and the boot identity (the `BootId` counter) across a manual clock step, a sleep, a hibernation and a reboot — the reboot must change it and nothing else may (else Windows runs in Unknown-boot mode, [80 §2.7.1]).

**Reference model and baselines.** The differential oracle is a naive in-memory Rust reference model inside the project ([60 §4]), written first and complete at M0 by a separate author from the specification — `BTreeMap` graph, derived state recomputed from scratch, branch states by replay from genesis (memoised at tips and every k-th commit, ≤ 512 MB per case, [74 A21]), merges by the §5a.7 table over materialised base and side states (the recursive virtual base by definition), cross-branch exclusion by I26′'s state definition over all live refs (not by markers, [72 M4]), its own query parser, binder and nested-loop evaluator, its own canonical-form encoder (commit ids compared byte for byte), a brute-force anchor resolver for R4 — deliberately using different algorithms from the engine and sharing no code with it; its rule tables are signed by the owner; it is test-only, never linked into the binary and never a backend. A test-only format oracle decodes every frozen structure independently of the product codec. For git objects and images the independent oracle is the git CLI; for file identity it is the ground truth of the `ProjectFs` simulator. No SQLite or other third-party embedded database is built, linked or run for any purpose — not as a backend, an oracle, a benchmark or an informational reference point, in or out of tree (§2.10; enforced by the dependency lint GT20 (b), §8.3 CORRECTNESS). Performance is judged only against the absolute budgets of §8.1/§8.3 and the physical floors of item 11 (durable commit p50 ≤ flush floor + 0.5 ms, at most one flush per durable commit — exactly one for a lone writer — open ≤ 3 ms at 1e6, private RSS ≤ 4 MB per CLI).

**Workloads** (1e3 / 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6 nodes, synthetic corpora with realistic body sizes): (a) point get; (b) list by status; (c) blocking task ids; (d) transitive subtasks and blockers; (e) create/update/delete with referential cascade; (f) commit, branch, diff, sync, merge; (g) FTS; (h) 8–16 processes mixing reads and writes across branches; (i) crash tests; (j) R4 link rendering, settles and bulk checks over 1e3 / 1e4 / 1e5 linked files, including 50 links under one moved directory; (k) the LQ examples and plan-choice pairs of [50 §5.12] and [15 §4.2].

**Correctness gates.**
- **Deterministic multi-process simulation** (own workstream, ~20 % of the build; mandatory from M1, [60 §3.13]; the in-memory `Vfs` enforces the fault model frozen at M0 — any subset of unflushed sectors lost and one sector torn, a create, rename or delete durable only after `sync_dir` of its parent, with any subset of the unsynced ones lost ([80 §2.3.5]), a failed flush leaving its range returning old or new bytes, possibly changing between reads, with pages reverted, invalidated or evicted while other processes append, mixed-sector concurrent reads, several pending groups with a flush holder dying before, during or after its flush (group commit), disk-full on any write or flush, read errors, pauses of any length and wall-clock steps — and crash states enumerate **every subset** of the unflushed sectors while a file has ≤ 12 of them (4,096 states), ≥ 10⁴ random subsets beyond, both `HEAD` slots exhaustively (9 states at every barrier point), bounded products across files, and "flush error, more commits, crash" sequences — per-file prefixes stay as the fast PR tier ([72 M3]), [60 §2.5]); after every crash state it asserts **post-crash read freshness** (every read by a process started after the crash reflects every acknowledged durable record before any writer runs, [72 B1]) and compares markers and leases with the model ([72 M1]): a `Vfs` trait with an in-memory implementation, seeded scheduling of N simulated processes, crash-point enumeration at every write/flush/publish boundary — including between the two appends of a flushed group (N3) — fsync-error injection, lock-release-delay injection, AV-style sharing violations. SQLite's WAL-reset race fell to this method in ~15 minutes [08 §3.1].
- **Windows kill loops** (mandatory from M1 through the storage driver, from M3 across branch bindings, from M5 with image exports and imports, from M6 with file intents, from M8 through the CLI binary, from M10 with the MCP server held open; in three variants — `TerminateProcess`, `NtSuspendProcess` of random processes for 1–120 s, wall-clock steps of ±1 h — with every acknowledgement reported to the harness over a pipe before it is printed): 16 writer + reader processes in 16 directories bound to different branches, `TerminateProcess` at random points, 10,000 iterations / 1 h; zero lost acknowledged **durable effects of any kind** — commits, leases, ref moves, marker effects, `FsIntent` outcomes, `gitmap` entries, backups — each reported by kind to the harness ([72 M12]); zero corrupt opens, `doctor --verify` clean on every branch head; a `backup` taken during the loop restores to a store that holds every commit acknowledged before the backup's `committed_lsn` (CM8); lease variants: a claim made through the CLI survives the CLI's exit until its TTL or run end under 1,000 `ready`/`claim --next` calls, a second restricted principal yields Unknown (never an expiry), and ±1 h clock steps change no expiry ([72 B2]). In the laptop-only profile L (#34) disk-full is not a kill-loop variant, because attaching a small VHDX needs elevation on Windows Home and a nightly job never elevates ([74 A20]): it is covered by the disk-full injection of GT1 and the simulator (fault-model item 5) and by one owner-run RG7 [60 §6] drill on an owner-created small VHDX. The Unix kill loops, with `SIGKILL`, `SIGSTOP` and external truncation of a sealed file, are port gates ([80 §5.3]).
- **OS-crash loop** (GT15; **deferred to after the release** by the owner review of 2026-09-27, V7, together with the port phase, so it is not a gate of M0–M11; when it runs: ≥ 1,000 cycles, then ≥ 5,000 cumulative — the volumes it had as the M1-exit and release criteria): a Windows Server 2025 Core evaluation guest (build 26100, the Windows 11 24H2 code base; ≈ 2 GB RAM; 180 days + one rearm) in VirtualBox 7 — VMware Workstation Pro as the cross-check — with the host I/O cache off and guest flushes honoured, one guest on the rig host (the owner's laptop in its agent-free windows under profile L, #34; [74 A06]), runs GT4's per-milestone workload plus evidence-hook bursts immediately before each power-off; every writer streams each acknowledged durable effect to the host; the host triggers a bugcheck (Sysinternals NotMyFault) or a hard power-off at random; after reboot every recorded effect must be present, reads before the first writer must be fresh, `doctor --verify` clean, `git fsck --strict` clean on the image with `gitmap` ⊆ the destination, `FsIntent` outcomes equal to the file system, and the last acknowledged backup must restore ([72 M12]). The rig cannot see loss of issued-but-unflushed writes or of the drive's own volatile cache: flush placement is certified by GT1 alone, and the design assumes the drive honours FLUSH ([60 §3.13] GT15). Every format field and protocol rule this rig would test stays in format v1 and the protocol; until it runs, crash safety rests on GT1, GT3, GT4, acknowledgement only after a covering flush, `backup`/`restore` and the daily image export, and real power loss is §10 risk 17's residual risk.
- **Mutation testing** (at every milestone exit, CI only, never in a product build): all mutants in functions changed since the previous exit plus a random pooled sample of 1,000 mutants over the semantic crates; the differential and property suites must kill a point estimate ≥ 90 % with a lower 95 % bound ≥ 88 % (≈ 6–29 h of wall time per exit instead of 20–230 h for every mutant, [74 A05]); protocol bugs seeded into the real engine at M1 exit (skip the flush, publish before the flush, adopt without CAS, adopt by re-flush only, delete before the durable `HEAD` barrier, truncate past valid records, trust a lazily published `committed_lsn`, serve a pre-crash view, a single-slot `HEAD` barrier, adopt a commit without its group's markers, skip a non-commit record, a rename without a namespace barrier, an intent roll-forward without the re-barrier (A1 re-review A1P-02; seeded in M0's toy log, in the engine at M6), accept a same-epoch record at the wrong position, and the thirteen group-commit bugs of [80 §2.4.4] — among them acknowledge before a covering flush or by position without the identity check, publish past a pending durable group, re-write the pending range outside the writer byte, accept a group whose predecessor differs, publish from a stale `HEAD` snapshot, strand a lazy group behind a dead writer's group) must each be caught ([60 §3.13] GT16, [72 B1, M1, M2, M8]).
- **Property tests** (differential against the reference model of [60 §4] unless stated; every threshold is a store parameter, swept over a tiny test profile and the production values, so threshold-gated paths are compared at model scale): incremental derived state == full recompute after 1e5 random ops incl. deletes, reparents, merges; reverse CSR == inverse(forward); `invert(changeset)` restores state; pin ⊕ ops == replay-from-genesis per branch; merge is deterministic; disjoint keys commute; a clean merge equals sequential application **through the composition and re-key functions** of §5a.7 (one-sided glob and path composition, derived-uid re-keys, [72 m4]); after every `sync`, `view(lane)` = the model's `state_at(sync)` = the exported `.moi` tree, with generators that combine `path_moves` on one side with globs on the other ([72 M5]); every uid binds to exactly one `#N` over the store's life, also when two lanes link one file before any merge ([72 M7]); no structural violation ever reaches a ref (I12 fuzz); **I25′** (no conflict on keys untouched by one side) over random DAGs with interleaved `sync`/`merge`; **I26′** by its state definition (§3.4), evaluated by the model directly ([72 M4]): an oracle generator (≥ 10⁶ histories, ≥ 5 refs) drives every door — `complete`, `set --done`, `set --status`, MCP `write` (`name: tx.complete`, or a `TX` with `SET t.done = true`), `apply`, `cherry-pick`, `revert`, `merge`, `sync`, image import — and every ref move — `reopen`, `Undelete`, `undo` of a reopen or an undelete, `op restore` in both directions, forks with `branch -D` or `undo` on the parent, staging and `merge --abort`, `TX` coalescing — and compares every `ready` and `claim` with the definition (CB3); markers on branches forked from branches and absorbed vectors after `undo` (CM1); branch-of-branch merges and a lane forked from a `plan/*` branch (CM9); two lanes with staged syncs plus a third clean sync (CM5); the merge variant of X1 (one side adds `X blocks P`, the other moves `C1` under `P` and adds `C1 blocks X`); criss-cross shapes; two stores importing one bundle export identical objects including the root tree (CB2); `export → import → export` byte-identical at `commit` granularity incl. `revert`/`cherry-pick`/`undo`/`Undelete`, lanes that synced (CM2), `rm` without `--replaced-by` whose dependent must stay out of `ready` after import (CM4), and bodies with hard breaks, no final newline and CRLF at write time (CM3); gate 0 hash reconstruction for every commit kind (CB1).
- **Fuzzers**: record and segment parsers, `.moi` parser generated from the ABNF (block strings, `---` in bodies, CRLF, bodies without a final LF, body conflicts, pathological escapes, `anchor` lines, `pathmove` blocks, named-query files), R4's anchor selectors and path specs (M0), the git object layer's loose/pack/idx/commit-graph/refs/bundle readers (M4), R4's fingerprints and ambiguity generators — identical copies, mirrors, backups, near-duplicate families, splits, swaps, rename-over, Unicode and CRLF variants, cloud-placeholder attributes — with the ground truth as oracle and the assertion that no silent wrong bind occurs at the file or anchor level (M6), the query grammar with parse → print → parse identity (M7), argv and transport (M8).
- **Differential tests** against `git fsck`/`git log`/`git cat-file` on every exported image; `gix` may be used as a read oracle in tests only. R4's per-commit exact renames equal `git diff-tree -M100%` over the owner's history, and ancestry equals `git merge-base --is-ancestor` for every worktree HEAD `git worktree list` reports against trunk (46 at [41 §1]'s count, 44 at [13 §1.3]'s a day earlier), including the 7 the commit-graph misses, and answers `unknown` — never `false` — across shallow and partial clones, replace refs and grafts, which join the corpus (M4, [72 m7]). R5: the query generator emits ASTs, the reference model evaluates them, a printer feeds text to the product, and ≥ 1e6 queries across every view kind plus `TX` blocks are compared by rows, order and multiplicity or by resulting state and committed diff (M7); verb output equals named-query output for every standard query and argv form; the metamorphic `p` / `NOT p` partition; hop bounds equal brute-force walk enumeration; canonical-hash invariance under formatting, spelling, aliases, variable names and parameter order; `DRY` diff equals committed diff; `EXPECT`/`IF TIP`/`IF TARGETS` never admit a lost update and no apply touches a target outside its `DRY` set under the multi-process simulator ([72 m1]); every CLI write verb's commit equals its `TX` expansion's canonical changeset, `complete` included ([72 M11]).
- **R4 pattern matrix and properties** ([40 §8.3]; GT17 from M6, re-run through the binary in M8): rows 1–36 of the move/edit matrix (32: directory promote-replace; 33: delete, unrelated re-create and parent-directory move before a settle, [72 M13]; 34–36: case twins from another OS, identical files created in one tick, the colliding checkout that keeps the first name and writes the last content, [80 §2.11.4] rules 1–2) on the `ProjectFs` simulator and on real NTFS in scratch directories created by the harness, never in a user repository; properties P1–P15 on random operation sequences with ground-truth identity — above all **P1, no wrong automatic re-bind** (precision 1.0 for the exact class, with backup, mirror and vendored-copy generators), P2 recall 1.0 for pure moves and directory renames, P3 purity, P4 reads append nothing, P5 idempotent settles, P6 quarantine-and-restore writes nothing, P7 reader and stale trees never write, P8 merge determinism, P9 convergence by observation, P10 two stores derive the same uids, P11 anchor states equal the model's brute-force search, P12 no inferred deletion, P13 no resurrection, P14 identical directory-move composition after an image round trip, P15 patch-integrated moves never folded; crash-point enumeration of the `FsIntent` protocol with sharing-violation and delay injection and with fault-model item (2)'s namespace semantics in the simulator ([72 M8]); a GT3 scenario with two settles in one writer tree and an interleaved move in which no observation regresses ([72 M11]); the replay corpora of [40 §8.3.4] — five targets on FL-1 at M0 (the renames, the citation sample with the window tie-break, `links mentions`, the dead memory paths, the scope scanner) and the three resolver-dependent ones (the delete-then-re-add `replaced` cases, the 46 worktree HEADs' gate decisions, the transcript census's edited+moved) first in M6, where all eight re-run on the product (the owner review of 2026-09-27, A7).
- **Replay of the owner's recorded incidents** as fixtures: the union merge that resurrected RESOLVED as OPEN in 10 places and the 185→188→191 counter [02 §7.3] must yield the correct result through `sync` + `merge` and through a git-side merge of the image.


### 8.3 Budgets and gates

The owner judges every choice on four priorities — **speed, minimal RAM, correctness, minimal agent tokens** — each with an explicit budget and a test or benchmark gate. This section consolidates the budgets of §8.1 and §8.2 and those the audits [70]–[74] added, one table per priority, and is the normative list: the "Gate" column names the mechanism (the gate catalogue of [60 §3.13]; GT11 = budgets measured under the M0 protocol of [60 §5.1]; "`Vfs` counter" = counted by instrumentation, not timed), and "From" is the milestone at which the gate becomes mandatory; it stays mandatory afterwards. Engine times are warm and exclude process spawn; "loaded" means the replayed 16-agent fixture; RAM is peak private bytes (`PeakPagefileUsage`, with the heap-only high-water mark reported beside it) unless a row says steady state; M0–M11 gate Windows only, and the per-OS notes after the TOKENS table state how each gate reads on Linux and macOS in the port phase ([80 §5.3]); a policy budget is a `config` key of §13, a contract budget is a fixed test. [60 §5.4] lists the same rows by milestone.

| Idle CPU | zero CPU and context switches (ETW) | `/proc` utime/stime and context switches | `task_info` thread times |
| R4 read with 50 links under a moved directory | ≤ 5 ms p50 (`OpenFileById`) | exact at settle through the frontier; at read, `unverified (budget)` beyond `files.read-budget-ms` (P7, never wrong) | ≤ 5 ms p50 (`fsgetpath`), est. |
| GT15 rig | Server 2025 Core in VirtualBox, after the release (deferred 2026-09-27) | LazyFS + dm-log-writes + dm-flakey + QEMU/KVM | Virtualization.framework on Apple hardware |

---

## 9. Roadmap

**Built once, in dependency order (owner decisions of 2026-09-26, §2.13).** No SQLite or other third-party embedded database is built, linked or run at any stage or for any purpose — not as a backend, a stepping stone, a test oracle, a benchmark or an informational reference point, in or out of tree (GT20's dependency lint). There are no interim or throwaway stages, no temporary modes, no node caps and no reduced guarantees taken to ship sooner. Every component is built to its final specification and passes its full gates before anything builds on it; no milestone modifies a certified one except through an extension point certified earlier (the section registry of M1, the validator and merge-rule tables of M2–M3, the built-in and named-query registries of M7); milestones are ordered by technical dependency; the owner's workflow starts using moirai only when the release gate below is met. [60] holds the full plan: the dependency graph with every edge justified, per-milestone scope, exclusions, exit criteria and gates, the format-freeze list, the reference-model specification, the measurement protocol, the owner-decision schedule, the release gate, the calendar and the risks. **R4 and R5 are integrated** (§5e, §7.7) and attach to their host components rather than forming late milestones of their own ([60 §2.4] item 5): built as one late milestone, R4 would have had to modify five certified ones [61 B1]. [40 §8.1] and [50 §8.2] give the per-package detail in [60]'s order.

**Harness-agnostic work** (owner decisions #43, #44; §7.8, [90 §10.2]). M0 adds the Codex probes, the reservations of [90 §10.1], LQ-Bench on Opus 5.5 with the Claude Code and generic-client transport arms (#38 (a); Codex under the `unknown` profile), the codec decision among pure-Rust options and the cross-target type check GT20 (e) with the pure-Rust lint, mandatory from M0; M1 the chosen codec; M7 the model-profile policy, L2's refusal code, L4's replacement texts and L8's echo rule (inside LQ-2, LQ-4 and LQ-7); M8 the caller-context resolver, lease kinds and minting policy, client profiles, byte ceilings, per-harness exit-7 texts and `apply --from` with `result.v1`; M9 `moirai integrate` for Claude Code, Codex and generic C0 harnesses, the portable skill rendering, the `AGENTS.md` block, Codex hooks on the command transport, the worker-pack rule, the orchestrator-lease mint and the per-harness ledger; M10 the portable schema profile, Codex's `_meta` context and `mcp_tool` handlers, store discovery by `tree`, lazy open, release at request end and the per-thread RAM gates, and conformance in Claude Code, Codex and a generic stdio client; M11 the harness matrix in the release gate. ≈ 15–23.5 units, in the calendar below; #38 (a) drops the Luna gate runner and the floor tier from M0's lane B (≈ 0.5–1 unit), which falls on lane B's slack and stays in the figures as contingency until the M0 re-issue.

**Why the agent interface precedes MCP.** The owner's brief names the surfaces in the order CLI → MCP → skills; the build order keeps the CLI first but puts the agent interface (packs, briefs, hooks, skills; M9) before the MCP server (M10). The server's `pack` and `brief` tools and its `mcp_tool` hook handlers serve exactly the pack algorithm, brief and hook logic M9 builds, so an MCP server built first would need that logic built inside it or stubbed — an interim stage the owner ruled out. M9 therefore certifies packs, briefs, hooks and skills on the CLI's command transport, and M10 adds the MCP transport for the same logic and certifies the default `hooks.transport = auto`. Nothing is used before the release gate, so the order changes which gates run first, not what the owner receives.

**Sizes of R4 and R5** (est., [60 §3], at ≈ 3 units per 1k product lines including their tests). **R4 ≈ 68–93 units** (≈ 22–30k lines of product Rust plus 10–14k of tests, [40 §8.1]): FL-1 19–25 in M0, FL-3 6–9 in M2, FL-7 3–4.5 in M3, FL-8 2.5–3.5 in M5, the whole of M6 29–39, FL-6 ≈ 1.5 in M7, FL-5 3–4.5 in M8, FL-9 and `links import` 3–5 in M9, the MCP operations ≈ 1 in M10. **R5 ≈ 56–83 units** (≈ 15–21.5k lines in M7 plus ≈ 2.5–4k elsewhere, [50 §8.2]): LQ-Bench 3–5 in M0 (plus the model's own LQ parser, binder and evaluator, LQ-3, inside its 16–20), producers 2–3 in M2–M3, M7 without FL-6 48–68.5, LQ-8 2–3 in M8, LQ-12 0.5–1 in M9, the `query`/`TX` tools 1–2 in M10. The integration raised M7 from 46–64 to 49.5–70 units, because [60] had carried [50]'s first-revision size (Review log, integration entry). These are pre-audit baseline sizes; [60 §7.1] adds the audits', the cross-platform and [90]'s deltas per milestone (M6 31–44, M7 51–72.5).

**Calendar** (est., [22 §7.1] units at 5–8 per week — the earlier plan's rate, not yet measured; velocity is measured at the M0 and M1 exits and the calendar re-issued then). The figures include **every delta since the pre-audit baseline**, per milestone in [60 §7.1]: the priority audits' scope (the configuration system and registry, the subset crash enumerator, holder anchors, bulk commits, the three-phase write, the token ledger, GT18–GT20; est. 23–40.5 units net of §11 #41's exclusions), the cross-platform design (9.5–16.5, [80 §5.4]) and the harness-agnostic design with the pure-Rust rule (15–23.5, [90 §10.3]). ≈ 369–508.5 units (P50 ≈ 439). **The calendar of record is two supervised agent lanes, both building on the owner's laptop, in the laptop-only profile L** (owner decisions #2 and #34, 2026-09-26; re-issued on 2026-09-27 after the owner review of 2026-09-27 deferred the OS-crash rig): storage engine certified at 12–26.5 weeks, R1 at 21–45, R3 at 23.5–50, R4 at 24–52, R5 at 25.5–54.5, **release at 35–75 weeks (P50 ≈ 50.5, P90 ≈ 60.5)** — the same Monte Carlo as before with profile L's machine time placed where it falls: 2–4 laptop windows instead of 1–2 host nights at every milestone exit, and RG3's 14 nights as completed runs in agent-free windows with only the soak inside a 3-day agent freeze ([60 §3.12, §7.1], [74 A02]); GT15's ≥ 1,000 M1-exit cycles on a laptop guest (1.25–2.1 weeks) left the calendar with the rig, which brings the release ≈ 1.2 weeks earlier (P10–P90 0.9–1.6; it was 35.5–76.5 weeks, P50 ≈ 52, P90 ≈ 62). A test host (not bought) would have given 33–69.5 weeks (P50 ≈ 47, P90 ≈ 57); running the synthetic long seeds (GT1, GT3, fuzzing, sampled GT16) on the free hosted runners of the public repository (#36) would recover ≈ 2 weeks (P50 ≈ 48.5, P90 ≈ 58.5), counted only once the M0 capacity check shows they carry the load. If M0 item 21 shows that the laptop cannot carry two lanes even in the agreed windows, one lane in profile L applies: R4 at 33.5–72.5, R5 at 40–87.5, release at 51–110.5 weeks (P50 ≈ 74.5, P90 ≈ 89.5) [60 §7]. The pre-audit baseline was ≈ 321.5–428 units, two lanes 28–56 weeks (P50 ≈ 39, P90 ≈ 47.5), one lane 42.5–88 (P50 ≈ 60, P90 ≈ 73). Not included: the leader (+ 3–4 units in M1 if M0 requires it), an own zstd-format encoder (+ 5–8 in lane B if M0 item 6 chooses it) and #45's on-demand scope items; plan against P90.

**Machine capacity and infrastructure** ([74 A02, A03]; owner decisions #2 and #34). Everything runs on the owner's laptop (Ryzen 9 5900HS, 8C/16T, 16 GB): both build lanes, the owner's own campaigns and every gate that measures time, RAM, floors, crashes or kill loops; the hosted runners of the public repository (#36) carry only the synthetic build, lint, type-check and test work, which does not need the gated Windows 11 + Defender profile. **Nights.** The gates need ≈ 5 agent-free nights a week from M1 exit to release — ≈ 29–39 weeks (P10–P90) on the calendar above, so ≈ 145–195 nights (P50 ≈ 165) — with GT11 exclusive first (≤ 2 h), then GT4, GT17, GT3 and capped fuzzers concurrently (no OS-crash guest: the rig is deferred to after the release); the 1e6 and 0.3–0.5 M rows weekly; sampled mutation testing ≈ 6–29 h per exit; RG3's 14 consecutive completed runs in the ordinary agent-free windows (2.5–3.5 weeks, [60 §3.12]); and a 3-day agent freeze per release-candidate iteration for the 72-hour soak. Fuzzing (≈ 3,000 CPU-hours, ≈ 9 window-weeks at 8 threads × 8 h × 5) also runs by day at ≤ 2 targets with `-rss_limit_mb=256`, so it stays off the critical path; test infrastructure disk ≤ 25 GB on the laptop ([74 A05, A24]). **RAM headroom.** The laptop has ≈ 1.8 GB free with 16 agent processes resident [M, 05 §2.4], and a building lane needs est. 1–3 GB at a `rustc` or test-link peak, so two lanes beside a 16-agent campaign would page (§10 risk 40). Mitigation: M0 item 21 measures one lane's and both lanes' peak RAM and CPU in a build-and-test cycle; the lanes build in windows agreed with the owner, never during his benchmark windows [02 §9] and not beside a 16-agent campaign; a machine-wide build semaphore lets at most one lane link or run a test binary at a time; `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` is capped at the value item 21 sets; no `rust-analyzer` runs in the lanes' worktrees (it held 2.9 GB for BoykoEngine [M, 08 §2]); a lane's build waits while free RAM is below 1.5 GB, like the gate jobs (§8.3 RAM); day-time fuzzing pauses while a lane links; PR checks run on the hosted runners, not in the agents' worktrees. If item 21 shows that two lanes cannot fit even in the agreed windows, lane B pauses and the one-lane profile-L calendar applies (P50 ≈ 74.5, P90 ≈ 89.5); the M0 exit reports which applies.

**Release gate ("complete and hardened", [60 §6]).** Every milestone exited with all its gates; every in-release item of [60 §1.3] built; R1–R5 demonstrated end to end through the binary, hooks and MCP server; multi-process simulation nightly for ≥ 4 weeks and the kill loops in every variant with the last 14 nights clean **on the release-candidate commit** (a change restarts the gates whose covered crates' source hashes changed — storage, graph or version-control crates restart everything; image, resolver or query crates their own subsets, [74 A12]); a 72-hour soak with kill loops across branches without a lost acknowledged commit or a `doctor --verify` finding; ≥ 1e7 differential cases per component without an open disagreement and ≥ 90 % mutation kill rate in the semantic crates; ≥ 7 CPU-days per fuzzer; every budget of §8.3 met at 1e4, 1e5, 1e6 and the owner's 0.3–0.5 M scale with no number still "claimed" or "pending", the token rows on the ledger of the campaign replay; backup, restore, repair, image-restore and disk-full drills; an **upgrade drill** to a synthetic format version 2 (a derived-file change through `repair --rebuild-from-log`, a log or canonical-form change through `image export --with-oplog` → `image import`, both state-identical against the reference model, and rollback by restoring the pre-upgrade backup); a synthetic replay of a recorded campaign with zero invariant violations and zero double dispatch; a final three-lens review with no open blocker or major finding; the cutover rehearsed on a copy; no open defect of major severity. GT15's ≥ 5,000 cumulative OS-crash cycles are not part of this gate: the owner review of 2026-09-27 deferred the rig to after the release, and real power loss is a stated residual risk (§10 risk 17). The release gate is Windows'; the ports have their own gate, which reads RG1–RG12 per OS ([80 §5.3]), and never block this release.

**Cutover.** Once, at the release gate, at a campaign boundary: backup; the rehearsed import of the standing rules, current pins, live lanes and open owner questions (and of the existing file citations through `links import`, owner decision #23); the rehearsal lists every imported rule whose text still appears in `CLAUDE.md` or a role file, and the owner decides per rule whether to replace the text with the rule id, so no rule is injected twice ([73 F5]); hooks, skills and the MCP server installed; `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`/`BACKLOG.md`/`MEASUREMENT-QUEUE.md` and the MEMORY.md resume block replaced by generated views (`export md`), regenerated, never merged; the 254 memory files kept as a read-only archive linked by `artifact` nodes. Rollback removes the hooks and the MCP entry; nothing of the old workflow is deleted.

**Not built, by decision** (exclusions not tied to a decision named below — the image destinations, HTTP MCP, reftable writing, network transport without git, E2, the nudge and the `PostToolBatch` delta — are not built by owner decision #41, §11; `rebase --onto` by #5). `rebase --onto`; the tracked-directory and orphan-branch image destinations; the leader and `watch` unless the M0 measurements require the leader (then M1; group commit is in M1, leaderless, on every OS, §2.8); the `shared` field class (decision #1); HTTP MCP; reftable writing; network transport without git; live cross-machine writers (decision #11); embeddings (§12); the Linux and macOS implementations of the OS layer, their binaries, runners and crash rigs (specified and frozen in M0 by owner decision #32, built in the unscheduled port phase of §14 and [80 §5], which does not block the release); the Windows OS-crash rig — GT15, its calibration (§8.2 item 17), the guest and its licence question, the WSL2 and Memory Integrity choices — deferred to after the release with the port phase (the owner review of 2026-09-27, V7; every format field and protocol rule it would test stays frozen); tree-sitter in the product (decision #22); R4's exclusions ([40 §1.4]: re-binding outside configured roots, following a file into another repository, semantic code tracking, rewriting path mentions, gitignored build output as a target, hydrating cloud placeholders automatically) and LQ's ([50 §1.3], §12). Also not built, each with a measured revisit trigger and its reservations kept ([74 A13, A17]): R4's USN-journal evidence E2 (trigger: a bound tree on a journaled volume whose journal outlives the median settle interval); the `PreToolUse` move nudge (trigger: raw `mv` of linked files above a measured share after the skill card ships); the `PostToolBatch` delta hook (trigger: a guard-conflict rate that a mid-turn delta would have prevented); the `refs/moirai/*` project-repository image destination (trigger: decision #16 lists the project remote or #11 changes).

## 11. Owner decisions

**What is an owner decision** (the owner's rule of 2026-09-26). Only a question whose answer **no configuration key can change later** is an owner decision: the on-disk format, the branch model, merge semantics, identity schemes, the frozen query surface, the product's code boundary and scope, spending money or hardware, data leaving the machine, consent to record the owner's activity (#35: it stays local, but only the owner may allow recording it) and the owner's workflow schedule (#40: the cutover date). Every runtime and operational policy — hook switches, auto-bind policy, cloud handling, lease TTLs, budgets, pack sizes, durability classes, image destination and granularity, retention windows, merge strictness — is a `config` key or a policy-data row with a documented default that the owner changes at any time (§13); the reference model and the tests implement every allowed value. The list below is deduplicated across this document, [40 §9.2], [50 §9.2], [60 §3.14] and the audits [74 §5]; numbers are stable, so references elsewhere (#1, #2, #3, #11, #16, #20–#29) still resolve, and new decisions are appended as #32–#45 ([80]'s Review log calls two questions it withdrew "former #42" and "former #43"; those are not the decisions numbered #42 and #43 here). Each decision is due before the first milestone whose format, protocol, code or spending it can change ([60 §3.14]). **On 2026-09-26 the owner answered every open decision** (the owner's answers of 2026-09-26, binding inputs): #2, #34, #36 and #38 as the first table records, every other one as recommended ("Everything else I approve as you wrote it"). Nothing is left open before any milestone of M0–M11; #42, #45's on-demand items and #21 (d) remain for later, with their defaults recorded. **The owner review of 2026-09-27** (binding inputs) confirmed every decision (B1–B14) and amended two in the table below: #34 — the OS-crash rig is deferred to after the release (V7) — and #38 — LQ-Bench runs through the Claude Code subscription, with no API billing (V1).

## 12. Appendix: what we deliberately do not build (anti-requirements)

| Not built | Why | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A separately managed daemon or auto-started server | recurring failure in prior art; still pays the CLI spawn; lifecycle, version skew, job-object kills | [08 §5], [03 §8.2], [05 §14.2] |
| Any background activity: polling, file watchers, timers, auto-compaction on a schedule, telemetry, network calls | the owner's quiet-window idle rule; `ReadDirectoryChangesW` is lossy. Maintenance is triggered only by a command or request that crosses a threshold and ends with its work — the delta checkpoint after a write, the MCP server's ≤ 5 ms slices between requests, the detached rollup child of §4.9 — and none of it runs in quiet mode | [02 §9], [08 §4 W10] |
| A CoW B+tree / prolly-tree / Merkle-state engine for current state | 4 KiB × depth per tiny commit; hardest to build; RAM tracks history size | [04 §3.2, §6], [05 §16 B] |
| A third-party embedded database (SQLite, redb, LMDB/heed, fjall, sled, RocksDB, Turso) anywhere — product, temporary backend, stepping stone, test oracle, benchmark or informational reference point, in or out of tree | owner decision of 2026-09-26 (§2.10, §11 #2); the reference model is the oracle; performance is judged only against the §8.1/§8.3 budgets and the M0 physical floors; the `Cargo.lock` dependency lint GT20 (b) enforces it (§8.3 CORRECTNESS) | [60] |
| Interim stages, temporary modes, node caps or "useful first slices" taken to ship sooner (a serialized-access stage, a swappable backend, "v1.1" deferrals of guarantees) | owner decision of 2026-09-26 (§2.13): every component is built once, to its final specification, before anything builds on it | [60 §1] |
| Git as the engine (objects in the project ODB as the live store, or working-tree JSONL as the source of truth) | worktrees diverge; merge drivers are not cloned and GitHub ignores them; loose-object churn under Defender; Beads deleted this path | [08 §7.1], [04 §9 C] |
| Automatic moirai branches following git branches/worktrees | unstable branch identity (44 worktrees, detached HEADs, `wf_*`); silent context switches; git operations do not map to DB merges | [08 §7.2] |
| A shared "coordination plane" that never branches, or provenance-scoped knowledge instead of branches | overruled by R1 | owner update |
| A CRDT core | never-failing merges hide conflicts; whole document resident; no DAG-cycle prevention | [04 §3.11, §9 D] |
| Soft delete as a status | leaks into every filter; unnecessary in a versioned store | [06 §8.1] |
| Hierarchical or kind-prefixed ids; UUIDs shown to agents | position ≠ identity bugs; 24 tokens per UUID | [06 §9.1] |
| Query-language features outside LQ v1 (the release has LQ itself, §7.7, by owner requirement R5, which reversed this row's former "no query language"): user-defined recursive rules, procedures and UDFs, regular expressions, standing queries and incremental views, valid time, multi-statement scripts, VCS rituals as statements, `MERGE` upserts and `DETACH DELETE`, purging history, path values and path enumeration, `SKIP`/`OFFSET`, a SQL surface, ISO GQL conformance, cross-store queries | every v1 production is permanent; a daemon would be needed for standing queries; `DETACH` silently unblocks dependents (X4); path counts explode; each has an LQ alternative named by E004 | [50 §1.3], [14 §7], [15 §7], [16 §5.3] |
| A file watcher, in-file IDs in code, NTFS object IDs or alternate data streams, rewriting path mentions in repository files, mapping or holding project files open, hydrating cloud placeholders automatically (R4) | watchers are lossy and block ancestor renames; agent edits destroy object IDs; mention rewriting has a long tail of wrong edits; moirai keeps hands off the user's files (DR9, I-F11, decision #21) | [40 §1.4, §4.7], [13 §1.4], [12 L14] |
| A git process on any R4 path (except the opt-in `file mv --git`); a spawn-based fallback for ancestry or renames; tree-sitter or any C parser in the product | spawns cost 130–750 ms against a 150 ms `SessionStart` budget and would be an interim stage [41 B2]; tree-sitter fails on 23.9 % of the owner's HLSL (decision #22) | [40 §5.2, §9.2], [11 §2.9] |
| Re-binding a link on equal content alone, inferring deletion from absence, or re-binding into ignored output, the Recycle Bin or another repository (R4) | a wrong link is worse than a broken one (DR1); absence is not intent | [40 §4.1], [41 B3] |
| Embeddings or a vector index in the core; `tantivy` | model RAM dominates; lossy for agent trajectories; many files | [05 §13], [03 §6.3] |
| Storing transcripts, journals, scratchpad bulk, build artifacts, code-symbol indexes, gate runners | GBs per session; owned by the harness/LSP/graphify | [02 §13] |
| LLM-driven rewriting of stored facts (summaries that replace sources) | 7 of 13 doc-rot repairs wrote new falsehoods; summaries outlive retractions | [02 §13] |
| Textual union merges of authority-bearing records; numbers without provenance; one growing record per campaign | the owner's incidents | [02 §7.3], [02 §13] |
| Policy enforcement by blocking tool calls | the existing hooks deliberately never block; moirai answers queries, hooks decide | [02 §13] |
| A `lease` node kind; `pack` as a write; 28 node kinds; `chars/3.5` token budgets; `#N` parsing without the sigil rule | see [22 §2, §3.3] | [22] |

## 13. Configuration reference

Every operational policy of moirai is a documented configuration key with a default the owner changes; nothing in this section is an owner decision (§11). The configuration system is specified at M0 ([74 A10]):

- **Syntax.** git-config style sections and keys (`[files]` `policy.auto = exact`), UTF-8, `#` comments, parsed by a hand-written parser (≈ 300 lines; no TOML crate, T10). Frozen at M0: the syntax, the precedence, the unknown-key rule, the registry format and `HEAD.config_gen` — **not the key set**, so later milestones add keys without a format change.
- **Two scopes; each key has exactly one.** *Store* (`<store>/config`, shared by every worktree and process of the store): every key that changes versioned writes or shared behaviour, so all writers of one store behave alike. *User* (`%APPDATA%\moirai\config` on Windows, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/moirai/config` on Linux and macOS): machine-local paths and volumes, security policy for data leaving the machine, and discovery — a store restored on another machine never carries them, and a store-scope key cannot govern the discovery that finds the store. **Policy data** (role policy, delete policies, per-kind merge auto-policies) are schema rows, versioned per branch (§7.3), listed at the end.
- **Precedence**: a command-line flag > an environment variable (where the registry names one) > the key's scope file > the built-in default.
- **Typed registry.** The binary carries every key's type (bool, int with range, size, duration, percent, enum, set, path, glob list, URL list, ref), default, scope, reload class and one-line documentation; `moirai config list --defaults` prints it and the documentation is generated from it. `moirai config set` validates (exit 2 on an invalid value); an **unknown key** in a hand-edited file is a warning (exit 0), listed by `moirai config check` and `doctor`; an invalid value at read time falls back to the default and is reported the same way. `--json` results gain an additive `config` key listing the non-default values that affected the result.
- **Reload classes.** *hot*: a CLI or hook process reads config per command; the long-lived MCP server re-reads it on its next request when `HEAD.config_gen` changes (bumped by `moirai config set`; a hand edit is detected by a stat of the file at open, 17–67 µs) — zero extra syscalls per request. *restart*: takes effect when the MCP server restarts. *init*: fixed when the store or the image destination is created (recorded in `HEAD` or the destination). *install*: applied by `moirai hooks install`.
- **`moirai hooks install` registers only the hooks enabled in the effective configuration** — one `hooks.<hook>.enabled` key per hook (Hooks table), `files.hooks.evidence` and `files.hooks.edit-evidence` for the evidence hooks — each with the transport `hooks.transport` selects, removes the ones that are disabled, and `doctor hooks` reports any difference between what is registered and what config enables. The git hook blocks are installed only by the owner with `moirai hooks install --git`.
- **Sweep plan.** The reference model and the tests implement every allowed value: every key is swept one at a time away from its default, keys that interact pairwise (`files.policy.auto` × `files.deletion-inference`, `merge.strict` × `merge.policy.<kind>`, `hooks.transport` × `files.hooks.edit-evidence`, `maintenance.rollup` × the quiet keys) are swept in pairs, each hook's `.enabled` key is set to `false` one at a time with `doctor hooks` checked after each install, and the full gate volume runs on the default profile; store parameters are also swept over the tiny test profile ([60 §2.5]).

Trade-off column: **S** speed, **R** RAM, **C** correctness, **T** agent tokens.

**Discovery, paths and roots**

| Key | Type | Default | Scope | Reload | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `discovery.git-hint` (env `MOIRAI_GIT_HINT`) | bool | `true` | user | hot | S: finds the shared store from all of the owner's worktrees (44 in [13 §1.3], 46 in [41 §1]) with no pointer files; C: `false` rules out finding an unexpected hinted store |
| `default-branch` | ref | `main` | store | hot | C: the branch an unbound client writes to |
| `roots.<name>` | path | none | user | hot | C: named roots re-bind inside themselves; machine-local by nature |
| `files.main-tree` | path | the directory where `init` ran | user | hot | C: `main`'s designated writer tree |
| `files.main-ref` | git ref | the branch checked out at `init` | store | hot | C: the ref the main tree must be on to write re-binds (I-F12) |
| `files.cloud` | enum `metadata-only`\|`refuse` | `metadata-only` | user | hot | C/S: automatic paths never hydrate; `refuse` forbids links under cloud roots |

**Store parameters** (tunable ones take effect at the next checkpoint or decision point; `init` ones live in `HEAD`)

| Key | Type | Default | Scope | Reload | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `store.checkpoint.ops`, `.bytes`, `.body-bytes` | int, size, size | 4,096 (M0 item 10), 4 MiB, 32 MiB | store | hot | S: a larger tail slows every open; R: bounds the replayed overlay; S: a smaller one checkpoints more often |
| `store.tail.max-overlay-bytes`, `.max-overlay-bytes.quiet` | size | 1 MiB, 2 MiB | store | hot | R: caps every process's compact tail overlay; S: a lower cap checkpoints more often |
| `store.tail.runtime-bytes` | size | 2 MiB | store | hot | R/S: triggers the runtime-only fold of lazy R4 records |
| `store.fold-width` | int | 3 | store | hot | S: fewer delta files to probe vs more fold work |
| `store.promotion.overlay-ops`, `.overlay-bytes`, `.age-checkpoints` | int, size, int | from M0 item 3 (est. 3,000–5,000), 1 MiB, 16 | store | hot | S: lane first read ≤ 10 ms; R: overlay ≤ 1 MiB; disk: one promoted file per promotion |
| `store.commit.inline-max-bytes` | size | 1 MiB | store | hot | R: bounds a commit's private working set; larger changesets become bulk commits |
| `store.fts.tier2-nodes` | int | 20,000 | store | hot | S: postings above it, scans below it |
| `store.kahn-fallback-edges` | int | 1,000 | store | hot | S: full Kahn vs incremental Pearce–Kelly at merge |
| `store.suspect-budget` | int | 10,000 | store | hot | S: bounds a `suspect` closure; C: a violation record beyond it |
| `store.image.loose-pack-threshold` | int | 8 (M0 item 8) | store | hot | S: fewer loose objects under Defender |
| `store.pack-objects-max` | int | 65,536 | store | hot | R: bounds export's idx staging (≤ 10 MB at 1e6) |
| `store.dict.train-sample-bytes` | size | 4 MiB | store | hot | R: bounds rollup's dictionary training; ratio measured at M0 |
| `store.log-extent-bytes`, `store.hist-frame-commits`, `store.hist-frame-bytes` | size, int, size | 64 MiB, 256, 1 MiB | store | init | R: a `hist` frame never costs more than ~2 MB to decode; disk |

**Durability, quiet mode, maintenance, locks, leases, retention**

| Key | Type | Default | Scope | Reload | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `durability.lazy-kinds` | set ⊆ {heartbeat, cursor, session-mark} | all three | store | hot | S: no flush per heartbeat or cursor; C: those records may be lost after power loss, an OS crash or a failed flush (graph mutations are always durable — a rule) |
| `quiet.tail-cap-multiplier` | int | 8 | store | hot | S/R: fewer checkpoints in a benchmark window vs a larger tail |
| `quiet.from-lane-measuring` | bool | `true` | store | hot | C: a lane in `measuring` implies quiet mode |
| `maintenance.rollup` | enum `auto`\|`explicit` | `auto` | store | hot | S/R: `auto` spawns the low-priority `moirai gc` child when deltas exceed the threshold; `explicit` leaves it to the merge ritual and `gc`, with a warning in `brief` |
| `maintenance.rollup-threshold` | percent | 25 | store | hot | S: fewer delta files vs more rollups |
| `maintenance.cli-threshold-multiplier` | int | 2 | store | hot | S: agents' CLI calls rarely carry maintenance; the MCP server takes it at 1× |
| `lock.writer-wait-ms` | int | 2,000 (M0 items 2, 12) | store | hot | S/C: bounded wait vs spurious exit 7 |
| `lock.flush-wait-ms` | int | 2,000 (M0 item 2) | store | hot | S/C: bounded wait for the flush byte (group commit, §2.8) vs a spurious exit 7 with outcome `pending` |
| `lease.ttl-default`, `lease.reclaim-older-than` | duration | 15m, 30m | store | hot | C: shorter TTLs free dead claims sooner but can expire slow work |
| `gc.reflog-expire`, `gc.cruft-delay`, `gc.trash-expire`, `gc.fileobs-idle-expire` | duration | 90d, 14d, 14d, 30d | store | hot | C: undo/as-of reach vs disk |
| `idempotency.retention` | duration (≥ the longest Workflow resume) | 30d | store | hot | C: retries replay instead of duplicating |
| `idempotency.default-window` | duration | 10m | store | hot | C: a key-less retry after a crash never duplicates ([72 m5]) |
| `backup.max-age`, `image.export.max-age` | duration | 1d, 1d | store | hot | C: the age after which `doctor`/`brief` warn; the export age also triggers the `SessionStart` export and, in every harness, the `--if-older` export on the orchestrate skill's first step, `apply`, `run close` and the merge ritual (§2.15) |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hooks.transport` | enum `auto`\|`mcp`\|`command` | `auto` (`mcp_tool` in Claude Code and Codex, command hooks elsewhere) | user | install | S: `mcp_tool` handlers are spawn-free (0.1–40 ms vs one native spawn of 25–73 ms p50 under load; the command hooks' p99 gates are 110–500 ms, §8.3); `command` works without a connected server |
| `hooks.session-start.enabled`, `hooks.user-prompt-submit.enabled`, `hooks.subagent-start.enabled`, `hooks.agent-launched.enabled` (Claude Code only), `hooks.subagent-stop.enabled`, `hooks.stamp.enabled` (Claude Code only) | bool | `true` each | store | install | T/C: off removes that hook's context or safety net — no brief at session start (agents call `brief`), no prompt deltas, no role pack or auto-sync at spawn, no `agentId` map (`SubagentStop` then releases by lease only), no lease safety net (run-scoped leases are still released by `apply`), no stamp (MCP writes keep their explicit `branch`/`lease`; rights still come only from the presented lease; `hooks.stamp.permission` is not applied); the pull fallbacks of §7.5 replace each ([90 §2.5]) — the first `brief` call, the worker's `pack`, the `behind main` header and the ritual's `sync`, and `image export --if-older` on the orchestrate skill's first step, `apply`, `run close` and the merge ritual; S/T: fewer hook runs and injected tokens |

| `hooks.subagent-start.auto-sync` | bool | `true` | store | hot | C: lanes receive clean syncs automatically (D5) |

| `hooks.delta.max-commits` | int | 2,000 | store | hot | S/T: bounded prompt deltas |
