**Policy data** (schema rows, versioned per branch, hot; §7.3, [50 §6.5]): `policy.self-claim-roles` (`developer`, `tester`: the roles any caller may self-claim; a role-less self-claim is `developer`) and `policy.mint.role-lease` (`orchestrator`, `owner`: who may mint run-scoped role leases and bulk claims), [90 §4.3]; `policy.hook-label = narrow` (a design rule listed for completeness, [90 §10.8]: a hook label only narrows a lease's rights, to the intersection of the two rows, and never grants); unleased callers always get the `general-purpose` row, `policy.role.<role>.mcp-write` (yes for architect, architecture-critic, researcher, project-analyst), `policy.role.<role>.tx` (every role under the per-statement policy; node `DELETE`, `RESOLVE` and query definitions orchestrator/owner through the CLI; bulk targets orchestrator only), `policy.role.developer.fields` (the narrow §7.3 allowlist), `policy.role.<role>.define-query` (orchestrator, owner), `policy.role.<role>.authority-owner` (the owner's main session with `--owner-quote`), `edges.blocks.on-src-deleted` and `edges.gates.on-src-deleted` (`flag` | `drop-notify`; default `flag`), and `merge.policy.<kind>` (per-kind auto-resolution, opt-in).

**Never a key** ([74 §5.4]): R4's resolver-version constants (thresholds, the 50 ms quiescence, `is_text`, the case fold, the never-candidate patterns, the E3d identity rule and the E6 window bound (named `files.budget.window` in [40]'s first revision) — a different window would change which evidence a tree sees, so it is a resolver constant, not a budget); the LQ grammar, logic, counting, hop bounds and error texts; the canonical form, the `.moi` codec, hash algorithms and identity derivations; the durability of graph mutations; the typed merge rules; I32′ and [40] #10/#11; the absence of timers, threads and watchers at idle; and, per [80 §2], the durability class of each protocol point and its per-OS calls, the `LOCK` layout and lock order, the group-commit and chain rules, the liveness and boot-identity rules, and the crash-gated store allow-list.

## 14. Cross-platform: Windows, Linux and macOS

Owner decision #32 (§11, decided 2026-09-26): moirai is designed for all three OSes now; Windows is implemented and gated in M0–M11; Linux and macOS follow in a later, unscheduled port phase. [80] (revision 2, which answers its review [81]) is normative for every detail summarised here.

**The OS layer.** One crate, `moirai-os`, holds every OS-specific line: twelve modules behind the `Vfs` and `ProjectFs` seams, implementations chosen at compile time and never swapped. Every other crate is target-independent, which the OS-layer lint GT20 (d) enforces on Windows from M1; beside it, the cross-target type check GT20 (e) — `cargo check` for `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`, `aarch64-unknown-linux-musl` and `aarch64-apple-darwin` — is a gate in the local pre-merge gate and PR CI from M0 (owner decision #44; a type check only: no binary, no test), which also makes every dependency pure Rust ([90 §11]). The principles ([80 §1] X1–X9): one little-endian, 64-bit, page-size-free format; one protocol written against the weakest OS; OS kind tags on runtime rows, which another OS reads as absent; nothing silently weakened — the store is refused or the answer marked; the weakest client (sandboxed, namespaced, another principal) defines the contract; public interfaces only in product code; ports never change format or protocol.

**Per-OS mapping** (compact; [80 §2] names every call):

| Concern | Windows (built in M1–M11) | Linux (port) | macOS (port) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lock bytes (beyond EOF on `LOCK`; user-space ownership first) | `LockFileEx`; bounded wait: overlapped + `WaitForSingleObject` + `CancelIoEx` | OFD locks (`F_OFD_SETLK[W]`); bounded wait: a waiter thread | OFD locks (public SDK of macOS 14); waiter thread |
| `durable` / `durable+meta` | `NtFlushBuffersFileEx(DATA_SYNC_ONLY)` / `FlushFileBuffers` | `fdatasync` / `fsync` | `F_FULLFSYNC` / `F_FULLFSYNC` |
| `durable-name` (a directory entry) | `FlushFileBuffers` on a directory handle (`BACKUP_SEMANTICS` + write) | `fsync(dirfd)` | `fsync(dirfd)` + an `F_FULLFSYNC` barrier |
| Log extent (reads as zero beyond the tail) | zero-fill + flush | `FALLOC_FL_WRITE_ZEROES` (ext4 ≥ 6.17, XFS ≥ 7.3) or zero-fill; btrfs sparse | sparse `ftruncate` |
| Rename: no-replace; replace; swap | `MoveFileExW` (no `REPLACE_EXISTING`); `REPLACE_EXISTING`; two renames + intent | `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)`; `renameat`; `RENAME_EXCHANGE` | `renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)`; `rename`; `RENAME_SWAP` |
| Sealed files | `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY`; `MapViewOfFile` read-only; `EXCEPTION_IN_PAGE_ERROR` → exit 7 | `0444`; `mmap(PROT_READ)`; `SIGBUS` → exit 7 | same as Linux |
| Store file systems (crash-gated allow-list) | NTFS (in M0–M11 on GT1/GT3 fault-model evidence and GT4 on real NTFS; its GT15 variant with the deferred rig, §10 risk 17) | ext4, XFS, btrfs | APFS |
| Boot identity; boot clock | the `BootId` counter (+ `MachineGuid`); `QueryInterruptTimePrecise` | `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`; `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` | `kern.bootsessionuuid` (else Unknown-boot mode); `mach_continuous_time` |
| MCP server parent watch | wait on the parent's process handle | `pidfd_open(getppid())` | `kqueue EVFILT_PROC` |
| IPC (leader only) | named pipe, per-user DACL | Unix socket in a 0700 runtime directory | Unix socket in the per-user temp directory |
| Private-memory gate | `PeakPagefileUsage` | cgroup-v2 `memory.peak`, floor-relative | `ledger_phys_footprint_peak`, floor-relative |
| R4 file identity; id → path | `FILE_ID_128` (sequence-numbered); `OpenFileById` | `f_fsid` volume key, inode and file-handle digest (the generation); the changed-directory frontier | volume UUID and file id; `fsgetpath` |
| R4 enumeration; journal | `FileIdExtdDirectoryInfo`; USN (E2 not built) | `getdents64` + `statx`; none | `getattrlistbulk`; FSEvents (E2 not built) |
| R4 creation time | tunneled, not copied: corroborates only when unique | not unique: never corroborates | copied by clones: never corroborates |
| Case; normalization | per-directory case flag; normalization-sensitive | casefold directories only | per volume; normalization-insensitive (NFC stored for untracked names) |
| Temporary files; user config | `<store>/tmp/`; `%APPDATA%\moirai\config` | `<store>/tmp/`; `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/moirai/config` | same as Linux |

**Frozen at M0 for the ports** ([80 §3]): X-F1 `LOCK` layout v1 (role bytes at 2^62 + {writer, leader, maintenance, quiet, flush}; 256 liveness slots of 128 B); X-F2 anchors, `ProcId`, the boot-identity rule and Unknown-boot mode, the boot-clock lease deadline; X-F3 leaderless group commit with chained group validity (the `group_end` chain trailer), scan and re-write under the writer byte, read-modify-write publishes and acknowledgement by identity; X-F4 the lock contract (user-space ownership first) and order; X-F5 durability classes and fault-model items (2), (3), (5), (7)–(12); X-F6 the mapping policy (`total_len` in sealed-file headers) and the crash-gated environment allow-list; X-F7 the path canonical form (P1–P10 and P12, the machine-local `abs` form; `fold_v1` with full case folding at Unicode 17.0.0; per-component canonical roots found by root id); X-F8 the tagged R4 runtime layouts (`OsFileId`, `JOURNALCUR`, `DIRMAP`) and the per-OS resolver rules (twins, the unique-creation-time copy rule, whole-id identity, denials as `Unknown`); X-F9 hashed named-query file names and portable, never fold-equal ref names; X-F10 numeric store file names; X-F11 the per-OS user-scope config locations (the new keys, `lock.flush-wait-ms` among them, are registered in §13, not frozen); X-F12 the shell transport rules T1–T10. No `HEAD` field is added.

**Minimum versions** ([80 §2.13]): Windows 11 x64; Linux kernel ≥ 5.10, 64-bit, static musl (x86_64, aarch64); macOS ≥ 14, arm64 (linked for 14 and checked at open). Only the macOS floor shapes code (the public OFD API). **Store file systems on Windows:** only NTFS is admitted in M0–M11; ReFS, and with it a Dev Drive, is refused until ReFS variants of GT4 and GT15 exist (not scheduled), so every repository that holds a store stays on NTFS (§11 #15).

**The port phase** ([80 §5]): documented, not scheduled, and outside the release gate; it starts after the Windows release gate and owner decision #42 (port-phase hardware, CI and platform coverage; its Linux CI and crash layers can run on hosted Linux runners with `/dev/kvm`, free because the repository is public, #36). The same post-release phase carries the Windows OS-crash rig that the owner review of 2026-09-27 deferred (V7: GT15 with its calibration, §8.2), which never blocks the release either. It begins with probes, including the Claude Code sandbox probes, then adds the Linux and macOS implementations and their gates: `Vfs` conformance, GT1–GT4, GT11 on floor-grade hardware, GT12 shells, GT15 per OS (LazyFS, dm-log-writes and dm-flakey, QEMU/KVM; Virtualization.framework on Apple hardware), GT17 per file system, a 72 h soak and the cross-OS format-identity gate G-X. Size ≈ 52–83 units (≈ 6.5–16.5 weeks with one lane). Designing for three OSes adds ≈ 9.5–16.5 units to M0, M1, M6 and M10, included in §9's calendar (the type-check row moved from M1 to M0 by #44, [60 §7.1]). **Nothing in M0–M11 builds, runs, tests or crash-tests a Linux or macOS binary.**

---

## Review log

The completeness review of the synthesis [30] (2026-09-26) returned three blockers, nine majors and nine minors. Each is listed in the review's order with its disposition and the sections that carry the fix; "adopted" means the specification above now says it. No decision in T1–T16 was reversed (the fixes refine T3′, T5, T10, T14 and T15); every fix is local to the sections named, and the numbers of §8 were re-derived where a fix touched them (marker check, `check`/`stale`, `backup`, file count).

| Id | Severity | Finding (short) | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CB1 | blocker | canonical form hashes `hlc` and `git.base`, the trailers carry neither; `Moirai-Ref` hashed or not unstated; `Resolve`/`Violation` recovery undefined → no native commit ever verifies and the S4 gate cannot pass | **Adopted.** Canonical-field → carrier table; new trailers `Moirai-Hlc`, `Moirai-Git-Base`, `Moirai-Foreign-Git` (plus `Moirai-Origin` made explicit); `Moirai-Ref`/`Moirai-Idem` declared unhashed; the canonical op list is the *net state diff*, so a `Resolve` needs no carrier; `Violation` ops only on staging refs, never exported; gate 0 "reconstruct and re-hash" per commit kind before any corpus | §4.6, §5b.4, §5b.5 rule 8, §5b.7, §9 M0, M5 |
| CB2 | blocker | hashed `.moirai-image` carries `store-id` and `granularity`, contradicting I28′; root trees differ per exporting store | **Adopted.** Marker keeps format version, object format and schema version only; `store-id`, per-ref granularity and aliases move to the unhashed `refs/moirai/meta/<store-id>`; a commit's granularity comes from its `Moirai-Kind`; root-tree equality added to the two-store test | §5b.1, §5b.3, §5b.5 rule 7, §5b.6 step 4 |
| CB3 | blocker | `settled`/`deleted` markers written only by the `complete`/`rm` verbs; `set --done`, MCP `write`, `apply`, cherry-pick/revert, merge and import re-dispatch; `reopen`/`Undelete` never clear | **Adopted.** Markers produced by the op applier for `SetStatus{→ done\|cancelled}` and `Delete`; `cleared` markers from `reopen`, `Undelete`, `undo`, `branch -D`; every door goes through write-path step 6, imports and merges included; I26′ restated; property test drives all ten doors | §4.5 step 6, §2.16, §3.4 I26′, §5d.1, §8.2, §9 M3 *(superseded in part: since the three-phase write the markers are computed from the net ops in §4.5 step 4, [72 M4])* |
| CM1 | major | "one gen-pruned ancestry check (µs)" is really O(commits since the marker) per candidate on every `ready`/`blocking`/`brief`/`claim` | **Adopted.** Per-ref `absorbed[(ref_id, ref_seq)]` vectors maintained by commit/fork/sync/merge/undo; a marker is inert for R iff `absorbed_R[X] ≥ ref_seq`; `ref_seq` monotonic per ref and never reused; O(1) per candidate; cost restated in §3.5 and §8.1; "ready with 50 stale markers" CI workload | §3.5, §4.2, §4.3, §5a.2, §5a.4, §5d.1, §8.1 |
| CM2 | major | `sync` commits store only resolutions, but the importer diffs against the first parent; every synced lane demotes; "lossless at commit granularity" false | **Adopted.** Canonical op list of a sync = full net diff against the lane parent (hashed from the fold the sync already performs; storage keeps the G17 residue); sync exported as a two-parent git commit; `commit` granularity walks both parents; the importer subtracts `main`'s window to recover the residue; the byte-identical gate is scoped to `commit` granularity and a state-identical gate defined for `checkpoint` | §4.6, §5b.4, §5b.6, §5b.7 |
| CM3 | major | body bytes normalised (LF, trailing whitespace, one final LF) while `SetBody` hashes raw bytes; hard breaks and missing final newlines demote commits | **Adopted.** Whitespace rule scoped to the header; body encoded as bytes + one LF and decoded by stripping one LF; the only body normalisation is CRLF → LF in the store at write time, a documented store rule; `info/attributes` `*.moi -text diff`; I40′ | §5b.2 rules 1 and 7, §5b.8, §3.4 |
| CM4 | major | tombstone files carry no out-edges, so a flagged dangling blocker vanishes on round trip and its dependent becomes `ready` (X4 returns through the image) | **Adopted.** Tombstones keep retained out-edges (`flagged` marker on structural ones, historical ones as-is); the importer recreates them and `has_dangling`; corpus case; I39′ | §5b.1, §5b.2 rule 8, §5b.7, §9 M5 |
| CM5 | major | staging refs collide (`merge/main` shared by every lane's sync); one staged sync blocks every other lane's sync, the hook auto-apply and step 0 of every merge | **Adopted.** Staging on `merge/<dst>/from/<src>`; refusal scoped to the same pair; `merge --continue\|--abort [SRC [--into DST]]`; `from` and the ref-kind names reserved; I41′; two-lanes-staged test in M3 and CI | §5a.1, §5a.7 steps 0 and 8, §7.1, §8.1, §9 M3 |
| CM6 | major | store placement inside a repository unspecified; `init` in the main checkout leaves the 44 worktrees storeless (the Beads phantom-store class) and `.moirai/` in a work tree can be swept into a commit | **Adopted.** `init` defaults to `<git-common-dir>/moirai/`; `--here` only in the main worktree or outside repositories, with an `info/exclude` entry; hint step 4 also probes `<main-worktree>/.moirai`; `doctor store` reports placement, shadowing and un-excluded stores; M8 exit criterion | §2.14, §0, §1 row 4, §4.1, §5c, §7.1, §9 M8 |
| CM7 | major | v1 `check`/`stale` depend on a commit-graph git rarely refreshes and on the v1.1 pack reader; pins never verified on the daily path | **Adopted (options a + c).** v1 `check`/`stale` use the `ANCESTRY` cache, then the commit-graph when it holds both commits, else one cached `git merge-base --is-ancestor` spawn per pair inside these explicit verbs only; `doctor lanes --refresh-graph` runs `commit-graph write` on request; the "never spawns" claim restated as "never on the pack/brief/ready/blocking/get/hook path"; pack reader in v1.1 | §2.13, §2.14, §3.5, §5c, §7.6, §8.1, §9 M4, M8 |
| CM8 | major | no `backup`/`restore` verbs; unmerged lane work has no off-store copy on a development machine that has experienced OS crashes and disk-full events | **Adopted.** `backup <dir>` (pin the segment set; copy `HEAD`, sealed segments, `hist`, blobs, dictionary, `gitmap`, `config` and the log bytes up to `committed_lsn`; verified) and `restore <dir> --into` (epoch re-roll, G25) in M1; default image export covers `lane/*` at checkpoint granularity; risk 13 with a `doctor`/`brief` backup-age warning | §0, §2.13, §2.15, §5b.8, §7.1, §8.1, §8.2, §9 M1, §10 |
| CM9 | major | "each side's fold is O(its own commits)" misses the parent ref's segment for a branch forked from a branch; wrong ops and wrong base | **Adopted.** Fold defined as a DAG walk over per-ref chain segments (follow `prev_on_ref`, hop to `fork_commit`, expand sync windows by reference) bounded by the LCA, priced O(segments involved); branch-of-branch and lane-forked-from-plan shapes in the M3 property tests | §5a.3, §5a.7 step 2, §2.17 F-D11, §8.2, §9 M3 |
| CL1 | minor | `.moi` grammar slips (`label:` vs `label`, undefined `order` encoding, undefined multi-line conflict form, `.gitattributes` inside the hashed tree) | **Adopted.** `label <value>`; `order` = base-62 fractional index; `conflict <key> class=… base=… ours=… theirs=…` with JSON-escaped multi-line values and the body section omitted; ABNF + golden fixtures as an M0 format-specification deliverable, implemented in M5; attributes in `info/attributes` | §5b.1, §5b.2, §5b.6 step 1, §9 M0, M5 |
| CL2 | minor | cross-store `#N` mentions in prose re-parsed on import point at the wrong local nodes | **Adopted** as a v1.1 requirement: on import `mentions` come only from `edge mentions` lines; foreign bodies marked `origin:<store>` and their `#N` rendered through the alias map | §5b.6 step 5 |
| CL3 | minor | blob/hist file arithmetic contradicts "≤ ~12 files" and the 8-entry segment list | **Adopted.** Blob files addressed through `BLOBTAB.file`, mapped lazily on first body read, merged at every tiered fold and at rollup; the file-count statement corrected everywhere to "a dozen stable files plus O(checkpoints since the last fold) blob/hist files plus pinned sets" | §1 row 14, §4.1, §4.9, §4.10 |
| CL4 | minor | MCP `pack` default of 40,000 chars trips Claude Code's 10k-token output warning on every call | **Adopted.** MCP default 32,000 chars with `more` paging; the CLI keeps 40,000; the threshold noted in §7.2 | §7.2, §7.4 *(superseded: the CLI ceiling is `pack.cli.max-bytes` = 24,000 (was `pack.cli.max-chars`), the MCP ceiling `pack.mcp.max-bytes` = 25,000, and budgets are UTF-8 bytes, [73 F1, F3], [90 §6.2])* |
| CL5 | minor | whether `claim` performs `open → in_progress` unspecified; "released by a dead `bg_task_id`" needs polling; PID reuse | **Adopted.** `claim` = `Lease` record only; `in_progress` by `claim --start` or the first `set`/`complete`; run-scoped leases released only by `apply`, `run close`, `reclaim`; liveness compares `(pid, process start time)` at read time | §6.2, §7.1 *(superseded: leases are held by the session's MCP server through holder anchors with tri-state liveness, [72 B2])* |
| CL6 | minor | owner-decision list misses public-remote exposure, per-project vs global store, native Tasks, `run` granularity; #6 and #8 are engineering choices | **Adopted.** Decisions 16–19 appended with recommended defaults; #6 narrowed to the permission posture; #8 narrowed to "prose in the repository" | §11 |
| CL7 | minor | ledger attributes `plan/*` to the owner; recommends moving it to v1.1 | **Adopted in part.** Attribution corrected (re-planning branches come from [04 §9.3]/[22]); `plan/*` **kept in v1** because under op-level markers (CB3) its write mask is the one cheap guard that stops a what-if experiment from writing `settled`/`deleted` markers that would block dispatch on every branch (X13); `PlanStatusIgnored` dropped since the mask makes it unreachable, so M3 gains no merge rule from it | §2.7, §2.17 D7 and X13, §5a.7, §5a.8 |
| CL8 | minor | reverting a merge refused; `undo` also removes later commits | **Adopted.** `revert <merge> --mainline 1` through the exact inverse and the validators; `--mainline 2` refused; reverting a `sync` still refused (residue-only storage) and the `moirai-branches` skill says so | §2.7, §5a.5, §7.1, §7.5 *(superseded: `moirai-branches` is folded into `moirai-orchestrate`, [73 F9])* |
| CL9 | minor | slice sizes never summed; the S3-before-next-campaign dependency unstated | **Adopted** then; **superseded on 2026-09-26**: the slice calendar and its campaign fallback were removed with the slices; §9 now carries the dependency-ordered calendar of [60 §7] (release gate at ≈ 28–56 weeks with two lanes, P50 ≈ 39) | §9 |

**Editorial corrections made while revising** (not raised by the review): the `blocks` and `gates` rows of §3.3 had their "on dst deleted" and "on src deleted" cells swapped relative to T5, X4 and §5d.3 (the flagging rule applies when the *blocker* — the edge's source — is deleted; deleting the dependent simply drops the edge); corrected. The orphan-parking ref of §4.5 step 2 was named `merge/<ref>`, colliding with the staging namespace; it is now `orphans/<ref>`. `Marker` records gained the `cleared` kind everywhere they are listed (§4.2, §4.3, §4.4, §5a.1, §6.5).

**Owner decisions of 2026-09-26 — no third-party database; built once, in dependency order.** The owner decided (verbatim translations): "No, we do not use SQLite — we build our own [engine] right away." and "'So that you can start using it as early as possible' is not important — it must be done properly right away." This withdrew decision #2's former default (the slice plan S0–S6: S1–S5 on a throw-away SQLite backend behind an engine trait, the engine swapped in at S6) and then an adoption-driven engine-first draft (a serialized-access stage used as a tracker under a node cap, the lock-free protocol later). The plan of record is now [60] issue 2, revised after the adversarial review [61] of its first issue: no third-party database at any stage; every component built once to its final specification, in dependency order M0–M11, each passing its full gates before anything builds on it and extending certified milestones only through extension points certified earlier; the on-disk format frozen at M0 with every R1/R3 reservation, the R4/R5 reservations of [40]/[50] (a slot in §4.6), the `Vfs` fault model and store parameters for every threshold; the evidence that can change the format (the query accuracy benchmark, R4's replay corpora, the T1/T2 trigger quantities, the gate-0 carrier table) gathered before the freeze; the complete Rust reference model, written first, as the differential oracle, with its own query parser and canonical-form encoder and owner-signed rule tables; the git CLI as the independent image oracle; an OS-crash loop and mutation testing among the gates; a measurement protocol frozen at M0; the owner's workflow starts using moirai only at the release gate, which includes a format-upgrade drill. Scope consequences: branch promotion, `op log`/`op restore`, the recursive virtual merge base (amending I31′, because the newest-LCA rule can take one side's criss-cross resolution silently; a key whose virtual-base value is a conflict value is clean when both sides agree), FTS tier 2, schema strengthening, `--with-oplog`, SHA-256 images, bundles and an in-process git object layer (replacing D10's two-backend plan and the `check`/`stale` spawn of CM7) are in the release; the leader is built only if the M0 measurements require it; the `shared` field class and decisions #5, #8 and #17 are due before M0. R4 and R5 appear as marked slots (the binding inputs, §1 rows 16–17, §2.11, §4.6, §8.1, §9, §12) that point to [40] and [50]; their integration is a later step, required before M0 exits. The finding texts above, and the CM7, CL2 and CL7 dispositions, record the positions before this amendment; CB3, CL4, CL5 and CL8 are marked where later passes superseded them. Edited: header, sources, how to read, §0, §1, §2.1, §2.2, §2.3, §2.7–§2.11, §2.13, §2.14, §2.15, §2.17, §3.5, §4.1, §4.4, §4.6, §4.8, §4.10, §5a.3, §5a.5, §5a.7, §5a.10, §5b.2, §5b.4–§5b.9, §5c, §6.1, §6.3, §6.5, §7.1, §7.4–§7.6, §8.1, §8.2, §9, §10, §11 (introduction, #1–#3, #5, #11, #15, #17), §12 and this log's pointers. T4–T6, T12, T14's discovery rules and T16 are unchanged.

**Integration of R4/R5 (2026-09-26).** The R4 design [40] (revision 2, which answers its review [41]) and the R5 design [50] (revision 2, which answers its review [51]) are integrated, replacing every slot the previous entry left: the binding inputs, §1 rows 16–17, §2.11, the reservation paragraph of §4.6, §8.1, §9 and §12. Two sections are new — **§5e** (move-proof file links: mechanism and guarantee, data model, resolution and settle, worktrees and branches, explicit verbs and the intent protocol, merge/history/image, git independence and accelerators, invariants I-F1…I-F14, placement and size) and **§7.7** (the query language Lachesis: the language, verbs as named queries, interfaces, execution and budgets, LQ-Bench, reservations and placement) — and the consequential edits of [40 §8.4] and [50 §10] were applied to the sections they name. [40] and [50] stay normative for detail; where this document summarises them, it points to the section that governs.

**Contradictions found and how they were decided.** [40] revision 2 had been written against [50]'s first revision and [60]'s first issue; [50] revision 2 against [40]'s first revision and [60]'s first issue; [60] issue 2 carried [40]'s and [50]'s first revisions. Each disagreement was decided on the evidence and fixed in every document that carried it:

| # | Contradiction | Evidence | Decision | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Directory moves**: [60 §2.5] (from [40] rev 1) reserved an op `PathPrefix`, a hashed canonical-form **item 11** and a trailer `Moirai-Path-Prefix`; this document's §4.6 slot left the choice open; [50 §3.10, §4.2] still named a `PathPrefix` op; [40] rev 2 made directory-move history versioned state | [41 B4]: an event inside a canonical form that is defined as the state diff against the first parent breaks CM2's one reconstruction rule, is lost when checkpoints fold commits, and needs a carrier of its own; as an add-wins set field on a root node it survives folding, git-side edits, revert and cherry-pick and composes deterministically at merge | the root node's versioned `path_moves` field; **no op, no item 11, no trailer, no commit annotation**; the canonical form keeps items 1–10 | [AR] §4.6, §5b.2 rule 9, §5b.4, §5e.2, §5e.6; [60] §2.5 (R-5, R-10), §3.6; [50] §3.10, §4.2 |
| 2 | **Value-type name**: `digest` in [60 §2.5] (from [40] rev 1, whose review log in [60] also "withdrew" an `oid` type) against `oid` in [40] rev 2 | identical layout (algo u8 + 20 or 32 B); [40] rev 2 names the field `oid`, stores git blob and commit ids in the same type, and follows git's vocabulary | `oid` | [60] §2.5 R-1; [AR] §3.1 |
| 3 | **Reservation sets**: [60 §2.5] and this document's slot carried R-1…R-14 (I-F1…I-F11; `uid_derivation` without `root-key`; no `FsIntentDone`/`FsIntentAborted`/`PrefixEv`/`GitFacts`; no `PREFIXEV`/`GITRENAMES`; no R-15…R-18) and F1–F15 | [40] rev 2 §2.11 (R-1…R-18) and [50] rev 2 §8.1 (F1–F18, after [51 M7, m8, m9]) are the reviewed revisions; [60]'s rule P9 makes the designs authoritative | R-1…R-18 and F1–F18, placed row by row in §4.6 | [AR] §3.1, §3.4, §4.1–§4.6; [60] §2.5, §2.6 |

**Consequential edits that resolve no contradiction.** §3.5 splits `ready` into the persisted structural `unblocked` and the read-time `ready` (§4.4's bitset follows); I1, I14 and I18′ are amended and I42′, I43′ added; §4.3 gains `SetEdgeProps`, `Schema{query}`, the discriminator and the unhashed `stmt_*`, `append_hlc` and `affected_complete`; §5a.7 gains the R4 and named-query merge rows, §5a.8 `PathClaim`, `QueryInvalid`, `QueryCycle`; §5c names a fourth optional git spawn (`file mv --git`); §6.4 binds idempotency keys to the canonical bound AST; §7.1's contract makes ids bare in argv, drops `@file` for `-f`/`--stdin`, adds exit code 10, the `q`/`tx` and file/link verbs and three example sessions, and command snippets elsewhere (§5d.3, §6.2, §7.4, §7.6) now use bare ids; §7.3–§7.5 gain the per-statement policy, the R4 role rows, link rendering in packs, the `moirai-ql` card and the R4 hooks; §8.1–§8.2 gain the R4 and R5 budget rows, CI gates, measurement 15's extension, the pattern matrix, the R4 properties and the LQ differential tests; §10 rewrites risk 18 and adds risks 21–27; §12 replaces the query-language row and adds three R4 rows. The other documents: [40] (header, §2.9, §2.11, §4.7, §6.3, §6.5, §8.1, §8.4, §9.2, Review log), [50] (header, §0.1, §1.3, §2.5, §2.6, §3.10, §4.1, §4.2, §6.3, §8.2, §9, §10, §12.4) and [60] (header, §0, §1.2, §1.3, §2.1, §2.4–§2.6, §3.1, §3.3, §3.6–§3.8, §3.13–§3.15, §7, §8, §9.3, §9.6, §10.4).

**Left open, deliberately.** (a) Neither revision has been re-reviewed; the M0 specification review covers both and this document's integrated text (risk 18). (b) Decisions #4, #6, #12 and #14 of §11 are operational policy under the owner's rule and belong in `config` like the R4/R5 calls; reclassifying them is outside this integration. (c) [60 §9]'s historical edit list (E1–E144) is not regenerated; its §9.3 and §9.6 record that the integration superseded the slot edits.

Edited: header, sources, for implementers, binding inputs, §0, §1 (rows 1, 2, 8, 9, 16, 17), §2.2, §2.10–§2.12, §2.16, §3.1–§3.5, §4.1–§4.9, §5a.1, §5a.4, §5a.6–§5a.8, §5b.1, §5b.2, §5b.4–§5b.7, §5b.9, §5c, §5d.1, §5d.3 (command snippets), §5e (new), §6.2 (command snippets), §6.4, §6.6, §7.1–§7.6, §7.7 (new), §8.1, §8.2, §9, §10, §11, §12 and this log. T1, T3′–T9, T13–T16 are unchanged in substance.

**Priority audits (2026-09-26): final editorial pass.** Five audits judged the integrated design on the owner's priorities — speed [70], minimal RAM [71], correctness [72], minimal agent tokens [73] — and on feasibility, scope and the configuration boundary [74]. Together they raised 6 blockers, 45 majors and 47 minors, plus 50 decision classifications. **Every blocker and major is fixed in the section it concerns; no finding is rejected as a whole**; the sub-items not adopted, and the places where two audits proposed different fixes, are listed after the tables. Every minor is applied. No owner decision was reversed or taken on the owner's behalf: the fixes stay inside the owner's rules (own engine, no third-party database anywhere, everything built once to its final specification, operational policy in `config`). The format items the fixes add are listed in §4.6 and carried in [60 §2.5]. [40], [50] and [60] were edited in the same pass (their review logs record it). Disposition: **A** adopted as proposed; **A\*** adopted with the modification stated.

*Speed [70]: 1 blocker, 8 majors, 9 minors — 18 fixed, 0 rejected.*

| Id | Sev. | Finding (short) | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | blocker | a lane view replays every `main` op of its synced windows; promotion counts only own ops | **A.** O(1) `overlay_ops`/`overlay_bytes` counters in the ref table incl. synced windows; promotion when they cross thresholds derived from the 10 ms gate (M0 item 3) and 1 MiB, by a sync that crosses them (in the syncing process after release), at checkpoints and after rollups; daily-sync fixture in M0 item 3 and the M1/M3 gates | §2.3, §4.2, §5a.3, §5a.10, §8.1, §8.2 item 3, §8.3; [60] |
| S2 | major | heavy work inside the writer byte | **A.** Three-phase write for every verb: compute against a snapshot before the lock (merges, syncs, `TX` `MATCH`, `rm` closure, settles with their quiescence), re-validate by key and re-parent in O(1) under it (`changeset_digest`), print and maintain after; hold p99 ≤ 5 ms per verb class | §2.2, §4.3, §4.5, §4.6, §5a.7, §6.1, §7.7.1, §8.3; [50 §5.9]; [60] |
| S3 | major | `mcp_tool` hooks can remove most hook spawns | **A.** `hooks.transport = auto` (§13): every per-event hook except `SessionStart` at launch is an `mcp_tool` handler on the session's server; stamp by server-side context keyed on `(session, idempotency key)`, or `updatedInput` if the M0 experiment confirms it; cursors in server memory; experiment in M0 item 7, re-run in M10 (§7.5; the Workflow experiment re-runs in M9). Sub-item (6) — Bash roles reading through MCP — not adopted: it is a token trade-off, and [73 F6] defers every schema | §1 rows 9–10, §2.9, §7.2, §7.5, §8.2 item 7; [40 §4.7, §6.4]; [60 §5.2] |
| S4 | major | `MARKERS` holds every marker ever; scans O(history) | **A.** Globally inert markers move to a cold `MARKERS_OLD` at every checkpoint fold; `brief_triage` ≤ 50 µs with 90k inert markers (format item) | §4.4, §4.9, §5d.1, §7.7.4, §8.3; [50 §5.12] |
| S5 | major | `dirty N` and other lanes' `files_owned` are O(tracked files)/O(lanes) per read; `lane.dirty_files` versioned | **A.** `dirty_files` removed from the `lane` schema; runtime `TREES.dirty` row written only by settles, `links sync`, git blocks and the edit-evidence hook, printed with its age; `files_owned` globs captured into the lease row (format items) | §3.2, §4.4, §5e.4, §6.2, §7.1, §7.4 C1 |
| S6 | major | read-path git work unmetered; `staleness()` computed in every pack | **A.** Git objects charged to `fs` units; reads use `GITFACTS`/`ANCESTRY` plus ≤ 1 uncached pair and ≤ 32 E6 commits, else `unverified (git)`; `staleness()` cache-only on pack/brief; gate ≤ 10 ms p99 | §2.14, §5e.3, §7.4 C6, §7.7.4, §8.3; [40 §4.3, §4.8]; [50 §2.6, §4.3, §5.10] |
| S7 | major | edited linked files re-hashed and re-anchored by every CLI read | **A.** Runtime `ANCHORRES` table (format item) written by settles and the spawn-free edit-evidence hook (`files.hooks.edit-evidence = auto`); gate: 50-link pack with 10 edited files ≤ 5 ms p50 | §3.5, §4.3, §4.4, §5e.3, §5e.7, §7.5, §8.3; [40] |
| S8 | major | settles write a record per verified link; `SessionStart` always spends its cap | **A.** One `TreeReg` epoch per settle, `FILEOBS` rows only on change, `verified_at` = max(row, covering epoch); `SessionStart` scope = brief + lane `files_owned`, others after `files.settle.others-after` (24 h); per-directory enumeration | §4.3, §5e.3, §7.5; [40 §2.6, §4.2] |
| S9 | major | the MCP server blocks all subagents behind unsliced work | **A\*.** Checkpoints, promotions and settles as resumable ≤ 5 ms slices; `links_sync` ≤ 200 ms per MCP call with a cursor; merges computed before the lock; **rollup never in the server** (it spawns the low-priority `moirai gc` child, [71 RAM-M3]) instead of a sliced in-server rollup; gate p99 ≤ 25 ms, max ≤ 100 ms under a burst | §2.2, §4.5 step 12, §4.9, §6.1, §8.3 |
| S10 | minor | fixed per-invocation file opens unbudgeted | **A.** Open restated as floor + discovery + tail; `Vfs`-counted opens ≤ 10/14/18; `packed-refs` only when the loose ref is absent | §4.7, §8.1, §8.3 |
| S11 | minor | incremental export ignores rename cost | **A.** One `packed-refs` transaction per run in bare image repos; stated flush order; budget ≤ 50 ms | §5b.6, §5b.9, §8.3 |
| S12 | minor | prompt delta: unbounded scan, writer-byte append | **A.** ≤ 2,000 commits scanned; try-lock cursor advance; server-memory cursors | §5d.1, §6.3, §7.5 |
| S13 | minor | flush grouping per verb unspecified | **A.** One flushed group per verb (`lane open`, sync-first merge) | §4.5, §5a.7, §7.6, §8.3 |
| S14 | minor | 50-link gate at its limit under load | **A.** Per-directory enumeration; ≤ 3 ms idle / ≤ 5 ms loaded | §3.5, §8.1, §8.3 |
| S15 | minor | maintenance in an agent's CLI tail | **A\*.** Server at 1×, CLI above 2× (`maintenance.cli-threshold-multiplier`); a sync crossing the promotion threshold still promotes in its own process (S1) | §4.5 step 12, §6.1 |
| S16 | minor | one 1 s hook gate too loose | **A.** Per-hook budgets (command and `mcp_tool`) | §7.5, §8.3 |
| S17 | minor | lane pack recomputes diff, staleness and glob scans | **A.** Lane pack budget ≤ 12/20/30 ms; `~main` set cached per (lane tip, `main` tip); `GLOBIDX` (format item); cache-only staleness | §4.4, §7.4, §8.3 |
| S18 | minor | BM25 statistics and schema load per process | **A.** Budgeted (parse + bind + plan ≤ 200 µs with the first schema load; lane `find` 1–3 ms); kept warm in the server | §8.3; [50 §5.12] |

*RAM [71]: 1 blocker, 6 majors, 9 minors — 16 fixed, 0 rejected.*

| Id | Sev. | Finding (short) | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RAM-B1 | blocker | commit size unbounded; one commit can hold the whole store | **A.** `store.commit.inline-max-bytes` (1 MiB); bulk commits in sealed `cs.NNNN` changeset segments mapped as a delta layer; canonical hash over a streamed `changeset_digest` with a per-uid key order matching `.moi`; `hist` frames ≤ 256 commits and ≤ 1 MiB raw; batched `links import`; gates (format items) | §4.1, §4.3, §4.6, §4.9, §5b.6, §8.3 |
| RAM-M1 | major | synced lane overlays carry `main`'s windows; old bases duplicate the hot index | **A.** Merged with S1: overlay-driven promotion at 1 MiB, promotion on sync and at checkpoints, re-basing after rollup (≤ 2 bases) | §2.3, §5a.3, §4.9, §8.3 |
| RAM-M2 | major | MCP servers multiply; count-bounded LRU; heap retention | **A\*.** `mcp.overlay-bytes` (4 MiB) as the bound with 8 as the count ceiling (not [74 A09]'s K = 4, which bounds count, not bytes); region arenas of `VirtualAlloc` chunks; steady-state, peak and aggregate gates (16 idle Σ ≤ 128 MB; everything Σ ≤ 256 MB) | §2.2, §6.1, §8.1, §8.3 |
| RAM-M3 | major | rollup inside the MCP server | **A.** Rollup only in a `moirai gc` process, spawned detached at low CPU, memory and I/O priority; streaming rollup ≤ 24/24/32 MB; `maintenance.rollup = auto \| explicit` | §2.1, §4.9, §6.1, §12, §13 |
| RAM-M4 | major | reading a project file is a whole-buffer operation | **A.** Fixed 128 KiB buffers, two-pass `oid`, chunked anchor scan, `files.max-line-hashes`, `files.max-read-bytes` redefined, ≤ 2 `--deep` content readers; gates | §5e.3, §8.3; [40 §2.5, §7.3] |
| RAM-M5 | major | quiet-mode tail and lazy runtime records inflate every replay | **A.** Compact overlay (≤ 60 B/op); `store.tail.max-overlay-bytes` (1 MiB, 2 MiB quiet); runtime records indexed, not decoded; `store.tail.runtime-bytes` runtime-only fold | §2.2, §4.3, §4.5, §8.3 |
| RAM-M6 | major | write paths have no memory budget | **A.** `wmem`; copy-on-write candidate; bodies compressed at seal time (or small-window parameters if M0 requires); `hooks.sync-auto-keys` (2,000); merge/sync/`TX` gates | §4.3, §4.5, §5a.3, §5a.7, §7.5, §8.3 |
| RAM-m1 | minor | git object layer has no cache bound | **A.** Byte-bounded delta-base LRU (256 KiB / 1 MiB), streamed large objects, per-commit E6 | §5b.6, §8.3, §13 |
| RAM-m2 | minor | MCP front end not thread-free; rmcp RSS a claim | **A.** ≤ 2 OS threads; runtime shape probed at M0 (item 19) with a synchronous JSON-RPC loop as the alternative; idle measured from 15 s after the last request | §2.2, §2.10, §8.2, §8.3 |
| RAM-m3 | minor | one pack per run; import unbudgeted; `gitmap` order | **A.** Packs of ≤ 65,536 objects; `gitmap` sorted with a fan-out table; import budgets | §4.1, §5b.6, §5b.9, §8.3 |
| RAM-m4 | minor | full-recompute paths unbudgeted | **A.** Chunked `doctor --verify`; repair through the checkpoint pipeline; Kahn derives implied edges on the fly | §4.10, §5a.7, §8.3 |
| RAM-m5 | minor | bulk paths pollute the page cache | **A.** `MEMORY_PRIORITY_LOW`, sequential scan, unbuffered backup copies; reported at M11 | §4.9, §8.3 |
| RAM-m6 | minor | markers, symbols, per-run actors grow with history | **A.** `MARKERS_OLD` (S4) with pruning after `gc.reflog-expire`; `SYMTAB` probed in place; `actor` widened to u32 (format item) | §3.1, §4.3, §4.4, §4.7 |
| RAM-m7 | minor | pack bodies accumulate in one arena | **A.** One reused 64 KiB body buffer | §4.7, §7.4 |
| RAM-m8 | minor | stack depth unspecified | **A.** Grammar nesting ≤ 64 with an E-code; iterative PK, closures and walks with heap stacks charged to `mem`/`wmem`; 256 KiB worker stacks; depth fuzz targets ([50 §5.2]) | §8.3 GT5; [50] |
| RAM-m9 | minor | RAM gates cover two process kinds at one scale | **A.** §8.3 RAM table; `heap_peak`; VMMap breakdown at M0 and M11 | §8.2 item 11, §8.3 |

*Correctness [72]: 2 blockers, 13 majors, 8 minors — 23 fixed, 0 rejected.*

| Id | Sev. | Finding (short) | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | blocker | the unflushed `HEAD` is trusted as a recovery and visibility bound after an OS crash | **A.** `HEAD.durable_lsn` and `boot_id` (format items); boot-change recovery and one `HEAD` flush before any read; recovery from `min(durable_lsn, checkpoint_lsn)`; an invalid record above `durable_lsn` ends the log, one below it is corruption; readers stop at it; freshness gates | §2.8, §4.2, §4.5, §4.7, §4.10, §8.2, §8.3; [60 §2.5] |
| B2 | blocker | lease liveness names no process; CLI leases die on exit; reboot and clock semantics | **A.** Holder anchor = the session's MCP server slot in `LOCK` (format items); the CLI never records itself; tri-state liveness, Unknown never ends a lease; wall + (boot, monotonic) deadlines; reboot releases non-run leases; same anchor for `FsIntent` recovery; GT4 variants | §2.16, §4.1, §4.4, §5d.1, §5e.5, §6.2, §8.2, §8.3 |
| M1 | major | adoption re-flushes, covers only commits, no group atomicity | **A.** Re-write every complete record in `(durable_lsn, end]`, applied by kind; `RecHdr.group_end` (format bit); position check; markers from net ops; M0 VHDX flush-failure measurement (item 18) | §4.3, §4.5, §4.10, §8.2 |
| M2 | major | the `HEAD` barrier flushes one slot | **A.** Both slots written and flushed; recovery rebuilds the segment set from durable `Checkpoint` records; 9-state gate | §4.2, §4.9, §8.3 |
| M3 | major | GT1 enumerates prefixes only | **A.** All subsets ≤ 12 unflushed sectors per file, ≥ 10⁴ random beyond, both slots exhaustively, cross-file products; prefixes as the PR tier; RG3 [60 §6] statement now true | §8.2, §8.3; [60 §3.1, §3.13] |
| M4 | major | marker lifecycle is event-based; the model shares the flaw | **A.** I26′ defined on states; markers a cache keyed `(#N, ref_id, commit)` with scoped `cleared`; `undo`/`op restore` recompute, `-D` re-attributes, staging emits none, net ops; the model evaluates the definition; state-oracle gate at M3 (the rule table is part of the owner-signed tables at M0) | §0, §2.16, §3.4, §4.3, §4.5, §5a.5, §5a.7, §5a.9, §5d.1, §8.2; [60 §4.2] |
| M5 | major | one-sided glob composition vs the G17 residue | **A.** Residue = every key whose merged value differs from `main`'s window alone; window ⊕ residue asserted at sync and by `doctor --verify`; equivalence gate | §4.3, §4.6, §5a.3, §5a.7, §8.2; [40 §5.5] |
| M6 | major | `image.anchor-text = hash-only` changes hashed content | **A.** The canonical form hashes anchor text through BLAKE3-128 digests carried on every anchor line; full mode verifies text; hash-only imports yield `text-unavailable`; I28′ restated; both modes in GT8 (format items) | §3.4, §4.6, §5b.2, §8.3; [40 §5.7, R-10, R-11] |
| M7 | major | no store-wide uid → `#N` index | **A.** `ALLOC` with uid and a `UIDX` section (format items), probed under the writer byte, used by import, rebuilt by repair; uniqueness gate at M2 | §2.16, §3.4, §4.4, §4.5, §4.8, §5d.1, §8.3 |
| M8 | major | file verbs and store renames lack namespace barriers | **A.** `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` for `file mv`; directory flush after deletes and temp → final renames; `sync_dir` in `Vfs`/`ProjectFs`; rig calibration covers namespace operations; `doctor` checks intents after a boot change | §4.10, §5e.5, §8.2 item 17; [40 §3.4, §3.5] |
| M9 | major | image export and backup acknowledged before their bytes and names are durable | **A.** Stated durability order; each export verifies the newest `gitmap` entries first; `backup` flushes and appends a `Backup` record; GT15 checks | §4.3, §4.10, §5b.6, §8.2 |
| M10 | major | import onto a locally diverged ref unspecified | **A.** Stated parents kept; divergent chains land on `import/<ref>` and merge by the typed rules; `image.import-merge = auto \| stage` | §5b.6, §7.6, §13 |

**Where the audits disagreed, and how it was decided.** (1) *Rollup* — [70 S9] sliced it inside the server, [71 RAM-M3] moved it to a spawned child, [74 A09] to explicit `gc` only: the child is adopted because its peak (17–35 MB) exceeds the server's whole gate whatever the slicing, and it keeps rollups happening without an operator; the merge ritual also runs `gc --rollup --if-needed`, and `maintenance.rollup = explicit` gives A09's behaviour. (2) *MCP overlay bound* — [71] bytes (4 MiB) with K = 8, [74] K = 4: the byte bound is what RAM needs; K stays 8. (3) *Aggregate RAM* — [71]'s per-kind aggregates (≤ 128 MB idle servers, ≤ 256 MB everything) replace [74]'s 16 × (10 + K). (4) *Token bound through MCP* — [73 F3] weighted units, [74 A07] a separate token key: one mechanism (units) serves both transports. (5) *Duplicate rules* — [73 F4] a lazy session mark, [74 A08] a rule revision in the dispatch marker: the hook cannot see the prompt, so the mark is used. (6) *Promotion placement* — [70 S1] promotes in the syncing process, [70 S15] keeps maintenance out of CLI tails: a sync that crosses the promotion threshold promotes in its own process (a sync is not on an agent's read path), all other maintenance follows S15. (7) *Rollup RAM budget* — [71] ≤ 24/24/32 MB (streaming) over [74]'s est. ≤ 16/64 MB. (8) *Sample sizes and `files.budget.window`* — the E6 window bound is a resolver constant (R-14, purity), not the key [40 R-13] listed; the time budgets are the keys `files.read-budget-ms`, `files.session-start-cap-ms` and `files.links-sync-ms` (§13).

**Owner decision #32 (2026-09-26): cross-platform design.** The owner decided (verbatim translations): "Take into account that the system must work under macOS and Linux too." and "Don't build binaries for Linux and Mac for now, just take into account that this will need to be implemented. But we are not testing this for now — there is no possibility." moirai is designed for Windows, Linux and macOS now, with one on-disk format and one protocol semantics that map natively to all three, frozen at M0; OS differences live only in the OS layer; build, CI, test and crash gates stay Windows-only in M0–M11; the Linux and macOS implementations are a documented, unscheduled port phase that does not block the release. [80], built from [X17]–[X20] and revised after its review [81] (2 blockers, 7 majors, 15 minors, all fixed, none rejected), is integrated here and summarised in the new §14; it resolves [74 A01].
- **Format and protocol, frozen at M0 (X-F1–X-F12):** `LOCK` layout v1 (lock bytes beyond EOF, a flush byte, 256 × 128 B slot records); the 32-byte anchor with a nonce, an intent anchor for `FsIntent`, `ProcId` diagnostics and a boot-clock lease deadline; a boot identity that is constant for one boot and invariant under clock steps, suspend and hibernation (the Windows boot time moved with the clock, [81] M2), with an Unknown-boot mode instead of a refusal ([81] M3); **leaderless group commit through a flush byte**, replacing T8's serial flush inside the writer byte (on Apple silicon `F_FULLFSYNC` costs 3–20 ms, so serial flushing would put a 16-writer burst at 50–320 ms), with each group's checksum chained to its predecessor through a `group_end` trailer, the pending range scanned and re-written under the writer byte, every publish a read-modify-write of the newest `HEAD` slot folding the covered groups' effects, and every acknowledgement checked by identity ([81] B1, M6, m4); the lock contract with in-process ownership decided in user space ([81] M1) and one lock order; the durability classes with exact per-OS calls, no downgrade, no direct-I/O path, and a no-replace rename plus `durable-name` of both parents at every rename point; fault-model items (2), (3), (5), (7), (8) amended and (9)–(12) added; the mapping policy with `total_len` in sealed-file headers; a crash-gated per-OS environment allow-list; path canonical forms P1–P11 (`fold_v1` with full case folding at Unicode 17.0.0, per-component canonical roots found by root id, hashed named-query file names, portable and never fold-equal ref names); tagged R4 runtime layouts (`OsFileId` with a fixed-source volume key and, on Linux, the file-handle digest that carries the inode generation, [81] B2; `JOURNALCUR`; `DIRMAP`) and the per-OS R-14 rules (the twin rule, [81] M4; the unique-creation-time copy rule, which also fixes [40] on Windows, [81] M5); numeric store file names; per-OS config locations and `lock.flush-wait-ms`; shell rules T1–T10.
- **Edits the first edit list had missed** ([81] M7): §4.2's boot check takes the flush byte before the writer byte; §4.5 step 7's shortcut applies only when nothing lies beyond L0; step 12, the phase-2 heading and intro, "Flush grouping", §1 row 18 and §4.10 follow the group-commit wording. The post-edit search of [60 §9.4] was re-run for `byte 0`, `L0`, `exactly one flush`, `two slot flushes` and `takes the writer byte`: what remains is §1 row 11's staleness levels L0–L3, the pack levels L0–L2 of §7.4, `L0` as the validated bound of §4.5, the new texts of §4.2 and §6.1 and §2.17's `gitmap` row (each takes the writer byte correctly under the new order), and historical rows of this log.
- **Checked and unchanged ([80 §3.2]):** no `HEAD` field is added; the canonical form, `.moi` codec and trailers, record and commit layouts, the git object layer, LQ, the envelope and exit codes (a store I/O fault reuses 7), MCP and the hook JSON.
- **Decisions.** #32 moves to "Decided" with the quotes. One new owner decision: #42 (port-phase hardware, CI and platform coverage — money). #21 gains option (d), macOS document ids; #41's E2 names FSEvents; #39 notes macOS notarization. The cross-target `cargo check` and the platform matrix are design defaults, not owner decisions: neither spends money, moves data or changes a frozen byte. *(superseded by owner decision #44: GT20 (e) is a gate from M0; the cost is in the calendar, [60 §7.1])*
- **Minimum versions** (§1 row 14, §14): Windows 11 x64; Linux ≥ 5.10, 64-bit, static musl; macOS ≥ 14, arm64, enforced by the link setting and a check at open.
- **Gates.** Flushes per commit: ≤ 1, ≤ 3 per 16-writer burst, every acknowledgement after a covering flush and an identity check; thirteen seeded group-commit bugs and the lost-group GT1 scenario; GT20 (d), the OS-layer lint; M0 measurement 22 (the boot identity across a clock step, a sleep, a hibernation and a reboot); §8.3's per-OS notes for the port. No Linux or macOS build, test, CI or crash gate is scheduled in M0–M11.
- **Cost to M0–M11:** ≈ 9.5–16.5 units, not yet in the calendar. The port phase is ≈ 52–83 units, unscheduled. *(superseded by owner decision #44: GT20 (e) is a gate from M0; the cost is in the calendar, [60 §7.1])*

**Owner decisions #43 and #44 (2026-09-26): harness-agnostic agent interface; cross-target type check as a gate.** The owner decided (verbatim translations): "It must work not only for Claude Code but also for Codex and other harnesses." and "Do cargo check for Linux and Mac." [90] (revision 2, which answers its adversarial review [91]: 2 blockers, 12 majors, 12 minors), built from the harness research [H21]–[H23], is integrated.
- **Agent interface (§7.8):** contract C0 (CLI on `PATH`; dual-era stdio MCP server named `moirai`, tools only, text results, explicit `branch`/`lease`/`agent`/`idempotency_key`/`tree`, store discovery by `tree`, instructions ≤ 512 characters; a ≤ 600-byte block at the top of `AGENTS.md` and a `CLAUDE.md` import line; a portable skill in `.agents/skills` beside the Claude plugin); Tier A Claude Code and Codex (a Codex plugin), every other harness through C0, Tier B templates only under #45; `moirai integrate` from one registry, writing no TOML; hooks as accelerators with named fallbacks; **rights only from presented leases** — task leases, new run-scoped role leases and the orchestrator's session role lease minted by the main session's `SessionStart` hook or the orchestrate skill; unleased callers on the `general-purpose` row everywhere; the actor resolved lease holder > attested (`_meta`, stamp) > declared > environment, recorded as `actor_src`; environment leases bound to their first thread; two harnesses' variables → `generic` with no session anchor; the liveness anchor on the identity each server's lifetime tracks (Claude Code session, Codex thread) with `session-ttl` anchors and release at request end for Codex's per-thread servers; the Codex sandbox routes (a writable root on the store by default, a narrowed execpolicy rule as P7's fallback, MCP always) and per-harness exit-7 texts with a `result.v1` fallback; budgets in UTF-8 bytes, each harness cap checked in its own unit, `pack.cyrillic-weight` removed and `*-chars` keys renamed `*-bytes`; MCP results 16,000 B under the `codex` profile for code mode; `--ids` pages; the both-ends rule; the portable schema profile with no object properties, `write` taking `TX` text or a named mutation; `result.v1` with `recorded` ids and `apply --from`; the worker-pack rule; LQ display spelling by ablation, model profiles with per-client defaults, named-only writes for unknown models and LQ-Bench v2.
- **Frozen at M0** ([90 §10.1], §4.6): lease kinds, anchors and `bound` in `LEASES`; the X-F2 amendment (the process-lifetime identity hashed, lazy slots); the `actor_src` byte; the byte units, both-ends, ASCII and `--ids` rules of the output contract; one error code and two refusal texts; the card's display spelling; the codec, decided by §8.2 item 6 among pure-Rust options (no pure-Rust crate writes zstd dictionary frames: `ruzstd` 0.9 compresses at its Fastest level only; the expected default is `lz4_flex` blocks with a raw-content dictionary for bodies and `ruzstd` frames for history).
- **Build:** GT20 (e) mandatory from M0 in the local pre-merge gate and PR CI; the binary crate a composition root only; the pure-Rust rule in GT20 (b) with a reviewed entry for every build script and no checked crate depending on a host-only crate; `blake3` `pure`, `sha1`/`sha2` without `asm`, `rmcp` with `server` + `transport-io` only, no C allocator; T10's C-code trigger answered.
- **Calendar (§9):** re-issued with every delta since the pre-audit baseline, per milestone in [60 §7.1] — the audits (est. 23–40.5 units net of #41's exclusions), the cross-platform design (9.5–16.5) and [90] (15–23.5): ≈ 369–508.5 units; two lanes 33–69.5 weeks (P50 ≈ 47, P90 ≈ 57), one lane 48.5–104 (P50 ≈ 70, P90 ≈ 85.5), against the baseline's P50 ≈ 39 / P90 ≈ 47.5 (two lanes).
- **Decisions:** #43 and #44 decided; #38 reopened (v2 default ≈ 115 M tokens, ≈ $310); #45 new (harness scope: Tier B, Codex cloud, a dispatch wrapper, the plugin package and extra export formats, `codex-csv` and `--structured`). Everything else in [90] is a configuration key, a design default or a measurement.
- **Edited:** header, sources, §0, §1 (rows 1, 9, 10, 14, 19 and the new row 20), §2.6, §2.9, §2.10, §2.17 (a superseded note), §4.1, §4.3, §4.6, §4.9, §5a.1, §5d.3, §5e.9, §6.1, §6.2, §6.4, §7.1–§7.7, the new §7.8, §8.1–§8.3, §9, §10 (risks 12, 14, 28, 29 and 30 amended, 34–39 added), §11, §12, §13, §14 and this log.

No frozen byte changes. New surface: `image export --if-older` and the TTL renewal rule (runtime records only); the `AGENTS.md` block text and the E406 text change before their M0 freeze. The residual search of [90 §12.7] was extended and re-run (item 2).

**The owner's answers of 2026-09-26 on §11: profile L, two lanes on the laptop, Opus-only LQ-Bench, a public repository, and every other decision as recommended.** The owner answered (verbatim translation, binding inputs): "For now no additional machine will be used, everything is here. Two lanes in parallel. For now benchmarks only on Opus 5.5. The moirai project itself will be stored in a public repository on GitHub. Record that all commits must be made WITHOUT Claude co-authorship. Everything else I approve as you wrote it."
- **#34 decided — profile L.** No test host and no guest licence. The nightly gates run in ≈ 5 agent-free laptop windows a week from M1 exit (≈ 145–195 nights), each release-candidate iteration needs a 3-day agent freeze, GT15 runs on one Server Core guest on the laptop (VirtualBox 7, VMware Workstation Pro as the cross-check), the floors and every timing and RAM gate are measured on the laptop, and the disk-full kill-loop variant is the simulator plus an owner-run drill (§8.2 CI paragraph, items 16, 17, the kill loops and the OS-crash loop; §9 M0 row; §10 risk 20).
- **#2 decided — two lanes, both building on the laptop** in owner-agreed windows. The RAM headroom risk is the new §10 risk 40 with its mitigation: item 21 measures both lanes and sets the build cap, one machine-wide build semaphore, no `rust-analyzer` in lane worktrees, a 1.5 GB free-RAM guard, PR checks on hosted runners, and the one-lane fallback (§8.2 item 21, §8.3 RAM, §9).
- **Calendar re-issued** with the same Monte Carlo (seed, draws, rate, schedule and every delta since the pre-audit baseline) and profile L's machine time placed where it falls in the schedule ([74 §2.4]): GT15's M1-exit cycles on one laptop guest (1.25–2.1 weeks instead of the host's 0.3–0.6); 2–4 laptop windows instead of 1–2 host nights at each exit M0–M10 (+ 0.2–0.5 week each, on the lane that owns the exit); RG3 in 2.5–3.5 weeks instead of 2.5. Two lanes: release 35.5–76.5 weeks, P50 ≈ 52, P90 ≈ 62 (a test host would have given 33–69.5, 47 / 57); one lane in profile L 51.5–112.5, 75.5 / 91. [74 A02]'s flat + 2–4 weeks assumed half the exits on the critical path; in the two-lane schedule M0–M3 and M7–M10 always are, and one of M5 and M6, so the delay is ≈ + 5 weeks (P10–P90 + 4.3 to + 5.5). Running the synthetic long seeds on the hosted runners would recover ≈ 2.5 weeks (P50 ≈ 49.5, P90 ≈ 59.5) and is not counted until M0 shows it (§0, §9, §10 risks 14 and 20; [60 §7]).
- **#38 decided — option (a).** LQ-Bench on Opus 5.5 only, with a Claude Code and a generic-client transport arm; no floor tier and no Codex arm; ≈ 53 M tokens, ≈ $280 (≈ $160–540); the `codex` client defaults to `unknown` (named mutations only, `DRY` → `IF TARGETS` as an opt-in, the reading echo on); (b) and (c) stay a later option; the Luna runner and the floor tier (≈ 0.5–1 unit of M0 lane B) leave and stay in the figures as contingency (§1 row 20, §7.7.5, §7.8, §8.3 GT13 and TOKENS rows, §9, §10 risk 36, §13).
- **#36 decided — a public GitHub repository**, `github.com/bluesteelll/moirai`: hosted Windows runners (free) for the build, the lints, GT20 (e) and the synthetic suites, never for a timing, RAM, floor, crash or kill-loop gate; #37 made critical (a gitignored local directory; CI never receives owner data or an API key); the port phase may use free hosted Linux runners (§8.2, §9 M0 row, §10 risk 41, §14).
- **Repository rule** (binding input, not a §11 decision): every commit authored by the owner with no AI co-author trailer and no "Generated with Claude Code" line in a pull request, enforced by the harness attribution settings and a `commit-msg` and PR-body check (binding inputs; [60 §3.1] item 7).
- **Every other open decision decided as recommended** (#1, #3, #5, #8, #11, #15–#18, #20, #21 (a)–(c), #22–#31, #33, #35, #37, #39–#41): moved into §11's "Decided as recommended" table; texts that said "due before", "unless the owner chooses" or "if #23 says so" now state the decision (§1 row 14, §2.3, §2.13, §5e.9, §9 M9 row, cutover and "Not built", §10 risk 2). Left for later with their defaults recorded: #42 (port phase; its floor-grade Linux default now a dual-boot partition of the laptop, since no test host exists), #45's on-demand items and #21 (d) (macOS port).
- **Edited:** header, binding inputs, §0, §1 (rows 14 and 20), §2.3, §2.13, §5e.9, §7.7.5, §7.8, §8.2, §8.3, §9, §10 (risks 2, 14, 20 and 36; 40 and 41 added), §11, §13, §14 and this log; [40], [50], [60], [80] and [90] record the same changes in their own logs. No frozen byte changes.

**Verification pass after the owner's answers (2026-09-26).** A cross-check of the six documents raised 2 majors and 7 minors; all are fixed.
- **GT4 in profile L** (major): the gate rows still required four variants, disk-full on a VHDX among them, against #34. GT4 now runs three variants nightly (`TerminateProcess`, `NtSuspendProcess`, ±1 h clock steps); disk-full is covered by the disk-full injection of GT1 and GT3 and one owner-run RG7 drill on an owner-created small VHDX, because a nightly job never elevates ([74 A20]) (§8.2, §8.3 GT4, §9 M1, §11 #34; [60 §3.2, §3.9, §3.13, §3.15]).
- **Where gates run** (major): [60 §3.13]'s Runs column said "CI" for GT7, GT10 and GT12, which would send owner repositories, owner-derived fixtures and live harness runs to the hosted runners; the cells now split hosted CI (synthetic) from the laptop (owner data, live Claude Code and Codex). **CI** is defined once in the conventions as the hosted PR checks, and budget gates are no longer called CI gates (§1 rows 12–13, §8.1; [40 §7.4, §8.3.4, §8.3.6], [50 §8.3]).
- **Decided, not open:** the reader guide, §0 risk (2) (`shared` not built, #1) and §5e.9 (`links import` built, #23) state the decisions; so do [60] RG11, [50 §9] (heading and columns) and [40 §9.2].
- **Figures:** [60 §5.2] item 21 measures one lane and both; [60] risk 16 uses 68.5–95.5 units; [50] risk 13 and §8.2 use M7's 51–72.5; [60 §3.12]'s size basis gives profile L's 2.5–3.5 weeks of nights and soak.

Every edit above this entry replaces text in place, so no line above it moved.

A2: the harness configuration with AI attribution off is committed (`.claude/settings.json`), and the owner reviews each commit message until [60 §3.1] item 7's check exists (binding inputs, risk 41). A3: the published repository content is reviewed and acceptable (binding inputs, risk 41). A4: this document with the normative [40], [50], [60], [80] and [90] is approved as the M0 specification (binding inputs, §9 M0 entry). A5: the consistency fixes of 2026-09-27 and the design team's six resolutions stand (previous entry). A6 (a): LQ-Bench at M0 runs on the reference model's own parser, binder and evaluator (LQ-3); the product's LQ-1 and LQ-2 stay in M7 and must reproduce the frozen error texts and lints; §7.7.5 with [50 §7.4] item 6 is the normative GT13 gate list, with no "within 5 points of the best candidate" gate (§7.7.5, §7.7.6, §8.3, §9; [60 §3.1, §3.13]; [50 §7.4, §8.2]). A7: the five replay targets of [60 §3.1] gate M0 exit on FL-1; the three resolver-dependent ones — delete-then-re-add `replaced`, the 46 worktree HEADs' gate decisions, the transcript census's edited+moved — gate M6 (§8.2, §9 M0 and M6 rows; [40 §8.1, §8.3.4]; [60 §3.1, §3.7]). A8: acknowledged — the two lanes build in windows agreed with the owner at M0 start; none is fixed yet (§9 M0 row, risk 40).
- **B1–B14 confirmed** unchanged.
- **V1 — LQ-Bench without API billing** ("I will not buy API access; only Claude Code by subscription is available"): no API key exists anywhere; every arm runs through the owner's Claude Code subscription in headless mode on the laptop; the neutral API runner becomes a Claude Code headless runner, and the scripted generic stdio client uses the same headless Claude Code as its model endpoint. What that changes is stated with every result ([90 §8.3]): the harness's system prompt and kept tool definitions are present, sampling is not controllable (a repeated sample measures the spread), token counts are Claude Code's reported usage, the Claude Code version is pinned and recorded, and one runner serves every arm. The cost is subscription quota — ≈ 53 M tokens at M0, mostly cached input, before Claude Code's per-call overhead — in several usage windows inside M0 within the weekly limits; if the quota is short, the prompt set shrinks by [50 §7.4] item 5's documented rule and each gate states its sample size and statistical power, and no gate is skipped. The real-session prompts still go only to Anthropic. Claude token counts for measurements 6 and 20, the card and the skills come from Claude Code's reported usage, because the token-counting endpoint needs an API key. Options (b) and (c) stay later options that need non-Claude model access (binding inputs, §7.7.5, §8.2 items 6 and 20, §8.3 GT13 and TOKENS rows, §11 #38).
- **V7 — the OS-crash rig deferred to after the release** ("No way to install it for now; such a deep test is postponed until the release, like mac and linux"): GT15 (the VM guest under VirtualBox or VMware, the OS-crash loop, the M1-exit ≥ 1,000 cycles and the release gate's ≥ 5,000), its calibration (§8.2 item 17), the guest-licence question and the WSL2 and Memory Integrity choices move to after the release, together with the unscheduled port phase, and never block the release. Every format field and protocol rule the rig would test stays frozen. Crash safety in M0–M11 rests on the fault-model simulator with crash-point enumeration (GT1), GT3, the kill loops (GT4), durable-before-acknowledge, `backup`/`restore` and the daily image export; real power loss is an explicit, unverified residual risk with its triggers (§10 risk 17). Windows `file mv` and the export's renames keep `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH` besides the directory flush until the rig's calibration can show it unnecessary (§4.10, §5b.6). The VHDX operations of V8 serve measurements 18 and 22 and the RG7 drill, not the rig, so they stay (binding inputs, §0, §1 rows 5, 14 and 18, §2.13, §4.10, §8.2, §8.3, §9, §10 risks 1, 13, 17 and 20, §11 #34, §14).
- **V8 acknowledged; V2–V6, V9 and V10 remain open** resource items for M0.
- **Calendar re-issued** with the same Monte Carlo (seeds 20260926 and 20260927, 20,000 draws, rate 5–8 units a week, schedule, units and deltas) after removing the rig's machine time: GT15's M1-exit cycles on the laptop guest (1.25–2.1 weeks, net of a host's 0.3–0.6) leave the model; the rig's last 1,000 cycles on the release candidate ran inside RG3's 14 completed runs, which GT3, GT4, GT17 and the soak's 3-day freeze still bind, so RG3 keeps 2.5–3.5 weeks; the M0 exit keeps its 2–4 windows (the calibration shared them with the 24 h FL-1 fuzzing). Two lanes in profile L: storage engine 12–26.5 weeks (P50 ≈ 17.5, P90 ≈ 22), release 35–75 (P50 ≈ 50.5, P90 ≈ 60.5), ≈ 1.2 weeks earlier (P10–P90 0.9–1.6); one lane 51–110.5 (P50 ≈ 74.5, P90 ≈ 89.5); a test host would have given 33–69.5 (47 / 57), so profile L now costs ≈ + 3.5 weeks at P50 (P10–P90 + 3.1 to + 4.2); the hosted-runner variant gives 48.5 / 58.5. The nights from M1 exit to release (≈ 145–195, P50 ≈ 165) are unchanged. The GT15 harness's 2–3 units of M1 and the rig's M0 provisioning stay in the figures as contingency until the M0 re-issue (dropping them would move the release ≈ 0.4 week more).
- **Follow-up checks of the same day.** The ≈ 53 M LQ-Bench tokens are labelled as the estimate made for a neutral API runner (≈ 2.3 calls per prompt, ≈ 7k input tokens per call), before Claude Code's per-call overhead (its system prompt and kept tool definitions), plus ≈ 1 M for the repeated 52-prompt sample; M0's first usage window measures that overhead and the quota plan is re-issued on it, with [50 §7.4] item 5's shrink rule as the fallback (§7.7.5, §8.3 cost row, §11 #38, the V1 item above). The M0 target for the 58 dead memory paths says that the rename chains come from the test-only git CLI extraction and are re-bound through FL-1 and the model's exact-evidence resolution, the product's E6 re-running it at M6 (§9 M0 row; [40 §8.3.4], [60 §3.1]). The allow-list (§4.1, §14) states that in M0–M11 NTFS is admitted on the GT1/GT3 fault-model evidence and GT4 on real NTFS, its GT15 variant running with the deferred rig (risk 17; [80 §0 item 7, §2.6]). §8.2 item 7 records the fallback if the owner has no Codex access (V9 asks the owner to confirm after V1): the Codex probes wait, their decisions keep the documented defaults (`integrate.codex.store-writes` `writable-root`), P10 skips Luna's column and GT12's Codex conformance is reported as not run ([90 §10.5]). §9's R5 size line names LQ-3 as the model's own parser, binder and evaluator ([50 §8.2]). No frozen byte changes.

**A1 re-review (M0 WP-80a), 2026-09-27.** The owner's item A1 made the re-review of [40] and [50] revision 2 part of the M0 specification review. Three lenses (performance and crash safety, semantics and correctness, agent fit and buildability; `docs/spec/reviews/a1-P.md`, `a1-S.md`, `a1-A.md`) raised no blocker, 14 majors and 41 minors, and confirmed PLAN §6.2's resolutions R1–R19, some with conditions. `docs/spec/reviews/a1-dispositions.md` records every disposition. What changed here (the detail is in [40]'s and [50]'s logs, which carry most of the changes; [60] and PLAN §6.2 carry theirs):
- **Protocol and crash safety.** A cross-volume `file mv` of a file is refused like a directory's, since a copy followed by an unlink has no namespace order under fault-model item (2) (A1P-01; §4.10, §5e.5); intent recovery re-establishes the directory barrier before any roll-forward, a protocol point and a seeded bug (A1P-02; §4.10, §5e.5, §8.2 mutation list, §8.3 seeded bugs); the M0 `ProjectFs` trait has `sync_dir` and no `journal_since` (A1P-02, A1P-17; §4.10, where this document wins over [80 §2.1]'s list); the vehicles of measurements 2 and 10 must reproduce the product's in-lock cost and overlay inserts, and M1's gates re-check both decisions (A1P-03; §8.2 items 2 and 10); `tx.max-work-in-lock` is calibrated under the hold budget (A1P-13; §4.5); measurement 15 renames as the protocol does, and flushes per verb count log and directory flushes separately (A1P-07; §8.2, §8.3); measurement 19 includes a pending settle, and hook and server settles never sleep (A1P-06; §8.2, §5e.3); GT20 (d) refuses direct `std::fs` file I/O in product crates other than `moirai-os` (A1P-10; §8.3).
- **Frozen bytes and rules** (each before the freeze, and each shown to the owner in the dispositions file): the `TreeReg`/`TREES` settle epoch gains `scope_kind` and `scope_ref`, one epoch kept per (tree, kind, ref) (A1P-04; §4.3, §4.4, §5e.3); the file-uid predecessor is the greatest candidate uid on the registering view and the dead-uid set is that view's, which changes derived uid values, and the re-key re-points every edge the re-keyed side added (S-01; §4.6, §5a.7 R4 row, §5e.2, §5e.6); the anchor capture digest takes the widened prefix and suffix and a `lines` window, and the anchor uid a stored predecessor term (S-03; §4.6, §5b.2, §5e.2, §5e.8); a range's `end` enters item 10 only as `end_h` (S-04; §4.6, §5b.2); the creating `AddEdge` carries the unhashed `aN` (A1P-12; §4.3); path roots in canonical values are encoded by name (S-19; §4.6); the `relink` vocabulary is closed (A-M1; [40 §2.2], §5e.5); `pathmove.hlc` is the candidate-time HLC, which a re-parent never changes (A-M2, S-20; [40 §2.4]).
- **Surface.** The CLI header gains a tree display label, the reader note moves to line 2 in one ASCII spelling, and the header limits are counted per part (A-M3; §7.1, §8.3 TOKENS); `link_state()` and `a.state` are total (`none`, `unresolved`) with warning W10 (S-05; §5e.2, [50]); the `unverified` details are a closed set (S-08; §5e.2, §5e.3); the portable form of named queries is computed on the bound AST (S-02; §5b.2); `oid`'s algorithm is per root (S-18; §5e.2); I-F10 and I-F14 are restated (S-13, S-14; §5e.3, §5e.8); the exit-5 text for agents names the raw mv (A-m4; §5e.5); the MCP `link` mutation takes `quote` and `end` (A-m6; §7.2).
- **Evidence plans.** The reference model takes git history as data ([60 §4.2], A-M4); the LQ-Bench quota plan is re-stated in raw tokens (≈ 80 M input, est.) and measured by the runner's first real calls before the owner is asked for quota (A-M5; §7.7.5, §8.3 TOKENS, §11 #38); measurement 11 reports the CLI baseline on `main` and on a lane (S-23; §8.2).
- **Deferred to WP-80 pass 1:** S-16 (a creation-time-preserving copy against the copy rule's line 2; measurement 15 now records `ChangeTime` on rename), S-22 (BM25 arithmetic, if the ablation keeps BM25), A-m1 (marker lengths). **Rejected:** none. Consequential text in [80 §2.1] (`os::project`: `sync_dir`, `journal_since`), [80 §2.11.4] rule 6 (`EXDEV` copies), [80 §5.5] and [90 §6.3, §8.3] (the header limits and the quota wording) is superseded by this entry and corrected at WP-99 with the owner's review; the chapters follow this document and [40]/[50] meanwhile.
- **Edited:** header, §4.3, §4.4, §4.5, §4.6, §4.10, §5a.7, §5b.2, §5e.2–§5e.6, §5e.8, §7.1, §7.2, §7.7.5, §8.2 (items 2, 10, 11, 15, 19 and the mutation list), §8.3 (flushes per verb, seeded bugs, GT20 (d), TOKENS header and LQ-Bench rows), §11 #38 and this log.
